| `409` | `Repository changed during authorization; retry the request` | Someone replaced the repository while you connected. Retry. |
| `409` | `Kortix cannot reach <owner>/<repo>: …`, printed as `remote: …` | The repository is disconnected. See [Repository disconnected](#repository-disconnected). |
| `502` | `No git upstream is configured for this project` or `git upstream unreachable` | Kortix cannot reach the upstream repository. Check the connection under **Git repo**. |
| `503` | `git_credential_unavailable` | Kortix could not get a credential for the upstream. Retry when the body says `retry: true`. Otherwise check the connection. |

### Commit and push

After a clone, commit and push with plain git. `kortix ship` runs these steps in one command:

1. It runs the `kortix validate` checks. `--no-verify` skips them.
2. It runs `git add -A` and commits, with the message from `-m`. The default message is `kortix: ship`. `--no-commit` fails when the working tree has changes.
3. It offers to set each `env` secret that has no value. `--no-env` skips this.
4. It pushes the current branch to the branch of the same name.
5. It connects each declared connector that still needs sign-in. `--no-connect` skips this.

`-n` / `--dry-run` prints the plan and changes nothing. `kortix deploy` is an alias.

#### The first ship

A folder with no `.kortix/link.json` gets a new project. Ship picks the repository from the folder's `origin`, without a prompt:

| `origin` of the folder | What ship creates |
| --- | --- |
| A GitHub URL | A GitHub project through the Kortix GitHub App. If the App is not installed, ship prints an install link and asks you to retry after you install it. With `-y` it stops instead. `--github-token <PAT>` links without the App; the token needs repository Contents read and write. |
| Another URL (GitLab, Bitbucket, plain git) | Nothing. Kortix links GitHub repositories only, so ship stops before any request and suggests `--origin managed`. |
| None, or `--origin managed` | A managed repository. Ship sets `origin` to the git origin and installs the credential helper. |

For a GitHub URL, the first ship pushes to your existing `origin` with your own git credentials.

`--origin <github-url>` sets `origin` to that GitHub URL first. `--account <id|slug>` picks the account, and `--name` the project name.

Ship writes `.kortix/link.json` the moment the project exists, and `git add -A` commits it. A teammate with push access (`project.gitops.push`, the Manager role) who clones the repository can then run `kortix ship` too. Later ships set `origin` to the project's git origin and install the credential helper.

### Who can clone and push

Five permissions control git:

| Permission | Allows |
| --- | --- |
| `project.gitops.read` | Read commits, diffs and change requests. For a session: clone and fetch. |
| `project.gitops.push` | Push. Open change requests. |
| `project.gitops.merge` | Merge change requests. |
| `project.gitops.ref.any` | For a session: push a branch other than its own. |
| `project.gitops.ref.delete` | Delete a branch. |

A project **Member** holds `project.gitops.read` only. A Member reads the branches, commits and change requests of their own sessions and of sessions they may open, and nothing of another member's private session. A clone serves every branch, so a person needs `project.gitops.push` to clone or fetch: a Member cannot clone from their own git client. A Member's sessions still clone and push their own branch. A **Manager** holds all five. Account owners and admins act as Managers on every project in the account. See [Accounts & access](/docs/#teams).

**People** with push access:

- may push any branch, including the default branch;
- need `project.gitops.ref.delete` to delete a branch;
- can never delete the default branch. Nobody can.

IP allowlist and MFA policies of the account apply to git requests too.

**Sessions** push with the session token:

- A session pushes only its own branch, the branch named after its `session_id`.
- Any other branch needs `project.gitops.ref.any` in the agent's `kortix_permissions`. Deleting a branch needs `project.gitops.ref.delete`.
- A push to the default branch also needs `project.agent.write`, `project.trigger.create`, `project.trigger.update` and `project.trigger.delete`.
- The agent's `kortix_permissions` must list the permission, or be `all`, and the agent's ceiling role must also hold it. Neither widens the other. The role of the person who started the session is not an input. An agent with no `kortix_permissions` key holds no project permission. See [What an agent may do](/docs/#agents).

By default, agents land work through [change requests](#change-requests). [Agents & permissions](/docs/#agents) shows how to grant leaves.

Kortix does not block a force-push. On a GitHub repository, add a ruleset that blocks force-pushes and deletions on the default branch. The ruleset does not block change-request merges, because a merge is a fast-forward or a merge commit.

#### Read a refused push

Kortix refuses the whole push when it refuses one ref. Git prints the reason for each ref:

```text
 ! [remote rejected] HEAD -> feature-x (a session may only push its own branch (<session_id>); commit there and open a change request to land this elsewhere)
```

| Reason | Cause |
| --- | --- |
| `<branch> is the default branch and cannot be deleted` | A delete of the default branch. |
| `deleting <ref> requires git ref-delete authority` | A person without `project.gitops.ref.delete` deleted a branch. |
| `a session may only push its own branch (…)` | A session without `project.gitops.ref.any` pushed another branch. |
| `pushing <branch> skips change-request review and needs agent and trigger write permission; …` | A session pushed the default branch without `project.gitops.ref.any` and the manifest permissions. |
| `pushing <branch> could change agent grants without review, and an agent grants only what it holds; …` | A governed agent pushed the default branch but does not hold every permission it could grant. |
| `a session may not delete its own branch` | A session without `project.gitops.ref.delete` deleted its branch. |

### Session branches

Each session works on its own branch. The branch name is the `session_id`, with no prefix. Kortix cuts it from the session's `base_ref`, which is the project default branch unless the session was created with another one.

Inside the sandbox, the branch is `$KORTIX_BRANCH_NAME`. The agent commits there and runs `git push origin HEAD`. [Sandboxes & previews](/docs/#computers) lists the other variables.

A pushed branch stays in the repository after the session stops and after the session is deleted. Work the agent did not push is lost when the sandbox is deleted.

Get an agent's work on your computer:

```bash
git fetch origin <session_id>
git checkout <session_id>
```

- `kortix files branches` lists the branches with commits ahead of and behind the default branch.
- `kortix sessions info <session_id>` shows `branch` and `base_ref`.
- To start a session on another branch, pass `base_ref` when you create it, in `POST /v1/projects/<project_id>/sessions` or in `sessions.create()` of the [SDK](/docs/#sessions-from-code). `kortix sessions new` has no flag for it.

When the base branch moves, open the command palette in the session and run **Ask Agent: Sync Branch & Reload**. The agent merges `origin/<base_ref>` into the session branch, resolves conflicts, runs the relevant tests, and commits. Its last command is `kortix sessions reload … --no-repo --force --yes`, which restarts the agent runtime and ends the turn. Send `continue` when the runtime is back.

### Change requests

A change request merges one branch, the head, into another, the base. Kortix runs the merge, the diff and the conflict check as git operations on the project repository, so change requests work on every kind of repository.

```text
open ──merge──▶ merged          (final)
open ──close──▶ closed ──reopen──▶ open
```

#### Open one from your computer

```bash
git push origin my-branch
kortix cr open --head my-branch --title "Add the triage agent" --description "Why and what changed"
```

- The base is the project default branch. `--base <branch>` targets another branch.
- A head with no commits ahead of the base is refused with `CR_HEAD_NOT_AHEAD`. Push first.
- Each later push to the head branch updates the same change request.

#### Open one from a session

An agent follows this contract:

1. Commit on `$KORTIX_BRANCH_NAME`.
2. Push: `git push origin HEAD`.
3. Open: `kortix cr open --title "…" --description "…"`. Inside a sandbox, `--head` and `--session` fill themselves from `$KORTIX_BRANCH_NAME` and `$KORTIX_SESSION_ID`.
4. Report the change request number to the person, and stop.

A change request from a session targets the session's `base_ref`. An agent cannot retarget it: `--base` with another branch returns `400` `CR_BASE_NOT_SESSION_BASE`. A person can open a change request for the same head with `--base`.

#### Review and merge

Review in the web app on the [Review page](#the-review-page), or from a terminal:

```bash
kortix cr ls                     # open change requests
kortix cr diff 3                 # the unified diff
kortix cr merge-preview 3        # can it merge? lists conflicts
kortix cr merge 3
kortix cr request-changes 3 --message "Rename the flag first"
```

A merge runs these steps:

1. Kortix validates `kortix.yaml` on the head branch, with its `imports:`. An invalid manifest stops the merge with `422` `MANIFEST_INVALID`. A head with no manifest passes.
2. Kortix fast-forwards the base when the head is strictly ahead.
3. Otherwise it creates a merge commit, `Merge CR #<n>: <title>`, authored by `Kortix <noreply@kortix.ai>`. `--message` sets another message.
4. A conflict stops the merge with `409` and the list of conflicting files.

After the merge, running sessions on that base pick up the new config, and Kortix syncs the project's connectors from the new `kortix.yaml`. See [Config comes from the base branch](#config-comes-from-the-base-branch).

**Request changes** records the note on the change request and sends it to the session that opened it as a new prompt. A sleeping sandbox wakes.

#### Agents that merge

- An agent that declares `kortix_permissions` merges only when the grant includes `project.gitops.merge`. An agent without `kortix_permissions` cannot merge. Only the `meta` coordinator and a project on the legacy v1 manifest with no agents declared act with the role of the person who started the session.
- A session cannot merge a change request it opened unless its grant in `kortix.yaml` includes `project.gitops.merge`. Without it, the merge returns `403` `CR_SELF_MERGE_REFUSED`.
- A merge that changes `agents`, `default_agent` or `triggers` also needs the permission the direct edit needs: `project.agent.write`, or `project.trigger.create`, `.update` or `.delete`. The merging agent cannot grant any permission it does not hold.

A grant that names `project.cr.open` or `project.cr.merge` counts as `project.gitops.push` or `project.gitops.merge`. Write the `gitops` name in new grants.

#### Permissions

| Action | Needs |
| --- | --- |
| List, show, diff, merge preview | Read access to the project. |
| Open | `project.gitops.push` |
| Edit, close, reopen | Write access to the project. An agent also needs `project.gitops.push`. |
| Merge | `project.gitops.merge`, plus the manifest permissions above for an agent. |
| Request changes | `project.review.act` |

#### `kortix cr` commands

`<cr>` is the change request number, such as `3`, or its UUID.

| Command | Does |
| --- | --- |
| `kortix cr ls [--status <s>]` | Lists change requests. `<s>` is `open` (default), `merged`, `closed` or `all`. |
| `kortix cr show <cr>` | Shows one change request. |
| `kortix cr diff <cr>` | Prints the unified diff. |
| `kortix cr open --head <branch> --title <t> [--base <branch>] [--description <t>]` | Opens a change request. Aliases: `--from`, `--into`, `--body`. |
| `kortix cr merge-preview <cr>` | Shows whether it can merge, and the conflicts. |
| `kortix cr merge <cr> [--message <t>]` | Merges it. |
| `kortix cr request-changes <cr> --message <t>` | Sends the note to the agent that opened it. |
| `kortix cr close <cr>` | Closes it without a merge. |
| `kortix cr reopen <cr>` | Reopens a closed one. |
| `kortix cr version-diff --from <head> --into <base>` | Summarizes a merge before you open a change request. |

Each command takes `--project <id>` and `--host <name>`. `ls`, `show`, `diff`, `merge-preview`, `request-changes` and `version-diff` also take `--json`. The REST routes are under `/v1/projects/<project_id>/change-requests` in the [API reference](https://api.kortix.com/v1/docs).

`GET /change-requests` returns one page, newest number first: 50 change requests by default, at most 500 with `limit` (a larger `limit` answers `400`). Pass `next_cursor` back as `cursor` for the next page; it is `null` on the last page. `kortix cr ls` follows it and lists every change request.

#### Errors

| Code | Status | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `CR_HEAD_NOT_AHEAD` | `422` | The head has no commits ahead of the base. Commit your work on the head branch and push it. |
| `CR_BASE_NOT_SESSION_BASE` | `400` | An agent tried to target a branch other than its session's base. |
| `MANIFEST_INVALID` | `422` | The manifest on the head branch fails validation. The body lists the issues. Run `kortix validate`. |
| (conflict) | `409` | The merge conflicts. The body lists the files. |
| (not open) | `409` | The change request is merged or closed. |
| `CR_SELF_MERGE_REFUSED` | `403` | A session tried to merge its own change request without a merge grant. |
| `CR_GOVERNANCE_UNVERIFIED` | `503` | Kortix could not read the manifest to check an agent's merge. Nothing merged. Retry. |
| (manifest unreadable) | `502` | Kortix could not read the manifest from the head branch. |

### The Review page

The Review page is the project's inbox for everything that waits on a person. Open **Review** in the project sidebar, or go to `/projects/<project_id>/review`.

| Item | Id | Comes from |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Change | `cr:<id>` | A change request. |
| Approval | `call:<id>` | A connector call that a policy holds for approval. See [Connectors](/docs/#connectors). |
| Output (`output`), question (`decision`), finished batch (`batch`) | its own id | An agent submits it with `kortix review submit`. |

The lists are **Needs you**, **Waiting** and **Done**. The filters are **All**, **Changes**, **Approvals**, **Outputs**, **Questions** and **Finished**. Keys: `j` and `k` move, `Enter` opens, `a` approves, `e` asks for changes, `d` dismisses, `1` `2` `3` switch lists, `/` searches, `?` shows help.

Each list loads 50 items at a time, newest first. **Load more** shows the next 50. The filters and the search apply to the items loaded so far. The **Review** count in the sidebar, and the dot on a session that waits on you, count every item that needs you.

What a verdict does depends on the item:

| Item | `approve` | `reject` | `changes` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Change | Merges the change request. | Closes it. | Sends the note to the agent that opened it. A note is required. |
| Approval | Lets the call run. | Denies it. | Not available. |

An item an agent submitted takes every verdict: `approve`, `reject`, `changes`, `answer` and `dismiss`.

The Review page shows a verdict only to a role that holds its permissions. Every verdict on a change or on an agent's item needs write access to the project (`project.write`):

| Control | Needs |
| --- | --- |
| **Ship it** (web), **Merge** (mobile) | `project.write` + `project.gitops.merge` |
| **Solve with agent** (web), **Resolve conflicts** (mobile): it replaces the merge on a conflicted change | `project.write` + `project.gitops.merge` |
| Ask for changes, close, dismiss, answer, and every verdict on an agent's item | `project.write` + `project.review.act` |
| **Approve** and **Deny** on a connector approval | A project manager, or the person who launched the session. The API refuses anyone else. |
| **+** on mobile, to open a change request | `project.write` + `project.gitops.push` |

A built-in member holds none of these permissions. It sees changes and agent items without verdicts, and it decides a connector approval only in a session it launched. The change request dialog on the Files page and on a session's change card follows the same rule: **Apply changes** and **Fix with agent** need `project.write` + `project.gitops.merge`, **Dismiss** and **Reopen** need `project.write`.

| Command | Does |
| --- | --- |
| `kortix review ls [--segment <s>] [--kind <k>]` | Lists items. Segments: `needs_you`, `waiting`, `done`. Kinds: `change`, `approval`, `output`, `decision`, `batch`. |
| `kortix review show <item>` | Shows one item, with a connector call's arguments. |
| `kortix review act <item> <verdict> [--message <t>]` | Decides one item. |
| `kortix review bulk <verdict> <id>…` | Decides several agent items. It skips changes and approvals, which each need their own review. |
| `kortix review submit --kind <k> --title <t>` | Submits an `output`, `decision` or `batch`. Options: `--summary`, `--risk` (`none`, `low`, `medium`, `high`), `--detail <json>`, `--agent`, `--session`. |

Reading needs `project.review.read`, a verdict needs `project.review.act`, and `submit` needs `project.review.submit`. A Member holds read and submit. A Manager holds all three.

The REST routes are under `/v1/projects/<project_id>/review`:

| Route | Returns |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /review/items` | One page of items: **Needs you**, then **Waiting**, then **Done**, newest first within each. Filters: `segment`, `kind`, `session_id`. `limit` is 50 by default; above 200 it answers `400`. Pass `next_cursor` back as `cursor` for the next page; it is `null` on the last page. A walk is not a snapshot: an item whose status changes between two pages can come back on a later page or be missed. `kortix review ls` follows the cursor and lists each item once, with its newest status. |
| `GET /review/items/<item>` | One item by its id, including `cr:<id>` and a pending `call:<id>`. |
| `GET /review/summary` | `{ needs_you_total, needs_you_by_session }`: the counts the sidebar shows. A connector call that you may not decide counts for the people who may. |

### Config comes from the base branch

A session runs the current config release of its base branch: `kortix.yaml`, agents, skills, tools and harness files. It does not run the files in `/workspace`.

- An edit in `/workspace` or on a session branch takes effect only when it lands on the base branch, through a merge or a push.
- Each commit on the base branch is a new config release. A running session converges to it at the start of its next turn. A push or a merge also starts the convergence in the background.
- To apply it at once, select **Reload config** in the session menu, or run `kortix sessions reload <session_id>`. It restarts the agent runtime. `--force` reloads during a turn and ends that turn; it asks first unless you pass `--yes`. `--no-repo` skips the git pull in `/workspace`. `--status` only reports whether the session is behind.
- To test config before a merge, create a session with `base_ref` set to your branch. Its change requests target that branch too.
- Such a session runs the branch's agent behavior: prompts, tools, skills and harness files. Governance still comes from the default branch: which agents are declared, and their secret, connector and permission grants. A branch cannot widen what an agent may use before it merges. When the project requires declared agents, an agent declared only on the branch is refused with `400` `AGENT_NOT_DECLARED`.

When a release fails to load, the session runs the last release that worked, or else the platform default config. The session header shows **Config failed to load**. Fix the config on the base branch. See [Sandboxes & previews](/docs/#computers) for troubleshooting.

| Limit | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Config release | 32 MiB compressed, 128 MiB uncompressed |
| Project snapshot | 512 MiB compressed. Above it, running sessions stop picking up config changes until a new session starts. |
| `kortix validate` warning | One file of 10 MiB or more, or more than 512 MiB in total |

To keep a file in git but out of the release, mark it `export-ignore` in `.gitattributes`. A directory rule covers everything under it. The release, the `kortix validate` size check and session config downloads leave those files out. The manifest file and the OpenCode config directory always ship whole.

```text
fixtures export-ignore
datasets/raw export-ignore
```

### Manage the repository

**Customize → Settings → Git repo** shows the repository and its **Status**, the **Base branch**, the **Manifest file**, **Work on this locally**, **Use your own Git client** and **People with access**. The base branch is the branch new sessions and change requests start from. From the CLI, `kortix projects set --branch <branch>` and `--manifest <path>` change the same fields. Both need `project.settings.write`.

#### GitHub connection

`GET /v1/projects/<project_id>/git/connection` returns the connection `state`:

| `state` | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `connected` | Kortix can reach the repository. |
| `reconnect_required` | The Kortix GitHub App installation is missing, unusable, or does not match the repository. Open the `install_url` in the response to install the App again. |
| `unavailable` | Kortix cannot reach the repository for a reason you cannot fix with the App. |
| `not_connected` | The project has no repository. |

#### Repository disconnected

Kortix reaches a GitHub repository through the Kortix GitHub App installed on the repository's owner. When that access goes away, the project is **disconnected**. `GET /v1/projects/<project_id>/git/connection` then returns `state: reconnect_required`, `reason: repository_disconnected`, a `disconnected_reason`, a `message` with the cause and the fix, and an `install_url`.

| `disconnected_reason` | Cause | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `app_uninstalled` | The Kortix GitHub App was uninstalled from the owner, or its connection was removed from your Kortix account. | A GitHub owner of the organization reinstalls the App: **Customize → Settings → Git repo → Reconnect GitHub**. |
| `app_suspended` | An owner suspended the App on GitHub. | A GitHub owner unsuspends it under GitHub **Settings → GitHub Apps**. |
| `repo_not_accessible` | The repository was deleted, renamed, or removed from the App's repository access. | A GitHub owner adds the repository again under GitHub **Settings → GitHub Apps → Kortix → Repository access**, or you replace the repository. |

While a project is disconnected:

- Every API route that reads the repository answers `409` with `code: repository_disconnected`, the `reason`, and the same message. The project detail still loads: its `config.manifest_version.unknown_reason` is `repository_disconnected` and `git_connection.status` is `error`.
- Session create and start fail at once with that `409`. A session that is already running keeps running.
- Scheduled triggers skip each run and record the message as the trigger's last error. They do not retry.
- `git clone`, `fetch` and `push` through the Kortix git origin print the message as `remote: …`.
- The web app shows **GitHub disconnected** in the sidebar, in **Git repo** and on a session that cannot start. `kortix projects info` prints the reason and the reconnect link.

Kortix checks GitHub again at most every 5 minutes. After the fix on GitHub, the project reconnects on the next check. Reconnecting through **Reconnect GitHub** checks at once, and a project that stored the old installation moves to the new one.

#### Replace the repository

Under **Git repo**, select **Change** and enter the new GitHub repository URL. Kortix verifies access through the GitHub App. The new repository needs the manifest file on its default branch. Stop active sessions, and close or merge open change requests, first. New sessions use the new repository. Existing sessions cannot restart after the change. The route is `PUT /v1/projects/<project_id>/git/repository`. It needs `project.settings.write` and `project.connector.write`.

#### Give people direct access

A managed repository lives on GitHub under Kortix. To let someone work on it on github.com, add their GitHub username under **People with access**, with **Can edit** or **Can view**. GitHub emails them an invite. This needs `project.members.manage`, and works on managed repositories only. For a repository you own, manage access on GitHub.

#### A repository on another git host

To let Kortix reach a repository on another git host, store a token with `PUT /v1/projects/<project_id>/git-credential` and the body `{ "token": "…" }`. Kortix keeps it as a platform credential, not as a secret a sandbox can read. It needs `project.connector.write`. GitHub and managed repositories refuse it with `409`. For a repository on another git host, `kortix sessions new` run in a checkout whose `origin` is that repository creates the session branch. It pushes your current branch with your own git credentials.

#### Browse without a clone

`kortix files` reads the committed repository. It acts on the default branch unless `--ref <branch|tag|sha>` picks another. Every subcommand needs `project.file.read`.

| Command | Does |
| --- | --- |
| `kortix files ls [<path>]` | Lists files. |
| `kortix files cat <path>` | Prints a file. |
| `kortix files search <query> [--content]` | Searches file names, or file contents. |
| `kortix files history <path>` | Lists the commits that changed a file. |
| `kortix files branches` | Lists branches, ahead and behind the default branch. |
| `kortix files commits [--path <p>]` | Lists commits on `--ref`. |
| `kortix files show <sha>` | Shows one commit and its files. |
| `kortix files diff <sha> [--path <p>]` | Prints a commit's patch. |
| `kortix files download -o <file.zip> [--path <p>]` | Downloads the repository or a subtree as a zip. |

For uncommitted files in a running sandbox, use `kortix sessions cp <session_id>:<path> <dst>`. See [Sandboxes & previews](/docs/#computers).

#### Delete

Deleting a project deletes its managed repository. A repository you connected yourself is left untouched. See [Projects](/docs).

## kortix.yaml reference

`kortix.yaml` is the manifest at the root of a project repository. It declares the agents and what each one may reach, plus the triggers, connectors, secret names, sandbox templates, harness settings and Apps of the project. This page is the key-by-key reference for people who write or review the file by hand. For the repository layout, see [Projects](/docs).

```yaml
# yaml-language-server: $schema=https://kortix.com/schema/kortix.v2.schema.json
kortix_version: 2
default_agent: kortix

env:
  optional: [STRIPE_API_KEY]

agents:
  kortix:
    file: agents/kortix.md
    connectors: all
    secrets: all
    skills: all
    kortix_permissions: all
```

### File and schema

The project's `manifest_path` (default `kortix.yaml`) sets where Kortix looks. Change it with `kortix projects set --manifest <path>` or in **Customize → Settings**. Kortix reads the first file that exists of `kortix.yaml`, `kortix.yml` and `kortix.toml` at that path. A repository with both a YAML and a TOML file uses the YAML file.

Add the schema line at the top of the file. A YAML editor then validates and completes every key:

```yaml
# yaml-language-server: $schema=https://kortix.com/schema/kortix.v2.schema.json
```

| Schema | URL |
| --- | --- |
| v1 | `https://kortix.com/schema/kortix.v1.schema.json` |
| v2 | `https://kortix.com/schema/kortix.v2.schema.json` |
| v3 | `https://kortix.com/schema/kortix.v3.schema.json` |
| All versions (selects by `kortix_version`) | `https://kortix.com/schema/kortix.schema.json` |

`kortix schema` prints the combined schema. `kortix schema --version 2` prints one version, and `--url` prints its URL instead of the body.

Validate before you push:

```bash
kortix validate                       # ./kortix.yaml
kortix validate --file config/kortix.yaml --json
kortix validate --scopes              # print every grantable kortix_permissions entry
```

| Flag | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `--file <path>` | Validate this file instead of the project manifest in the current directory. |
| `--json` | Print a JSON report: `valid`, `path`, `issues`. |
| `--scopes` | Print the grantable `kortix_permissions` entries and their aliases, then exit. |
| `--no-dockerfile-lint` | Skip the checks on sandbox template Dockerfiles. |

`kortix validate` exits `0` with no errors, `1` with one or more errors, and `2` when the file is missing or unreadable. The same schema check runs in `kortix ship` and in the change request merge check. An error blocks the push or the merge. A warning never does. Kortix ignores unknown top-level keys.

The CLI also checks the files around the manifest. In a v2 manifest it reports these as errors:

- a missing agent `.md`;
- a missing tool module;
- a skill with no `skills/<name>/SKILL.md`;
- an agent `connectors` or `connectors_required` slug that `connectors` does not declare (`kortix_slack`, `kortix_teams`, `kortix_email` and `computer` always count as declared);
- an agent `secrets` or trigger `secret_env` name that `env` does not declare;
- an agent `policy_sets` value that names a set the connector does not define, or `base`.

An agent `policy_sets` key for a connector that the agent is not granted, or that `connectors` does not declare, is a warning.

It lints each template Dockerfile. It warns when the files in Git exceed 512 MiB or when one file reaches 10 MiB.

### Versions

`kortix_version` is required and picks the schema.

| Version | Format | Agent behavior | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `1` | `kortix.toml` or YAML | In the agent `.md` | Legacy. Reads and runs. See [Migrate from kortix.toml](#migrate-from-kortixtoml). |
| `2` | YAML only | In `agents/<name>.md` (prompt and frontmatter) | `kortix init` and **Create project** write v2. The dashboard agent editor writes v2. An omitted grant means none. |
| `3` | YAML only | Inline in `kortix.yaml`: `prompt` or `prompt_file`, `model`, `mode`, `permission`, `description` | Rejects `agents.<name>.file`, `agents.<name>.disable` and the top-level `opencode` key. Write and edit it by hand. The dashboard agent editor edits only v2 manifests and shows a v3 agent read-only. |

A version above `3` is an error. A `kortix.toml` that declares version `2` or `3` is an error. No automatic migration from v2 to v3 exists.

A v3 agent carries its behavior in the manifest:

```yaml
kortix_version: 3
default_agent: support
agents:
  support:
    description: Answers questions from the help desk.
    mode: primary
    prompt_file: prompts/support.md     # or prompt: "You answer…", not both
    permission:
      bash: ask
    secrets: [HELPDESK_TOKEN]
```

A v3 agent block also takes `variant`, `temperature`, `top_p`, `steps`, `color`, `hidden` and `options`. `prompt_file` is a repository-relative `.md` path. A v3 agent gets no project repository in `/workspace` unless it sets `workspace: { project_repository: true }`. See [Workspace](#agent-workspace).

### Top-level keys

| Key | Type | Default | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `kortix_version` | integer | required | `1`, `2` or `3`. |
| `default_agent` | string | required (v2, v3) | Names a declared agent that is not `enabled: false`. Sessions and triggers that name no agent use it. |
| `agents` | map | required (v2, v3) | Agent name to agent block. At least one agent. See [agents](#agents). |
| `imports` | list | none | Other YAML files whose collections merge into this one. See [imports](#imports). |
| `project` | map | none | `name`, `description`. `project.name` is the project's name: a push that changes it renames the project, and a rename in Kortix commits it here. `description` is for people who read the repository. |
| `env` | map | none | `required`, `optional`: secret names. See [env](#env). |
| `runtime` | string | — | Deprecated: selects nothing, every new session runs pi. `opencode` validates with a warning. See [harnesses, opencode and pi](#harnesses-opencode-and-pi). |
| `harnesses` | map | none | `pi.packages`, `opencode.plugins`. |
| `opencode.config_dir` | path | `harnesses/opencode`, then `.kortix/opencode` | OpenCode config directory. An error in v3. |
| `pi.config_dir` | path | `harnesses/pi`, then `.kortix/pi` | pi config directory. |
| `tools` | map | all five Kortix tools | Tool name to `kortix:<name>` or a module path. See [Tools](/docs/#agents). |
| `commands` | map | none | v3 only. Slash command name to its template. See [commands (v3)](#commands-v3). |
| `sandbox` | map | platform image | `templates`, `default`. See [sandbox](#sandbox). |
| `triggers` | list | none | Schedules, webhooks, connector events, App events; each runs an agent or calls an App. |
| `connectors` | list | none | Apps, APIs and MCP servers an agent can call. |
| `apps` | map | none | App slug to App configuration. |
| `policy.default_mode` | string | `allow_all` | `allow_all` or `risk`. See [policy and policies](#policy-and-policies). |
| `policies` | list | none | Project-wide connector rules. |
| `approvals.ttl` | string | `24h` | How long a held connector call waits for a decision. See [policy and policies](#policy-and-policies). |

A v2 or v3 manifest rejects `channels`, `sandboxes`, and the image keys `image`, `dockerfile`, `slug`, `cpu`, `memory`, `disk`, `entrypoint`, `context`, `context_dir` and `gpu` set directly on `sandbox`.

### imports

`imports` splits one manifest across several YAML files. The platform resolves every import and works on the merged document: validation, triggers, agent grants and connector sync all see one manifest.

```yaml
# kortix.yaml
kortix_version: 2
default_agent: kortix
imports:
  - triggers/          # every .yaml and .yml file below this directory
  - agents.yaml        # one file
agents:
  kortix:
    connectors: all
```

```yaml
# triggers/reports/weekly.yaml
triggers:
  - slug: weekly-report
    type: cron
    agent: kortix
    cron: "0 0 15 * * 0"
    prompt: Build the weekly report.
```

1. A path is relative to the repository root. `..`, absolute paths and globs are errors. A path that matches no `.yaml` or `.yml` file is an error.
2. A directory import takes every `.yaml` and `.yml` file below it, at any depth, sorted by path.
3. An imported file declares `triggers`, `connectors`, `agents`, `apps`, `tools` and `imports`. `kortix_version`, `default_agent`, `runtime`, `project`, `env`, `opencode`, `sandbox`, `policy` and `policies` in an imported file are errors. Kortix drops any other key in an imported file, for example `harnesses`.
4. Imports nest up to 8 levels and 200 files. A cycle is an error. A file reached twice merges once.
5. One name, one file. A trigger slug, connector slug, agent name, App slug or tool name declared in two files is an error that names both files.
6. Entries merge in order: the importing file first, then its imports as listed.

A broken import fails the whole manifest:

```text
imports: triggers "weekly-report" is declared in both triggers/a.yaml and triggers/b.yaml — one name, one file
```

### agents

`agents` maps an agent name to its governance block: whether it may run and what it may reach. In v2 the behavior (prompt, model, mode, permissions) lives in the agent `.md`. What each grant means is on [Agents & permissions](/docs/#agents).

An agent name matches `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,127}`.

| Field | Default | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `file` | `agents/<name>.md` | v2 only. The agent `.md`, relative to the repository root. |
| `enabled` | `true` | Boolean. `false`: Kortix does not start the agent. |
| `connectors` | none | Grant set of connector slugs. |
| `secrets` | none | Grant set of secret names the agent receives in its sandbox. |
| `skills` | the `.md` decides | Grant set of skill names. Set, it overrides `permission.skill` in the agent `.md`. Omitted, the `.md` rule applies. |
| `apps` | none | Grant set of App slugs the agent may open when the App access is `restricted` or `private`. |
| `policy_sets` | none | Map of connector slug to the name of a policy set on that connector. The agent's calls on that connector use the set's rules before the base rules. A connector with no entry uses the base rules. See [Policy sets](/docs/#connectors). |
| `commands` | none | v3 only. Grant set of command names from the top-level `commands`. The agent runs only these. |
| `kortix_permissions` | none | Grant set of project permissions. Capped by the agent's ceiling role, never by the role of the person who starts the session. See [What an agent may do](/docs/#agents). |
| `tools` | all | `all`, `none`, a list of tool names, or `{ exclude: [names] }`. The map form `{ bash: false }` also works. |
| `sandbox` | project default | A sandbox template slug, or `default`. |
| `workspace.project_repository` | v2 `true`, v3 `false` | Boolean. `true`: new sessions of this agent check out the project repository. `false`: no project repository in `/workspace` and no repository access. See [Workspace](#agent-workspace). |
| `harnesses` | none | Per-agent `pi.packages`, `pi.exclude`, `opencode.plugins`, `opencode.exclude`. |
| `network_egress` | none | Declaration only. Accepts `{ version: 1, default: deny, rules: [] }` and warns that egress is not enforced. |

A grant set is `all` (or `*`), `none` (or `""`), or a list of names. A list that contains `*` means all.

**`file` rules.** The path ends in `.md`. It does not start with `/` or `-`. It has no `.`, `..` or empty segment. Each segment uses letters, digits, `_`, `-`, `.` and spaces. With `file` omitted, Kortix reads `agents/<name>.md`, then `.kortix/opencode/agents/<name>.md`, or `<opencode.config_dir>/agents/<name>.md` when that key is set. The API refuses a request that changes `file` (`400`, `invalid_config`). To move an agent, move its `.md` and edit `file` in the same commit.

**`kortix_permissions` rules.** Each entry is a project permission. `kortix validate --scopes` prints the list. Account permissions such as `member.*`, `billing.*` and `project.create` are errors. The alias entries `project.cr.open`, `project.cr.merge`, `project.customize.read` and `project.customize.write` resolve to their current permissions and warn. The no-op entries `project.session.exec`, `project.gateway.routing.edit`, `project.schedule.*`, `project.webhook.*` and `channel.*` are errors in v2 and v3.

**Errors in a v2 agent block.** `env` (use `secrets`), and every behavior key: `description`, `model`, `mode`, `variant`, `temperature`, `top_p`, `options`, `color`, `steps`, `hidden`, `prompt`, `prompt_file`, `permission`, `disable`, `opencode`. The message points at the agent `.md`.

**Deprecated fields.**

- `connectors_required`: parsed, must be a subset of `connectors`, not read at session start.
- `connectors_personal`: alias of `connectors_required`. Warns.
- `kortix_cli`: alias of `kortix_permissions`. Warns. A different value from `kortix_permissions` is an error.
- `repository_access`: alias of `workspace.project_repository`. A different value is an error.
- `workspace` as a string: `branch` means `project_repository: true`, and `runtime` means `false`. `read` without an explicit boolean refuses session start (`409`, `WORKSPACE_MODE_UNAVAILABLE`).

#### Agent workspace

`workspace` sets what new sessions of the agent get in `/workspace`. It has one key today:

```yaml
agents:
  engineer:
    workspace:
      project_repository: true   # v3 default false, v2 default true
```

- `project_repository: true`: the session checks out the project repository on its own session branch. Set it on every agent that reads or edits the repository, runs scripts from it, or opens change requests.
- `project_repository: false`: the session gets no checkout, no git variables, and no repository API access (`403`). Its [project tools](/docs/#agents) still load: the sandbox receives only the modules of the tools the agent may call.
- Omitted: `false` in `kortix_version: 3`, `true` in versions 1 and 2. A v2 project keeps its behavior. The starter, `kortix init`, Create project, the agent editor and `kortix migrate` write the value explicitly, so a new or migrated agent states it.
- A running session keeps the value it started with. A change applies to new sessions.
- Any other key under `workspace` is an error. A typo does not fall back to the default: Kortix withholds the repository from that agent until the block is fixed.

### commands (v3)

In v3, a slash command is declared in `kortix.yaml`. The project registers it under `commands`, and each agent that may run it enables it by name. v3 reads no harness folder, so `harnesses/opencode/commands/` and `harnesses/pi/prompts/` are not read.

```yaml
kortix_version: 3
default_agent: support
commands:
  triage:
    description: Sort a ticket by urgency.
    prompt: "Read ticket $1 and reply with its urgency: low, normal or high."
  release-notes:
    prompt_file: prompts/release-notes.md
agents:
  support:
    prompt_file: prompts/support.md
    commands: [triage, release-notes]   # or: all
```

| Key | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| name | Lowercase letters and digits, in words joined by `-`. At most 64 characters. |
| `prompt` | The template, as a non-empty string. |
| `prompt_file` | A repository path to a `.md` file. Kortix uses the whole file as the template; it does not remove a frontmatter block. |
| `description` | Optional. Shown in the `/` menu. |

- Set exactly one of `prompt` or `prompt_file`. Any other key is an error. An `agent` key points you to `agents.<name>.commands`.
- `$ARGUMENTS` takes everything typed after the command. `$1`, `$2` and so on take the words in order, and the highest one takes the rest. A template with neither gets the arguments appended.
- An agent runs only the commands its `commands` grant names: `all`, `none` or a list. Omitted means none. A command that no agent enables is left out.
- Running a command on an agent that does not enable it answers `403` with `{"error": "command_not_enabled", "command": "<name>", "agent": "<agent>"}`.
- Commands come from the manifest on the project's default branch.

### env

`env` declares the secret names the project expects. Values live in [Secrets](/docs/#security), never in the manifest.

```yaml
env:
  required: [DATABASE_URL]
  optional: [STRIPE_API_KEY]
```

| Field | Type | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `required` | list of names | The dashboard and `kortix ship` prompt for a missing value. A missing value never blocks a session. |
| `optional` | list of names | Offered by the same prompts. |

A name matches `^[A-Z_][A-Z0-9_]*$`. Any other key under `env` warns. `kortix ship --no-env` skips the prompts. In a v2 manifest, `kortix validate` requires every name in an agent `secrets` list and every trigger `secret_env` to appear here.

The Secrets store refuses a name longer than 64 characters or one that starts with `KORTIX_`. Such a name validates here but never gets a value. Only the Teams connection sets `MS_TEAMS_*` names, and only ChatGPT subscription onboarding sets `CODEX_AUTH_JSON`.

### sandbox

`sandbox.templates` lists named sandbox images. With no template, every session boots the platform image. What Kortix adds on top of an image is on [Sandboxes & previews](/docs/#computers).

```yaml
sandbox:
  default: ml
  templates:
    - slug: ml
      name: ML development
      dockerfile: Dockerfile.ml
      cpu: 4
      memory: 16
      disk: 50
```

| Field | Default | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `slug` | required | Unique. `default` is reserved. A slug over 64 characters warns: the runtime drops it. |
| `name` | slug | Label in the template picker. |
| `dockerfile` | — | Repository-relative path. Set exactly one of `dockerfile` and `image`. |
| `image` | — | Public image with a tag or a digest. No tag is an error. `:latest` warns. |
| `entrypoint` | the image's | Overrides the container entrypoint. |
| `cpu` | provider default | vCPU cores, 1–32. |
| `memory` | provider default | GiB, 1–128. |
| `disk` | provider default | GiB, 1–500. |
| `container_runtime` | `false` | `true` runs Docker in the sandbox. See [Hardware and Docker](/docs/#computers). |

A hardware value is a positive integer. A value below the minimum is an error. A value above the maximum warns and is clamped. `gpu` warns and does nothing.

`sandbox.default` names a template in this manifest, or `default` for the platform image. A session picks its template in this order:

1. The `sandbox_slug` of the session request.
2. The `sandbox` of the agent the session starts with.
3. `sandbox.default`.
4. The platform image.

A template change applies to the next session, not to a running one.

### harnesses, opencode and pi

Every new session runs pi. OpenCode is deprecated and serves only sessions that already run it; `runtime` selects nothing. See [Harnesses: OpenCode and pi](/docs/#agents).

| Key | Default | Holds |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `opencode.config_dir` | `harnesses/opencode`, then `.kortix/opencode` | Deprecated with OpenCode. Files only OpenCode reads: `opencode.jsonc`, `plugins/`, `tools/`. Without the key, Kortix uses the first directory that holds an `opencode.json` or `opencode.jsonc`. |
| `pi.config_dir` | `harnesses/pi`, then `.kortix/pi` | Files only pi reads: `settings.json`, `extensions/`, `skills/`, `prompts/`. |

**`harnesses.pi.packages`** lists the pi packages every pi session loads:

```yaml
harnesses:
  pi:
    packages:
      - npm:pi-web-access@0.30.0          # exact version
      - source: npm:pi-mcp-adapter@2.36.0
        extensions: ["extensions/*.ts"]   # load only these files
        skills: []                        # load none of its skills
      - ./extensions/audit.ts             # one extension file in this repository
agents:
  reviewer:
    harnesses:
      pi:
        exclude: [pi-web-access]          # package name, no version
        packages: [npm:@my-org/pi-review@1.2.0]   # this agent only
```

- An entry is `npm:<name>@<x.y.z>` (an exact version), a `./` path that stays inside the repository, or `{ source, extensions, skills, prompts, themes }`. An omitted filter loads everything. `[]` loads nothing.
- A `./` path to a `.ts` or `.js` file is one extension. It can live anywhere in the repository, so a project needs no `harnesses/pi/` folder. It loads from the session's config release, and a change to a file in its folder restarts the pi runtime.
- A session reads the package list when it starts. A running session keeps its list until it restarts.
- At most 20 entries. A range, a missing version and a git source are errors.
- An agent loads the top-level list, minus its `exclude`, plus its own `packages`. `exclude` exists only on an agent.
- The change request merge builds the packages. A session never runs `npm install`. A session that starts before the build exists boots without the project packages.

**`harnesses.pi.codemode`** sets pi's codemode tool. The model writes one JavaScript script that calls the other tools (`await tools.bash({ command })`), and only the script's output reaches the model, so one call can batch, chain and filter several tool calls.

```yaml
harnesses:
  pi:
    codemode: on        # on (default) | only | off
agents:
  reviewer:
    harnesses:
      pi:
        codemode: off   # this agent only
```

| Value | The model gets |
| --- | --- |
| `on` (default) | every tool, plus `codemode` |
| `only` | `codemode` alone; every other tool runs from a script |
| `off` | no `codemode` |

- An agent's value replaces the top-level one. Read when the session starts.
- A script's calls pass the same permission policy as direct calls: a `deny` rule blocks them, and an `ask` rule pauses the script until someone answers.
- A script cannot start a subagent (`task`) or ask a question (`question`). The model calls those directly.
- Scripts run in a sandboxed JavaScript VM (QuickJS) with no file system, network or timers. They reach the box only through the tools.

**`harnesses.opencode.plugins`** (v2) lists the files in `<opencode config dir>/plugins/` that load, for example `audit.ts`. Without the key, every plugin file loads. An agent adds its own with `agents.<name>.harnesses.opencode.plugins` and drops top-level ones with `exclude`. A listed file that does not exist fails the config build.

### tools

`tools` maps a tool name to `kortix:<name>` (a Kortix tool) or a repository-relative `.ts` or `.js` module. With no `tools` key, sessions get all five Kortix tools. With a `tools` key, sessions get only the tools it lists. The rules, errors and module contract are on [Tools](/docs/#agents).

### triggers, connectors, apps

A trigger or connector entry is identified by its `slug`, and an App by its key under `apps`. Each one matches `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,127}` and is unique in its collection.

**`triggers`.** Full reference: [Triggers & reminders](/docs/#automations). An entry is `slug` + `on:` (what fires it: `cron`/`every`/`at` with `timezone`, `webhook: { secret }`, `event` + `connector`, or `event` + `app`) + `run:` (`prompt` with `agent`, `model`, `session`; or `app`, `path`, `method`, `payload`). The flat form below means the same thing.

| Field | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| `slug`, `type`, `prompt` | Required in the flat form. `type` is `cron`, `webhook`, `event` or `app`. `prompt` is a non-empty template, unless `target` calls an App. |
| `name`, `agent`, `enabled`, `model` | Optional. `agent` defaults to `default_agent` and names a declared agent. `model` is `provider/model`. |
| `session_mode` | `fresh` (default), `reuse`, `pinned` (needs `session_id`) or `keyed` (needs `session_key`). |
| `filter` | Optional. Map of dotted path to expected string. A fire runs only when every path matches. See [Triggers & reminders](/docs/#automations). |
| `cron` type | `cron` (6 fields, seconds first), `every` (a duration, at least `60s`) or `run_at` (ISO-8601), and `timezone` (IANA, default `UTC`). Fires at most once every 60 seconds. |
| `webhook` type | `secret_env`: the name of the signing secret. |
| `app` type | `app`: the slug of the App whose posted events fire it. `on: { event: <name> \| "*", app }`; in the flat form a name is `filter: { event.name: <name> }`. No feature flag. |
| `target` | Optional, any type. `{ app, path, method, payload }`: the fire calls the App instead of an agent. |
| `event` type | `connector` and `event`. Optional `account`, `source`, `config`. Needs the `event_triggers` feature flag (Composio event triggers), off by default. |

`type: monitor` is refused with `MONITOR_REMOVED`: an App that posts events replaces it. An event trigger rejects every cron and webhook field. `connector`, `account`, `source` and `config` are errors on any type except `event`.

**`connectors`.** Full reference: [Connectors](/docs/#connectors). Credentials live in the platform, never in the manifest.

| Field | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| `slug`, `provider` | Required. `provider` is `composio`, `pipedream`, `mcp`, `openapi`, `postman`, `graphql`, `http` or `channel`. |
| Provider field | `composio` and `pipedream`: `app`. `mcp`: `url`, optional `transport` (`http` or `sse`). `openapi` and `postman`: `spec`. `graphql`: `endpoint`. `http`: `base_url`. `channel`: `platform` (`slack`, `teams` or `email`). |
| `name`, `enabled` | Optional. |
| `authorization_strategy` | `project` (default) or `user`. |
| `credential` | `shared` only. |
| `sensitive` | `true`: an unmatched call needs approval. |
| `auth` | `type`: `bearer`, `basic`, `custom`, `api_key`, `oauth1`, `hmac`, `aws_sigv4`, `mtls` or `none`. `in`: `header` (default), `query` or `cookie`. `name` for `custom` and `api_key`. `oauth1` only on `openapi`, `postman` and `http`. |
| `headers` | Static headers, at most 32. Never a credential. |
| `policies` | The base rules. List of `{ match, action, conditions? }`. `match` is a glob over tool names. `action` is `always_run`, `require_approval` or `block`. `conditions` is the same as in [project rules](#policy-and-policies). |
| `policy_sets` | v2 and v3. Map of set name to `{ description?, policies? }`. A name matches `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,63}` and is not `base`. At most 32 sets, each with at most 200 rules in the `policies` shape. Not allowed on `channel` or `computer` connectors. Agents get a set with `agents.<name>.policy_sets`. See [Policy sets](/docs/#connectors). |

`computer` is not a valid provider: connecting a computer creates that connector. The slugs `kortix_slack`, `kortix_teams`, `kortix_email` and `computer` are reserved for the built-in connectors. Connecting a channel writes its `channel` connector for you: see [Slack, Teams & email](/docs/#channels).

**`apps`.** Full reference: [Configure in kortix.yaml](/docs/#configure-in-kortixyaml).

`apps` maps an App slug to its configuration. The keys are `path`, `type` (`static`, `bundle`, `dockerfile`, `oci_image`), `image`, `dockerfile`, `command`, `port`, `root`, `output_dir`, `install_command`, `build_command`, `spa`, `readiness_path`, `idle_timeout_seconds`, `always_on`, `uses`, `resources`, `env`, `secrets`, `processes`, `http_service`, `services`, `mounts`, `vm`, `deploy`, `labels` and `display`. Any other key is an error.

### policy and policies

`policy.default_mode` decides a connector call that no rule matches:

- `allow_all` (default): the call runs.
- `risk`: a read runs; a write or destructive call needs approval.

`policies` holds project-wide rules. A rule matches a fully qualified tool path, `<connector-slug>.<tool>`, with `*` globs. Kortix checks project rules first, then the calling agent's [policy set](/docs/#connectors) on the connector, then the connector's base `policies`. The first matching rule wins. `conditions` limits a rule to calls whose arguments match:

```yaml
policy:
  default_mode: risk
policies:
  - match: gmail.send_email
    action: require_approval
    conditions:
      - arg: to
        match: "*@example.com"     # a glob, or /regex/ in slashes
  - match: gmail.send_email
    action: block
```

`approvals.ttl` sets how long a held call waits for a decision: an integer and `m`, `h` or `d`, such as `30m`, `4h` or `7d`. Kortix clamps it to 5 minutes through 30 days. Without it, or with any other value, a held call waits 24 hours. A held call that nobody decides in time expires.

```yaml
approvals:
  ttl: 4h
```

All conditions of a rule must hold. `negate: true` inverts one condition. How approvals reach a person is on [Policies and approvals](/docs/#connectors).

`kortix validate` does not check `policy` or `policies`. It does check `policy_sets`: see [File and schema](#file-and-schema). When Kortix syncs connectors, it skips a malformed rule and reports it as a connector sync error. Check the rules in the connector settings after the merge.

### Dashboard and CLI edits

The dashboard and the API edit the manifest in the project repository. `kortix triggers add|rm|enable|disable` edit the local file, and `kortix ship` pushes it. With `--apply`, they change the project now. In each case:

- An edit to an entry rewrites only the file that declares it, the root or an imported file. A new entry goes to the root file.
- Keep `kortix_version` as the first key.
- A new trigger is written as `slug`, `name`, `on:`, `run:`, then its options. An edit keeps the form the entry has. In the flat form, keep the order the editor writes: `slug`, `name`, `type`, `agent`, `model`, `enabled`, the session fields, `filter`, the type fields, then `prompt`.

A manifest edit takes effect when it reaches the default branch. A dashboard, API, SDK or `--apply` edit commits there at once. Any other edit takes effect when `kortix ship`, a direct push or a change request merge brings it there. See [How a change takes effect](/docs).

#### kortix.yaml is the source of truth

Everything the manifest can declare is owned by the file. The dashboard, the API, the SDK and the CLI never change a declared setting in the database alone:

| You change | In the dashboard, API, SDK or CLI | After a push that edits the file |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A trigger, an agent's config, scope and grants, a skill, a connector, `default_agent` | Commits to kortix.yaml | Applied on the next sync |
| An App's `resources`, `idle_timeout_seconds`, `always_on`, `labels`, `display`, `uses` | Commits to `apps.<slug>` (the block is added when the App has none), then changes the App | Applied to the App on the next sync, without a deploy |
| Deleting an App | Removes `apps.<slug>` and every `uses` or `display.parent` that names it, then deletes the App | — |
| A connector's `auth.secret` | Commits to `connectors[].auth.secret`, then binds it | Applied on the next sync |
| `project.name` | Commits to `project.name`, then renames the project | A changed value renames the project on the next sync |
| A sandbox template the file declares | Refused with `409 template_declared_in_manifest`: edit `sandbox.templates` | Applied on the next sync, `entrypoint` included |

A write that cannot commit (the repository is unreachable, the value is invalid) changes nothing. Kept out of the file on purpose: runtime state (last fire, status, run history), secret values, reminders, runs an App schedules for itself, feature flags, and an App's access mode. An App's release config (`env`, `processes`, `mounts`, …) applies when you deploy: a release is the build plus the config it was deployed with, so a rollback runs an older config by design. `project.description` is informational.

### Migrate from kortix.toml

Kortix reads and runs a v1 `kortix.toml`. To move it to v2, open **Customize → Settings → Upgrades** and run **Migrate manifest to v2 (kortix.yaml)**, or run:

```bash
kortix projects upgrade [<id>]
```

Both start a session with the project's default agent. The agent rewrites the manifest, runs `kortix validate` and opens a change request. The session never merges it. Starting it needs permission to start sessions in the project.

To migrate by hand:

1. Turn the `[[agents]]` array into the `agents:` map, keyed by name, and add `default_agent`.
2. Rename each agent `env` to `secrets`. v1 gives an agent every secret when `env` is omitted. v2 gives none, so list every secret and connector each agent needs.
3. Delete `[[channels]]` and reconnect each channel from the dashboard.
4. Write each `[[sandbox.templates]]`, `[[triggers]]` and `[[connectors]]` table as a YAML list entry with the same fields.
5. Remove `credential = "per_user"`, `agent_scope`, and the no-op `kortix_permissions` entries. All three are errors in v2.
6. Run `kortix validate --file kortix.yaml`, then delete `kortix.toml`.

## CLI

`kortix` is the Kortix command line. It does from a terminal what the web app does: create and clone projects, run sessions, review change requests, and configure agents, connectors and secrets. Use it on your own machine, in CI, from a coding agent, and inside a session sandbox, where it is already installed and signed in.

```sh
curl -fsSL https://kortix.com/install | bash
kortix login
kortix --help
```

### Install

The install script supports macOS and Linux on x86_64 and arm64. On Windows, run it inside WSL. It does these steps:

1. Downloads the release binary for your system from GitHub Releases.
2. Checks it against the release's `SHA256SUMS`. A missing or wrong checksum stops the install.
3. Puts the binary in `~/.kortix/kortix`.
4. Links `kortix` and `kortixt` (the [TUI](/docs/#everywhere)) into `/usr/local/bin`. If that directory is not writable, it uses `~/.local/bin` when it exists, else `sudo`.

| Variable | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `KORTIX_VERSION=<x.y.z>` | Install that release instead of the latest. |
| `KORTIX_CHANNEL=dev` | Install the dev channel build (`dev-latest`). |
| `KORTIX_BIN_DIR=<dir>` | Link the commands into this directory instead of `/usr/local/bin`. |

```sh
curl -fsSL https://kortix.com/install | KORTIX_VERSION=0.13.52 bash
```

Check the install with `kortix version`. To update or remove the CLI, see [Maintain the CLI](#maintain-the-cli).

### Sign in

```sh
kortix login
```

The CLI signs in to one Kortix instance at a time. An instance is a **host**. The order is always host › account › project › session: you sign in to a host, pick an account in it, pick a project in that account, and run sessions in the project.

`kortix login` does these steps:

1. Opens your browser. You approve the CLI there, and the token comes back on a local callback.
2. Selects your account. With one account it picks it. With several it asks. `--account <slug>` skips the question.
3. Sets a default project for the host. `--no-project` skips this step.

| Flag | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `--token <token>` | Sign in with a token, with no browser. Use it on a headless machine. |
| `--account <slug>` | Select this account without a prompt. |
| `--no-project` | Do not set a default project. |
| `--host <name>` | Sign in to this host instead of the active one. |
| `--api <url>` | The API URL for the host. |

To create a token for `--token`, see [API keys and tokens](/docs/#security).

#### Hosts

Every host keeps its own login. You use these two built-in hosts:

| Host | URL |
| --- | --- |
| `cloud` | `https://api.kortix.com`. The default. |
| `selfhost` | `http://localhost:13738`. `kortix self-host start` sets it to your instance's URL. |

`kortix hosts ls` also lists `local-dev` and `kortix-internal-dev`. Kortix uses them to develop Kortix. You do not need them.

| Task | Command |
| --- | --- |
| Add a host and sign in | `kortix hosts add <name> --url <api-url> --login` |
| Switch the active host | `kortix hosts use <name>` |
| Run one command on another host | `kortix <command> --host <name>` |
| List hosts and their login state | `kortix hosts ls` |
| Show one host or the active one | `kortix hosts info [<name>]` (`--json`) |
| Show the signed-in user on a host | `kortix hosts whoami [<name>]` |
| Remove a host you added | `kortix hosts rm <name>`. A built-in host is reset, not removed. |
| Sign out of a host | `kortix logout`, or `kortix hosts logout <name>` |
| Switch the active account | `kortix accounts use <slug>` |

The CLI changes a remote `http://` URL to `https://`. A private address keeps `http://`: `localhost`, a LAN IP, a name that ends in `.local`, `.lan`, `.internal` or `.home.arpa`, and a name with no dot, such as a Docker Compose service.

The logins live in `~/.config/kortix/config.json`, with file mode `0600`. `KORTIX_CONFIG_FILE` sets another path.

#### Check who you are

| Command | Output |
| --- | --- |
| `kortix whoami` | The host, the user, the active account, the default project and the token context. `--json` for scripts. |
| `kortix token` | Only the token context: the token kind (user, project or session token), its project, session and agent, and its connector and permission grants. It never prints the token itself. |

### Which project a command uses

A project command runs against the first project it finds in this order:

1. `--project <id>` on the command.
2. `.kortix/link.json` in the current directory. The CLI does not look in parent directories.
3. The `KORTIX_PROJECT_ID` environment variable.
4. The host's default project.

| Task | Command |
| --- | --- |
| Set the host's default project | `kortix projects use [<project_id>]` |
| Bind the current directory to a project | `kortix projects link [<project_id>]` |
| Remove the binding | `kortix projects unlink` |
| Clear the default project | `kortix projects unset` |

`kortix projects use` also switches the active account to the project's account. `kortix projects link` writes `.kortix/link.json` and works only in a directory that has a `.kortix/` directory or a `kortix.yaml`. `kortix projects clone` and the first `kortix ship` write the link for you.

The link also records the host. A command in a linked directory goes to that host, whatever host is active.

A session ID works across projects. If the session is not in the resolved project, and you did not pass `--host` or `--project`, the CLI searches your other accounts and logged-in hosts. The 8-character short ID from `kortix sessions ls` works anywhere a session ID goes.

### The everyday loop

The loop runs on the project's git repository. `kortix ship` pushes your branch, each session works on its own branch, and you merge its change request. The [Git](/docs/#git) page covers the repository, cloning, pushing from any git client, session branches and change requests.

<Steps>
<Step title="Get a project">

Start a new project, or clone an existing one:

```sh
kortix init my-agent && cd my-agent      # new project from the starter
kortix projects clone <project_id> my-agent
```

`kortix projects clone` clones through Kortix with your Kortix login and installs a git credential helper, so plain `git pull` and `git push` work in the clone.

</Step>
<Step title="Push your changes">

```sh
kortix ship
```

`kortix ship` validates `kortix.yaml`, commits every change (`git add -A`), asks for missing secrets, pushes the current branch to the branch with the same name, and asks you to connect declared connectors. The first run also creates the project. `kortix deploy` is the same command.

</Step>
<Step title="Start a session">

```sh
kortix sessions new --prompt "Add a skill that drafts the weekly status report"
```

The agent works in its own sandbox, on its own branch.

</Step>
<Step title="Follow it">

```sh
kortix chat <session_id>              # line chat, or -p "…" for one prompt
kortix sessions shell <session_id>    # a shell in the session's sandbox
kortix tui                            # the whole product as a terminal app
kortix sessions wait-for <session_id> # block until the agent is done
```

</Step>
<Step title="Review and merge">

```sh
kortix cr ls
kortix cr diff 1
kortix cr merge 1
git pull
```

`1` is the change request number from `kortix cr ls`. `kortix cr request-changes 1 --message "…"` sends the note to the session that opened it.

</Step>
</Steps>

### Work with sessions from a terminal

The commands below are `kortix sessions <subcommand>`. `chat` and `connect` also exist at the top level: `kortix chat`, `kortix connect`. [Sessions](/docs/#computers) explains the behavior behind each one.

#### Start and list

| Command | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `new --prompt "<text>"` | Start a session. `--agent <name>` and `--model <id>` override the project defaults. `--wait` blocks until the sandbox is ready. `--json` prints the session object. |
| `new … --with-file <path>` | Upload a local file to `/workspace/incoming/<name>` before the prompt goes in. Repeatable. |
| `new … --label <label>`, `--meta <key>=<value>` | Classify the session. Both are repeatable. |
| `new … --connector <alias>=<connection-id>` | Bind a connection to the session. Repeatable. `--no-connectors` binds none. |
| `ls` | List sessions with who started each. `--mine`, `--shared`, `--automated`, `--search <q>`, `--label <label>`, `--children <session_id>`. The list leaves out the sessions you archived and marks a pinned session `(pinned)`. `--pinned` lists only your pinned sessions, `--archived` only your archived ones. `--deleted` lists the deleted sessions you can restore, with their full IDs and the date each one stays restorable. |
| `pin <session_id>…`, `unpin <session_id>…` | Pin sessions in your own list, or unpin them. Only your list changes. |
| `archive <session_id>…`, `unarchive <session_id>…` | Take sessions out of your own list, or put them back. Archiving unpins a session and does not stop it. |
| `status` | One line per active session with what its agent is doing now. `--all` adds stopped sessions. |
| `info <session_id>` | One session. |

#### Talk

| Command | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `chat [<session_id>]` | A line chat with the agent. With no ID it picks your most recent running session. `--new` starts one. |
| `chat <session_id> -p "<text>"` | Send one prompt, print the reply and exit. `--json` prints the reply as JSON. |
| `chat <session_id> -p "<text>" --queue` | Store the prompt in the session's inbox and return at once. It survives a sleeping or busy sandbox. |
| `chat <session_id> -p "<text>" --steer` | Give the prompt to the running turn. The agent reads it at its next step. With no turn running, it starts one. |
| `queue <session_id>` | The inbox: `ls`, `rm <prompt-id>`, `now <prompt-id>`, `hold`, `release`. |

#### Watch

| Command | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `log [<session_id>]` | The recent messages, read-only. A stopped session is read from its saved transcript and is not woken. `--limit <n>` (default 10). |
| `wait-for <session_id>` | Block until the agent is done. Exit `0` done, `3` blocked on a question or permission, `124` timed out. `--timeout <seconds>` (default 300). |
| `connect [<session_id>]` | Deprecated with OpenCode. Attach the OpenCode TUI to a session that still runs OpenCode. With no ID it shows a picker. Every new session runs pi, which has no TUI to attach: the command exits `1`. Use `chat` or `shell` instead. Details are on [TUI](/docs/#everywhere). |
| `digest` | A short review of recent sessions. `--since 7d`. |

#### Unblock

| Command | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `pending <session_id>` | The questions and tool permissions the agent waits on. |
| `approve <session_id> [<request-id>]` | Answer a permission. `--always`, `--reject`, `--message "<why>"`. |
| `answer <session_id> [<request-id>]` | Answer a question. `--option <value>`, `--text "<answer>"`, `--reject`. |
| `approvals <session_id>` | Connector calls a policy holds for a person: `ls`, `approve <execution-id>`, `deny <execution-id>`. |

#### Files and ports

| Command | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `cp <src> <dst>` | Copy between your machine and a sandbox, or between two sandboxes. A sandbox path is `<session_id>:<path>`. `-r` for directories. |
| `files <session_id> <sub>` | The live `/workspace`: `ls`, `status`, `find`, `write`, `touch`, `mkdir`, `mv`, `rm`. |
| `attachments <session_id>` | Uploads and files the agent showed. Works while the session is stopped. `--download <id>`, `--all`, `--out <dir>`. |
| `preview <session_id> [port]` | Print a preview URL for a sandbox port. The default port is `3000`. |
| `forward <session_id> --port <sandbox>[:<local>]` | Forward a sandbox port to `localhost`, HTTP and WebSocket. Runs until Ctrl+C. |
| `shell [<session_id>]` | A raw terminal in the sandbox, with no agent. `--new` opens a fresh one. |

#### Lifecycle

| Command | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `stop <session_id>` | Stop the sandbox. The disk is kept. |
| `start <session_id>` | Wake the session. `--wait` blocks until it is ready. |
| `restart <session_id>` | Stop and start the sandbox in place. The disk is kept. When the sandbox is gone, boot a new one from the session's branch. |
| `reload <session_id>` | Pull the repository and load the base branch's agent config into the running sandbox. It refuses during a turn unless you pass `--force`. `--status` only reports whether the session is behind. |
| `model <session_id> <model-id>` | Change the model. A running sandbox restarts its agent runtime, which ends the current turn. |
| `compact <session_id>` | Summarize the conversation and continue from the summary. |
| `rename <session_id> <name>` | Set the name. `""` restores the automatic title. |
| `update [<session_id>]` | Add or remove labels and metadata. |
| `rm <session_id>…` | Stop and delete sessions. Each one is restorable for 30 days; after that its messages, files and branch are deleted. Prints the `restore` command for each session. `--json` adds `restore_until` and `project_id`. |
| `restore <session_id>` | Restore a deleted session within 30 days. Pass the full session ID (`ls --deleted` lists it), and `--project` when the session is not in the linked project. |

#### Access

| Command | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `share <session_id> --mode private\|project\|members` | Set who in Kortix can open the session. `--member <id\|email>`, `--group <id>`. |
| `scope <session_id>` | Read or replace the session's secrets and connections. The change applies to the next prompt. |
| `links <session_id>` | Public links to one preview port or one file: `ls`, `create`, `revoke <share-id>`. |
| `open <session_id>` | Open the session in the web app. |

### Run in CI and scripts

Give the job a token in the environment. The CLI then needs no `kortix login`:

| Variable | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `KORTIX_TOKEN` | A personal access key (`kortix_pat_`). |
| `KORTIX_PROJECT_ID` | The project to act on, when the job runs outside a linked directory. |
| `KORTIX_API_URL` | The host's API URL. Leave it unset for Kortix Cloud. |

`KORTIX_TOKEN` outranks any stored login, and the CLI never writes it to disk. Create a key that can reach only one project:

```sh
kortix tokens new ci-nightly --project <project_id> --expires 90d
```

The key prints once. Store it in your CI secret store. `--expires` takes an ISO-8601 date or a span such as `30d`, `12h`, `6w` or `1y`.

```yaml
# .github/workflows/nightly-report.yml
name: Nightly report
on:
  schedule:
    - cron: "0 6 * * *"
jobs:
  report:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    env:
      KORTIX_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.KORTIX_TOKEN }}
      KORTIX_PROJECT_ID: ${{ vars.KORTIX_PROJECT_ID }}
    steps:
      - run: curl -fsSL https://kortix.com/install | bash
      - run: |
          id=$(kortix sessions new --prompt "Write the nightly report" --wait --json | jq -r .session_id)
          kortix sessions wait-for "$id" --timeout 1800
          kortix sessions log "$id" --limit 1
```

Pipe `--json` output into `jq`. Update checks are off when `CI` is set or stdout is not a terminal. To start sessions on a schedule inside Kortix itself, use [Triggers & reminders](/docs/#automations).

### Use Kortix from your coding agent

A coding agent such as Claude Code, Codex, Cursor or pi needs only the binary, a login and the platform manual.

<Steps>
<Step title="Install and sign in">

Install the CLI and run `kortix login`, or set `KORTIX_TOKEN` in the agent's environment.

</Step>
<Step title="Check the setup">

```sh
kortix doctor
```

`kortix doctor` checks the login and the project, starts a throwaway session, sends `ping`, waits up to 180 seconds for a reply and deletes the session. It exits `0` when every check passes and `1` when one fails. `--no-session` stops after the login and project checks.

</Step>
<Step title="Load the manual">

```sh
kortix system-skills get kortix-system
```

The host serves the Kortix system skills live, so they match the version you talk to. Tell your agent to run this command first.

</Step>
<Step title="Wire the project into local tools">

In a cloned project, run:

```sh
kortix init --force
```

It asks which local coding tools to wire. `--primary <tool>` and `--agents <list>` answer without a prompt: `opencode`, `claude`, `codex`, `pi`, `cursor`. It links the project's skills where each tool looks for them, and adds a root `AGENTS.md` pointer for Codex, pi and Cursor. It does not change how sessions run in the cloud.

</Step>
<Step title="Delegate work">

```sh
id=$(kortix sessions new --prompt "Fix the failing export test" --wait --json | jq -r .session_id)
kortix sessions wait-for "$id" --timeout 900
case $? in
  0) kortix sessions log "$id" ;;
  3) kortix sessions pending "$id" ;;
esac
```

Exit `3` means the agent waits on a question or a permission. Answer it with `kortix sessions answer` or `kortix sessions approve`, then wait again.

</Step>
</Steps>

An MCP client can use Kortix without the CLI. See [Kortix MCP server](/docs/#connectors).

### Inside a session sandbox

Every sandbox has the CLI installed and signed in. The platform sets `KORTIX_TOKEN`, `KORTIX_API_URL` and `KORTIX_PROJECT_ID`, so the agent runs commands with no login and no link. The token is a session token: `kortix token` shows its session, its agent and its grants. It acts only on the session's own project.

Commands an agent uses:

| Command | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `kortix connectors call <slug> <action> [json]` | Call a connector action. See [Connectors](/docs/#connectors). |
| `kortix secrets request <NAME>` | Mint a link, valid for 7 days, for a person to enter a secret value. |
| `kortix secrets sync` | Pull this session's secrets and grants now. |
| `kortix cr open --title "<text>"` | Open a change request from the session branch. Inside a session it fills `--head` itself. |
| `kortix review submit --kind <kind> --title "<text>"` | Submit an output, a decision or a batch for review. |
| `kortix remind "<prompt>" --in 24h` | Prompt this session again later. Reminders are a project flag, on by default; `kortix projects features disable reminders` turns them off. |
| `kortix sessions update --label <label>` | Label the session the agent runs in. |

What an agent may do is set by its `kortix_permissions` in `kortix.yaml`. When the API refuses a command, the CLI prints the token kind, the agent and its grant. See [Agents & permissions](/docs/#agents). These commands are refused for an agent session's token, whatever the grant:

- `kortix projects cli-tokens new` and `rm`
- `kortix gateway keys` (`ls`, `new`, `rm`)
- `kortix sandboxes provider <name>` and `--clear`

`kortix update` is refused in a sandbox. The platform manages the binary: restart the session to get the current CLI.

A `.kortix/link.json` for another project needs a stored login for that project's host. Run `kortix login` in the sandbox first.

### Check and validate

| Command | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `kortix validate` | Check `kortix.yaml` against the schema, lint each `sandbox.templates` Dockerfile, and warn about large files in git. Exit `0` valid, `1` errors, `2` file not found. `--file <path>`, `--json`, `--no-dockerfile-lint`. `kortix ship` runs the same checks. |
| `kortix schema` | Print the `kortix.yaml` JSON Schema. `--version 1\|2\|3` picks one version. `--url` prints the schema's URL instead, such as `https://kortix.com/schema/kortix.v3.schema.json` for version 3. |
| `kortix doctor` | Check the login and project, then start a throwaway session, send `ping` and wait for a reply. `--no-session`, `--keep-session`, `--prompt "<text>"`, `--timeout <seconds>` (default 180). Exit `0` pass, `1` fail. |
| `kortix system-skills list` | List the Kortix system skills. `kortix skills` is the same command. |
| `kortix system-skills get <name>` | Print one skill and the paths of its reference files. `--full` adds every reference file. |
| `kortix system-skills file <name> <path>` | Print one reference file. |
| `kortix system-skills path [<name>]` | Print the skill's directory on disk. |

The schema fields are on [kortix.yaml reference](/docs/#kortixyaml-reference).

### Command map

Every group prints its subcommands and flags with `kortix <group> --help`. The linked page explains the feature.

#### Sign in, sessions and the CLI

| Group | Purpose | Page |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `login`, `logout`, `whoami`, `token`, `hosts` | Sign in and pick a host. | [Sign in](#sign-in) |
| `sessions`, `chat`, `connect` | Start, talk to and manage sessions. | [Work with sessions from a terminal](#work-with-sessions-from-a-terminal) |
| `tui` | The terminal app. | [TUI](/docs/#everywhere) |
| `validate`, `schema`, `doctor`, `system-skills` | Check the project and the setup. | [Check and validate](#check-and-validate) |
| `version`, `update`, `uninstall`, `feedback` | Manage the CLI itself. | [Maintain the CLI](#maintain-the-cli) |

#### Projects and git

| Group | Purpose | Page |
| --- | --- | --- |
