`kortix self-host init` writes `docker-compose.yml`, `.env`, a `Caddyfile` and `updater.sh` into `~/.config/kortix/self-host/<instance>/`. `kortix self-host start` runs `docker compose up`. One Compose project, `kortix-<instance>`, holds every service.

| Service | What it does |
|---|---|
| `frontend` | The web app. |
| `kortix-api` | The REST API at `/v1`. It relays any `/v1/llm` request that reaches it to `llm-gateway`. |
| `llm-gateway` | The standalone LLM gateway. Caddy sends `api.<domain>/v1/llm*` here. |
| `kortix-migrate` | A one-shot job. It migrates the database before `kortix-api` starts. |
| `kortix-updater` | Pulls new images once a day, migrates, and rolls the app services. It mounts the Docker socket. |
| `supabase-*` | The Supabase distribution, every image pinned by digest: Postgres (`supabase-db`), auth, REST, realtime, storage, imgproxy, meta, functions, Kong, the Supavisor pooler, Studio, analytics and vector. |
| `caddy` | Domain mode only. Terminates TLS on ports 80 and 443 with ACME HTTP-01 and routes traffic. |
| `cloudflared` | Tunnel mode only. Publishes `kortix-api` through a Cloudflare tunnel. |

Off the box:

- **Sandboxes.** Every session runs on Platinum, the reference sandbox provider. `kortix-api` creates them with `PLATINUM_API_KEY`. The sandbox calls back to the instance's public API URL, `KORTIX_URL`.
- **Images.** `kortix/kortix-frontend`, `kortix/kortix-api` and `kortix/kortix-gateway` on Docker Hub. Pulls need no credentials.
- **Model providers.** The gateway calls the providers your projects connect.

#### Reachability modes

| Mode | How you set it | Public URLs | App replicas | Sessions run |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| domain | `--domain <domain>` | `https://<domain>` and `https://api.<domain>` | 2 each of `kortix-api`, `llm-gateway`, `frontend` behind Caddy | yes |
| tunnel | `--tunnel cloudflare` | The web app stays on `http://localhost:13737`. Only the API is public, at the tunnel URL. | 1 each | yes |
| local | neither flag | none: every port binds `127.0.0.1` | 1 each | no, the sandbox cannot reach the API |

Domain mode is the production setup. Tunnel mode is for evaluation on your own machine. Without a domain, the services publish loopback ports: frontend `13737`, API `13738`, Supabase `13740`. When the stack is stopped, `start` moves a port that is in use to a free one.

### Requirements

- **A Linux server** with a public IP. The bootstrap installs Docker Engine and the Compose plugin. On a server that has them, install only the CLI. The CLI runs on Linux and macOS, on x86_64 and arm64.
- **DNS.** A or AAAA records for `<domain>` and `api.<domain>`, both pointed at the server, before the first start. Caddy needs them to get the certificate.
- **Ports 80 and 443** open inbound.
- **Wildcard records** for the optional origins:
  - `*.p.<domain>` for sandbox previews. Use it on every public instance: without it, browser previews fall back to a path proxy that breaks root-absolute links, and `doctor` fails.
  - `*.<apps base domain>`, for example `*.apps.<domain>`, to host [Apps](/docs/#apps-reference).
- **A Platinum API key** (`PLATINUM_API_KEY`). Without it, sessions do not start.
- **A model provider key.** You add it in the web app after the first sign-in.

### Install

#### Bootstrap a bare server

The bootstrap script runs on Linux only. It installs Docker and the Compose plugin when they are missing, installs the CLI, runs `kortix self-host init`, sets the domain, and runs `kortix self-host start`. Re-running it is safe.

| Flag | Default | Sets |
|---|---|---|
| `--domain <domain>` | none | `KORTIX_DOMAIN`. Turns on Caddy and TLS. |
| `--api-domain <domain>` | `api.<domain>` | `KORTIX_API_DOMAIN` |
| `--email <email>` | `admin@<domain>` | `KORTIX_ACME_EMAIL`, the certificate contact |
| `--channel <stable\|latest>` | `stable` | The image channel |
| `--auto-update <on\|off>` | `on` | The daily updater |
| `--instance <name>` | `default` | The instance name |

The bootstrap runs `init` non-interactively. It sets no admin email, no Platinum key and no preview domain, so run `kortix self-host configure` and then `kortix self-host start` after it. When the script adds your user to the `docker` group, open a new shell before the next `kortix self-host` command.

#### Install by hand

Use this path on a server that already has Docker and the Compose plugin.

```sh
curl -fsSL https://kortix.com/install | bash
kortix self-host init --domain kortix.example.com --admin-email you@example.com
kortix self-host start
```

A first `init` in a terminal asks every setup question. `--yes`, or a shell with no terminal, skips the questions and keeps the defaults. `start` runs `init` first when the instance has no configuration. When the sandbox key is missing, `init` and `start` print a warning and continue.

#### Evaluate with a Cloudflare tunnel

To try Kortix on your own machine with no domain:

```sh
kortix self-host init --tunnel cloudflare
kortix self-host start
```

The web app opens at `http://localhost:13737`. `cloudflared` publishes the API at an `https://<random>.trycloudflare.com` URL. That URL changes each time `cloudflared` restarts, and `start` and `update` capture the new one. For a stable URL, create a named tunnel in Cloudflare Zero Trust, point its public hostname at `http://kortix-api:8008`, then set both values:

```sh
kortix self-host env set CLOUDFLARE_TUNNEL_TOKEN=<token> CLOUDFLARE_TUNNEL_HOSTNAME=kortix-api.example.com
kortix self-host start
```

### Configure

`kortix self-host configure` asks the setup questions again, in this order. Each answer is pre-filled with the current value, so Enter keeps it. `configure` writes the files only. Run `kortix self-host start` to apply them.

| Step | What it sets |
|---|---|
| Reachability | `domain` (domain, API subdomain, ACME email) or `tunnel` (quick or named tunnel). |
| Admin email | `KORTIX_PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS`, comma-separated. These people are platform admins. Also `--admin-email <email>`. |
| Enterprise license | `ENTERPRISE_LICENSE_AVAILABLE`. With a license, it unlocks SSO, SCIM, role-based access control and audit. Also `--enterprise-license`. |
| Restrict account creation | On by default. Only a platform admin can create a new account. Others get `403` `account_creation_restricted`. Sign-ups, invites, SSO and each person's personal account still work. Also `--no-restrict-account-creation` and `--restrict-account-creation`. |
| Sandbox provider | `platinum` (the default) or the legacy `daytona`, written to `ALLOWED_SANDBOX_PROVIDERS`, plus that provider's settings (below). |
| Apps hosting | Optional. `KORTIX_APPS_BASE_DOMAIN`, which needs a wildcard DNS record. |
| Preview origins | `KORTIX_PREVIEW_BASE_DOMAIN`, suggested `p.<domain>`, which needs a `*.p.<domain>` record. In domain mode the default answer is configure. |
| Composio | Optional. `COMPOSIO_API_KEY`, the app catalog for [connectors](/docs/#connectors). |
| Update policy | Auto-update on or off, channel, daily time and timezone. |

| Provider | Key | Other settings and defaults |
|---|---|---|
| `platinum` | `PLATINUM_API_KEY` | `PLATINUM_API_URL` (`https://api.platinum.dev`), `PLATINUM_TEMPLATE` (optional) |
| `daytona` (legacy) | `DAYTONA_API_KEY` | `DAYTONA_SERVER_URL` (`https://app.daytona.io/api`), `DAYTONA_TARGET` (`us`) |

<Callout type="warning">
**Legacy: Daytona.** Daytona is a legacy fallback provider. Its removal is scheduled. Existing Daytona sessions keep working for now, but Daytona gets no new features and no support guarantee. Use Platinum. E2B support is discontinued.
</Callout>

SAML sign-in is on in every instance, and `init` generates `SAML_PRIVATE_KEY` once. Do not change that key after you register an identity provider: it is the instance's SAML identity. To set up SSO, see [Sign-in, SSO & API keys](/docs/#security).

### First sign-in and setup

1. **Sign up as the admin.** Open `https://<domain>` and sign up with an address in `KORTIX_PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS`. A password, email-code or social sign-in matches that list. A SAML sign-in never does. To change the admins later, set the full comma-separated list: `kortix self-host env set KORTIX_PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS=you@example.com,ops@example.com`. The value replaces the list.
2. **Connect managed git.** Open `/admin/git`. Kortix creates and pushes every new project repository through this one GitHub identity. Pick **Create a GitHub App** (recommended), **Paste an existing App**, or **Use an access token**. Until it is set, Kortix cannot create project repositories. You can still connect an existing repository. See [Git](/docs/#git).
3. **Create the team account.** Your sign-up account is personal. It cannot hold SAML SSO (`403` `sso_personal_account`), and a project cannot move to another account. The menu that creates accounts lives inside a project, so first create one project at `/new`. In it, open the project menu → **Switch Project** → **Create an account…**. Only platform admins can create accounts by default. Invite your team and set up SSO in this account.
4. **Create a project** in the team account. After **Create an account…**, Kortix opens `/new` with the new account selected.
5. **Add a model key** in the project: **Customize → Models → Providers**, or `kortix providers set <provider>`. Each project needs its own key. A self-hosted instance has no Kortix-managed models. See [Connect your own provider](/docs/#models).
6. **Turn on email.** Set one connection string:

   ```sh
   kortix self-host env set EMAIL_URL=smtp://user:password@smtp.example.com:587
   ```

   Kortix then sends sign-in and invitation mail through it, requires email confirmation for new accounts, and adds magic-link sign-in. `EMAIL_FROM` defaults to `Kortix <noreply@<domain>>`. Other forms: `smtps://user:password@host:465`, `resend://<api-key>`, `ses://<key-id>:<secret>@<region>`, `ses://<region>`, `mailtrap://<api-token>`. Separate several with commas for a fallback chain, tried left to right.
7. **Close sign-up.** When your team has signed up, run `kortix self-host env set DISABLE_SIGNUP=true`, then `kortix self-host start` so the auth service reads it.

A new instance starts with these defaults:

| Setting | Default |
|---|---|
| Sign-in methods | Password only. Email sign-ups confirm without an email, so a sign-up's address is unproven. See [Unconfirmed email addresses](#unconfirmed-email-addresses). |
| Social sign-in | None |
| Billing | Off |
| Marketing pages | Off. Every marketing route opens the web app. Turn on with `KORTIX_PUBLIC_DISABLE_LANDING_PAGE=false`. |
| Connector app catalog | Hidden until `COMPOSIO_API_KEY` is set |
| Account creation | Platform admins only |

### Secure the instance

`kortix self-host doctor` runs these checks. It exits `0` when all pass and `1` when one fails. `--json` prints the result as JSON.

| Check | Fails when | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| `docker` | `docker --version` fails | Install Docker. |
| `docker-compose` | The Compose plugin is missing | Install the Compose plugin. |
| `compose-config` | The rendered `docker-compose.yml` is not valid | Correct the value the error names with `env set`. |
| `instance-dir` | The instance directory moved since the last write | Run `kortix self-host configure`, or any of `init`, `start`, `update`, `env set`. |
| `email-url` | `EMAIL_URL` is set but does not parse | Correct the value. |
| `preview-origins` | Domain mode and `KORTIX_PREVIEW_BASE_DOMAIN` is empty | Point `*.p.<domain>` at the server, then `kortix self-host env set KORTIX_PREVIEW_BASE_DOMAIN=p.<domain> KORTIX_PREVIEW_ALLOW_DIRECT_EDGE=true`. |
| `open-sign-up` | A domain or tunnel instance has sign-up open and email confirmation off | Set `EMAIL_URL`, or set `DISABLE_SIGNUP=true` once your team is in. |

<Callout type="warning">
A new instance confirms every sign-up without an email. Until you fix `open-sign-up`, anyone who reaches the instance can create a confirmed account for any address, an admin address included. Sign up as the admin first, then set `EMAIL_URL` or `DISABLE_SIGNUP=true`. `kortix self-host init` prints the same warning on a domain or tunnel instance. `init --disable-signup` turns sign-up off from the first start, for an install that creates its accounts another way: while it is off, nobody can sign up, the admin included.
</Callout>

What the stack does by itself:

- Only Caddy publishes public ports, 80 and 443. Every other published port binds `127.0.0.1`.
- `.env` and `docker-compose.yml` are written with mode `0600`.
- [Connectors](/docs/#connectors) refuse private and internal addresses with `connector_egress_blocked`. List the internal hosts they may reach in `KORTIX_CONNECTOR_EGRESS_ALLOW_HOSTS`.
- `kortix-updater` holds the Docker socket, so its image is pinned by digest.

Keep `.env` safe: it holds every secret, and `API_KEY_SECRET` in it encrypts every stored secret, connector credential and token. A lost `API_KEY_SECRET` makes them unreadable. The Secrets page marks each such project secret **Undecryptable**, and **Customize → Models → Providers** does the same for each account key and ChatGPT login. A connector credential shows no mark: a call through it returns `credential_undecryptable`. A session start names the first value it needs, and any other request that needs one answers `409 SECRET_UNDECRYPTABLE`. Enter each value again to fix it, and save each OAuth application again before you reconnect its account.

#### Unconfirmed email addresses

`ENABLE_EMAIL_AUTOCONFIRM=true` is the default until you set `EMAIL_URL`. Auth then confirms each sign-up without an email, and the API reads the same value from `.env`. While it is `true`, the API treats the email of a password, email-code or social login as unproven, because whoever signed up first holds the address:

| Path | With `ENABLE_EMAIL_AUTOCONFIRM=true` |
|---|---|
| Sign-in with a pending invite | Sign-in does not accept the invite. |
| SCIM `POST /Users` for that address | Creates a pending invite (`201`). No membership. A password login does not see that invite, and accepting or declining it returns `403` `email_unconfirmed`. The invite page then shows **Continue with single sign-on**: it signs in through the account's SAML provider, also for an address outside the provider's domain, and the person joins. An admin can instead click **Resend** on the pending invite: it is then an invite a person sent. |
| **Members** → **Invite** for that address | Creates a pending invite instead of adding the login. |
| An invite a person sent or resent (**Members** → **Invite** or **Resend**, a project invite) | The login that holds the address sees it in the project chooser and the **Switch Project** menu, and can accept it. That login is whoever signed up first with the address, so it may not be the person you invited. |
| `KORTIX_PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS` | Matches a password login with the address. Whoever signs up first with an allowlisted address is platform admin. |

A login that is already a member of the account, and a login through the account's own SAML provider, still match inside that account. `KORTIX_PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS` never matches a SAML login. Setting `EMAIL_URL` turns `ENABLE_EMAIL_AUTOCONFIRM` off and restores the matching. A login created while it was on is not confirmed again: after you add email, its address matches invites and SCIM like a confirmed one. Set `EMAIL_URL` before you invite people. Without it, invite a person only after that person has signed up: an address has one password login, so nobody else can take it then. A cloud instance never sets the value.

### The platform admin console

Platform admins open `/admin`. On a self-hosted instance, a platform admin is a person listed in `KORTIX_PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS`.

| Page | Path | What it holds |
|---|---|---|
| Overview | `/admin` | Live instance numbers, refreshed every 15 seconds. |
| Accounts | `/admin/accounts` | Every account. Filter, sort and inspect one. |
| Projects | `/admin/projects` | Every project across every account, most active first, with its operator-managed [feature flags](/docs). |
| Analytics | `/admin/analytics` | Daily platform activity, by UTC day. |
| Sandboxes | `/admin/sandboxes` | Where new sandboxes are placed, and the live fleet. |
| Git | `/admin/git` | The instance's managed GitHub identity. When `MANAGED_GIT_*` environment variables set it, the page is read-only. |
| Maintenance | `/admin/utils` | System-wide notices and the full-lockdown switch. |

### Connect your clients

`kortix self-host start` registers the CLI host `selfhost` on the server. To point the web, CLI, TUI, desktop and mobile clients at the instance, see [Connect to a self-hosted instance](/docs/#everywhere).

### Integrations

Set these with `kortix self-host env set`. Each row says what the variable turns on or off.

| Variables | Effect |
|---|---|
| `COMPOSIO_API_KEY` | The connector app catalog. Without it, Easy connect, the `/connections` page and the onboarding apps step are hidden. Custom connectors work without it. |
| `COMPOSIO_WEBHOOK_SECRET` | Composio [connector events](/docs/#automations) (the `event_triggers` flag, **Composio event triggers**). Without it, `POST /v1/webhooks/events/composio` answers `503` `event_ingress_not_configured`. |
| `KORTIX_EVENT_POLLS_ENABLED` | Default `true`. `false` pauses every check of the connector events that Kortix checks itself ([source `mcp`](/docs/#automations)). The triggers stay, and fires already queued still run. Checks resume when it is `true` again. |
| `KORTIX_OAUTH_CLIENTS` | Your own OAuth clients for the [app catalog](/docs/#connectors) apps whose servers have no dynamic client registration. JSON, one entry per vendor: `google`, `github`, `slack`, `hubspot`, `asana`, `box`. Without an entry, those apps are not listed. See [Kortix OAuth clients](#kortix-oauth-clients). |
| `SLACK_CLIENT_ID`, `SLACK_CLIENT_SECRET`, `SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET` | One-click **Add to Slack** with your own Slack app. Without all three, each project connects its own Slack app. See [Slack, Teams & email](/docs/#channels). |
| `MICROSOFT_APP_ID`, `MICROSOFT_APP_PASSWORD`, `MICROSOFT_APP_TENANT` (default `botframework.com`) | One shared Microsoft Teams bot. Without them, each project brings its own bot app. |
| `RESEND_API_KEY` | Transactional email, and the Resend team that holds managed inboxes for the email channel. |
| `RESEND_MANAGED_EMAIL_ENABLED` | `true` offers Kortix-managed inboxes on `RESEND_MANAGED_DOMAIN`. Unset: projects connect email only with their own Resend key. Each managed inbox is billed to the deployment's Resend team. |
| `RESEND_MANAGED_DOMAIN` | Domain for managed inboxes, verified with receiving on in that Resend team. |
| `TUNNEL_ENABLED` | Default `true`. `false` turns off [computers](/docs/#computers): `/v1/tunnel/*` answers `503` `tunnel_disabled`. |
| `PLATINUM_API_KEY` | Project Files (`volumes`). |

OAuth callbacks, such as `<KORTIX_URL>/v1/connectors/oauth2/callback` and the Slack and Teams redirects, use `KORTIX_URL`. Domain mode and a named tunnel keep it stable. A quick tunnel changes it at every restart and breaks every callback you registered.

#### Kortix OAuth clients

Some MCP servers in the catalog do not let Kortix register itself. Kortix signs in to them with an OAuth client that you create at the vendor once per instance.

1. At the vendor, create an OAuth app with the redirect URI `<KORTIX_URL>/v1/connectors/oauth2/callback`.
   - Google: a **Web application** client. Select **Use this client for an AI-powered agent**, enable the Gmail, Calendar, Drive, Docs and Sheets APIs and their MCP services, and add the scopes from Google's [MCP server guide](https://developers.google.com/workspace/guides/configure-mcp-servers). The Workspace MCP servers are a Google developer preview.
   - GitHub: an OAuth app (not a GitHub App).
2. Store the clients as one JSON value:

   ```sh
   kortix self-host env set KORTIX_OAUTH_CLIENTS='{"google":{"client_id":"<id>","client_secret":"<secret>"},"github":{"client_id":"<id>","client_secret":"<secret>"}}'
   ```

3. Restart the API. The apps of each configured vendor appear in the catalog.

#### Receive Composio events

One Composio project has one webhook URL, so it serves one instance.

1. Set `COMPOSIO_API_KEY`.
2. In a checkout of `kortix-ai/suna`, run the subscription script with [Bun](https://bun.sh). Add `--dry-run` to see the plan first.

   ```sh
   cd apps/api
   COMPOSIO_API_KEY=<key> bun scripts/composio-webhook-subscription.ts \
     --url https://api.kortix.example.com/v1/webhooks/events/composio
   ```

3. The script prints the signing secret once, when it creates the subscription. Store it: `kortix self-host env set COMPOSIO_WEBHOOK_SECRET=<secret>`.

#### Composio toolkits that need your own OAuth app

Some toolkits have no Composio-managed sign-in. Syncing one fails with `422` `composio_auth_config_required`. In the Composio dashboard, open **Auth Configs** and create one with your own OAuth app. Then sync again. No restart is needed.

### Updates and versions

`kortix-updater` checks once a day at `KORTIX_UPDATE_TIME` (default `02:00`) in `KORTIX_UPDATE_TZ` (default `America/New_York`). Auto-update is on by default.

| Track | Images |
|---|---|
| `stable` (default) | A release that Kortix promotes to self-hosted instances after it runs in production. |
| `latest` | Every Kortix release. |
| `--tag <version>` | One exact version. A pin wins over the channel. |

Each run:

1. Checks free disk space. It stops below `KORTIX_MIN_FREE_DISK_MB` (default `2048`).
2. Pulls the configured tags. When no image changed, it stops.
3. Runs `kortix-migrate`. When a migration fails, nothing is swapped and the previous version keeps serving.
4. Rolls `kortix-api`, `llm-gateway` and `frontend`. With 2 replicas, new containers must pass their health check within `KORTIX_ROLLOUT_TIMEOUT` (default `300` seconds) before the old ones stop, so the instance stays up. A swap that fails keeps the previous version. With 1 replica, each service restarts in place with a short interruption.
5. Removes superseded images after a fully successful run. It keeps the current and the previous version for a fast rollback.

`kortix self-host status` shows the last outcome (`ok`, `degraded`, `failed`, `skipped` or `never-run`), the version change, a drift check of running images against the configuration, and the schedule. Set `KORTIX_UPDATE_WEBHOOK_URL` to receive each run's status as a JSON `POST`.

```sh
kortix self-host update                          # apply the channel now
kortix self-host update --channel latest         # switch channel
kortix self-host update --tag <version>          # pin one version
kortix self-host rollback --release <version>    # go back to a version
kortix self-host version                         # running, latest and channel
```

A manual `update` uses the same rollout as the daily run. When another update holds the lock, it exits `75` and skips the rollout. A pin holds through every daily run. `init` or `update` without `--tag` moves the instance back to its channel. To change auto-update, the time or the timezone, run `kortix self-host configure`, then `kortix self-host start`.

#### Saved conversations after an update

Since the session log release, every session's saved conversation is its session log. An instance that ran an older version also holds conversations in the older v1 store. After the update, the API converts them by itself, so you do nothing:

- A background pass (`SESSION_LOG_BACKFILL`, default `write`) converts every old session, oldest first, on the leader API container. It logs `[session-log] backfill tick` with counts, and `the v1 mirror holds no session without a log` when it is done.
- Opening an old session before the pass reaches it converts that session first.
- A conversation over the conversion size cap (about 24 MB, `SESSION_LOG_RESTORE_MAX_BYTES`) is converted only by the background pass. Until then, its log read answers `503` `session_log_converting` with `Retry-After: 60`.

`SESSION_LOG_BACKFILL=off` stops the pass. Keep it on until that line appears: the v1 store is the only copy of a conversation the pass has not converted.

### Operate and tune

#### Settings and secrets

Every setting lives in the instance `.env`.

- `kortix self-host env ls` lists every value by group and masks secrets. `--show` reveals them.
- `kortix self-host env set KEY=VALUE ...` writes values and restarts the services that read them. A bare `KEY` prompts for the value. A new `API_KEY_SECRET` makes every stored credential undecryptable, so `env set` warns first: at a terminal it asks you to confirm, and a script must pass `--yes` (without it the command exits `2` and writes nothing).
- `kortix self-host env rotate <KEY>` or `--all-generated` regenerates a generated secret. Rotating `SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET` also regenerates the anon and service-role keys. The CLI refuses `API_KEY_SECRET`, `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` and `SAML_PRIVATE_KEY`, because a new value breaks stored data or connections.

`env set` refuses the image and version keys (`KORTIX_VERSION`, `API_IMAGE`, `FRONTEND_IMAGE`, `GATEWAY_IMAGE`). Use `--tag` or `--channel` instead. When a change does not take effect, run `kortix self-host start`: it recreates every service whose configuration changed.

#### Memory limits

| Variable | Default | Container |
|---|---|---|
| `KORTIX_API_MEMORY_LIMIT` | `2048m` | `kortix-api`, which relays `/v1/llm` request bodies when they reach it |
| `KORTIX_GATEWAY_MEMORY_LIMIT` | `2048m` | `llm-gateway` |
| `KORTIX_FRONTEND_MEMORY_LIMIT` | `512m` | `frontend` |

Postgres has a fixed limit of `1280m`. `KORTIX_GATEWAY_MAX_REQUEST_BYTES` raises the gateway's request-body cap for image-heavy turns. Raise the gateway memory limit with it.

#### Session and sandbox lifecycle

| Variable | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `KORTIX_SANDBOX_AUTOSTOP_MINUTES` | `15` | A sandbox stops this many idle minutes after its last turn ends. The same window bounds how long an open session page holds the sandbox (KRTX-2451): the page never keeps it past this. |
| `KORTIX_SANDBOX_TURN_GRANT_MINUTES` | `240` | How long a running turn holds its sandbox. Model calls and an observed running turn renew it. |
| `KORTIX_SANDBOX_TURN_WAITING_MINUTES` | `120` | How long a turn that waits on a person holds its sandbox. At most half the turn grant. Unattended runs get 30. |
| `KORTIX_SANDBOX_PREVIEW_GRANT_MINUTES` | `30` | How long preview traffic keeps a sandbox running. |
| `KORTIX_SANDBOX_WARM_GRANT_MINUTES` | `60` | The lifetime of an unclaimed warm sandbox. |
| `KORTIX_SANDBOX_IDLE_DELETE_MINUTES` | `60` | A sandbox of a session whose `/workspace` is a Platinum volume (`workspace_volume`) is deleted after it has been stopped this many minutes. The next open boots a new sandbox on the same volume and restores the conversation from the session log. `-1` keeps every stopped sandbox. |
| `KORTIX_WORKSPACE_VOLUMES` | on | `off` gives no new session a `/workspace` volume, whatever the project's `workspace_volume` flag says. A session that has a volume keeps it. |
| `KORTIX_SANDBOX_PROVIDER_AUTOSTOP_MINUTES` | `720` | The provider's own idle stop, a backstop for sandboxes the API cannot reach. |
| `KORTIX_SANDBOX_AUTOARCHIVE_MINUTES` | `720` | Legacy Daytona only: a stopped sandbox moves to archive storage. |
| `KORTIX_SANDBOX_AUTODELETE_MINUTES` | `-1` | Legacy Daytona only: never delete automatically. |
| `ALLOWED_SANDBOX_PROVIDERS` | `platinum` | The providers sessions may use, comma-separated. `daytona` is a legacy fallback. |
| `KORTIX_DEFAULT_SANDBOX_CPU`, `_MEMORY_GB`, `_DISK_GB` | `2`, `4`, `20` | The sandbox size when a template sets none. |

Users see the effect of these values on [Sessions](/docs/#computers) and [Sandboxes & previews](/docs/#computers).

#### Logs, containers and the database console

- `kortix self-host logs [service]` follows the logs, for example `kortix self-host logs kortix-api`. Each container keeps 3 log files of 10 MB.
- `kortix self-host ps` lists the containers. `status` adds the update report.
- Supabase Studio answers on `http://127.0.0.1:13740` (`SUPABASE_PORT`) on the server. Reach it through an SSH tunnel and sign in as `kortix` with `DASHBOARD_PASSWORD` from `env ls --show`.

#### Several instances on one server

Pass `--instance <name>` to every command. A name starts with a letter and holds letters, digits, dots, underscores and dashes. Each instance has its own directory and Compose project. Only one instance per server can run in domain mode, because Caddy binds ports 80 and 443. `KORTIX_SELF_HOST_CONFIG_DIR` moves the root directory from `~/.config/kortix/self-host`.

#### How Caddy routes

| Host | Path | Goes to |
|---|---|---|
| `api.<domain>` | `/v1/llm*` | `llm-gateway:8090` |
| `api.<domain>` | everything else | `kortix-api:8008` |
| `<domain>` | `/rest/v1`, `/auth/v1`, `/storage/v1`, `/realtime/v1`, `/functions/v1`, `/graphql/v1` | `supabase-kong:8000` |
| `<domain>` | everything else | `frontend:3000` |
| `*.<preview base domain>` | all | `kortix-api`, one origin per sandbox port |
| `*.<apps base domain>` | all | `kortix-api`, one hostname per App |

Caddy health-checks each replica and retries a failed connection on the other one. It sends `Strict-Transport-Security` with a 30-day max-age. The two wildcard blocks exist only when their base domain is set. Caddy gets one certificate per hostname on its first request. The API approves each hostname before Caddy asks for the certificate. You need a wildcard DNS record, not a wildcard certificate.

### Back up and uninstall

The instance directory, `~/.config/kortix/self-host/<instance>/`, holds everything to back up:

| Path | Contents |
|---|---|
| `.env` | Every secret and signing key |
| `volumes/db/data` | The Postgres database |
| `volumes/storage` | Stored files |

Stop the stack so the database files are consistent, copy the directory, then start again:

```sh
kortix self-host stop
sudo tar -czf kortix-backup.tgz -C ~/.config/kortix/self-host default
kortix self-host start
```

To restore, put the directory back in the same place and run `kortix self-host start`.

<Callout type="warning">
`kortix self-host uninstall` runs `docker compose down --volumes --remove-orphans` and deletes the instance directory, with its database and files. It cannot be undone. In a terminal you type the instance name to confirm. Without a terminal it needs `--yes`.
</Callout>

### Commands

Every subcommand takes `--instance <name>` (default `default`).

| Command | What it does |
|---|---|
| `init` | Writes or refreshes the Compose file and `.env`. A first run in a terminal asks the setup questions. |
| `start` | Pulls the images and starts the stack. Alias `up`. |
| `stop` | Stops the stack. Data stays. Alias `down`. |
| `restart` | `stop`, then `start`. |
| `configure` | Asks the setup questions again. |
| `status` | Container state, the last update, drift and the schedule. `--json` |
| `ps` | `docker compose ps` only. |
| `doctor` | Runs the checks in [Secure the instance](#secure-the-instance). `--json` |
| `plan` | Validates the Compose file. Applies nothing. `--json` |
| `logs [service]` | Follows the logs. |
| `open` | Opens the web app in a browser. |
| `version` | The running version, the newest release and the channel. |
| `update` | Applies the channel, or `--tag <version>`, now. Aliases `upgrade`, `reconcile`. |
| `rollback --release <version>` | Updates to that version. `--tag` also works. |
| `env ls [--show]` | Lists every setting. `--json` |
| `env set KEY=VALUE ...` | Sets values and restarts the affected services. |
| `env rotate KEY` or `--all-generated` | Regenerates a generated secret. |
| `uninstall` | Deletes the stack, its data and its directory. |
| `connect-github` | Deprecated. Prints where to connect GitHub. Use `/admin/git`. |

| Flag | Used with | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `--domain <domain>` | `init`, `configure`, `update` | Domain mode. `--domain ""` clears the domain. |
| `--tunnel cloudflare` | `init`, `configure`, `update` | Tunnel mode. |
| `--tag <version>` | `init`, `update`, `rollback` | Pin a version. Aliases `--version`, `--release`. |
| `--channel <stable\|latest>` | `init`, `update`, `configure` | The channel to track. |
| `--auto-update <on\|off>` | `init`, `update`, `configure` | The daily updater. Takes effect at the next `start`. |
| `--update-time <HH:MM>`, `--update-tz <zone>` | `init`, `update`, `configure` | The daily window, as 24-hour time and an IANA zone. Takes effect at the next `start`. |
| `--admin-email <email>` | `init`, `configure` | Sets `KORTIX_PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS`. |
| `--enterprise-license` | `init`, `configure`, `update` | Sets `ENTERPRISE_LICENSE_AVAILABLE=true`. |
| `--restrict-account-creation`, `--no-restrict-account-creation` | `init`, `configure`, `update` | Account creation for admins only, or for everyone. |
| `--disable-signup` | `init`, `configure`, `update` | Sets `DISABLE_SIGNUP=true`. Nobody can sign up, the admin included. `env set DISABLE_SIGNUP=false` opens it again. |
| `--yes` | any | Accept defaults. Required for `uninstall` without a terminal. |
| `--json` | `status`, `doctor`, `plan`, `env ls` | Machine-readable output. |

## SDK

`@kortix/sdk` is the TypeScript client for the Kortix API. It covers projects, sessions, connectors, secrets, accounts and the live runtime of each session. Use it to start and drive sessions from your own app, server, script or CI job.

### Install

```bash
npm install @kortix/sdk
```

| Import | Use it for | Runs in |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `@kortix/sdk` | The client and every framework-free module. | Any runtime with a global `fetch`: browsers, Node 18 or later, Bun, edge workers. |
| `@kortix/sdk/react` | Hooks, `useSession` first. Needs the optional peers `react` (18 or later) and `@tanstack/react-query` (5.75.2 or a later 5.x). | React and React Native. |
| `@kortix/sdk/server` | `createScopedKortix`, `runWithKortix`, `createKortixAuth`, `createKortixAppGuard`, `readAppViewer`, `createAppViewerKortix`. It imports `node:async_hooks`. | Node and Bun. Never bundle it for a browser. |
| `@kortix/ui` | The Kortix React components and `@kortix/ui/session`, the whole session chat on top of `useSession`. A separate package: `npm i @kortix/ui`, the same version as `@kortix/sdk`. See [Kortix UI](/docs/#kortix-ui). | React hosts. |

Import from the root. Two other subpaths are stable. `@kortix/sdk/wire-message-id` holds the wire-id clock in one module with no imports, and the root exports the same names. `@kortix/sdk/workspace-search` adds ranked file search over a session workspace, and the root does not export it. The other subpaths (`@kortix/sdk/turns`, `@kortix/sdk/projects-client` and similar) are deprecated aliases of root names. The full list is in [Entry points and stability](/docs/#entry-points-and-stability).

For a page with no build step, load the browser bundle. It defines `window.Kortix` with the same names as the root, for example `Kortix.createKortix`:

```html
<script src="https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@kortix/sdk@<version>/dist/kortix.global.js"></script>
```

The package also ships an ES module bundle at `dist/kortix.esm.min.js`. Replace `<version>` with a published version.

A browser page calls the Kortix API directly only from an origin the API allows. Kortix Cloud allows its own origins. From your own site, send browser calls through your server, for example the same-origin proxy of [Sign in with Kortix](/docs/#sign-in-with-kortix). A self-hosted instance adds origins with the API's `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` setting.

### Create the client

Call `createKortix` once per host, with the API base URL and a function that returns a token.

```ts
import { createKortix } from '@kortix/sdk';

export const kortix = createKortix({
  backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1',
  getToken: async () => process.env.KORTIX_API_KEY ?? null,
});
```

| Option | Required | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `backendUrl` | yes | The API base URL, including `/v1`. Kortix Cloud is `https://api.kortix.com/v1`. A self-hosted instance is its API origin plus `/v1`. |
| `getToken` | yes | Returns the bearer token, or `null` when no one is signed in. The SDK calls it on every request and stores nothing. Your host owns storage and refresh. |
| `fetch` | no | A fetch implementation for tests, edge runtimes and adapters. Default: the global `fetch`. |
| `eventStreamTransport` | no | Delivers the live event stream's bytes when your `fetch` cannot stream a response body (React Native without `expo/fetch`). |
| `clientVersion` | no | Your surface and version as `<surface>/<version>`, for example `my-app/1.2.0`. The SDK sends it as `X-Kortix-Client-Version`. The API writes it to the request log only. |
| `onError` | no | An error sink for a toast or a log. Default: no-op. |
| `onAccessRemedy` | no | Receives `sso_required` or `account_mfa_required` when the API refuses a request until the person signs in through single sign-on or completes multi-factor authentication. It runs for every response of the REST client, not for the session runtime or its event stream. A browser also gets the `kortix:sso-required` and `kortix:mfa-required` window events. Default: no-op. |

The client holds one configuration for the whole process. A second `createKortix()` call with a different `backendUrl` or `getToken` re-points every earlier client and logs one console warning. A server that acts for many people at the same time uses `createScopedKortix()` from `@kortix/sdk/server`. See [Authentication](/docs/#sdk-authentication).

The client exposes these handles:

| Handle | What it reaches |
| --- | --- |
| `kortix.projects` | List, provision, read and update projects. |
| `kortix.project(projectId)` | Everything inside one project: `sessions`, `secrets`, `connectors`, `apps`, `triggers` and more, with the project id bound. |
| `kortix.session(projectId, sessionId)` | One session: readiness, prompts, the event stream, messages, files and previews. |
| `kortix.connector(slug)`, `kortix.connectors` | Connector calls with a token that already carries its project, such as a session token. |
| `kortix.auth`, `kortix.accounts`, `kortix.iam`, `kortix.billing`, `kortix.notifications` | Sign-in, accounts and members, access control, billing reads, and the caller's notifications. |
| `kortix.validateToken()` | Checks the token `getToken` returns now and gives `{ valid, identity?, error? }`. It never throws. |

The full map is in the [SDK reference](/docs/#sdk-reference).

### Get an API key

A script, a CI job or a server uses a personal access key (`kortix_pat_`, **Settings → Personal access keys** or `kortix tokens new`). Unattended automation uses a service account key (`kortix_sa_`, **Account settings → API keys**). Every credential: [Choose a credential](/docs/#choose-a-credential).

Kortix shows the key once. Store it as a secret and return it from `getToken`. Never put a personal access key in a browser bundle: a browser app signs people in with [Sign in with Kortix](/docs/#sign-in-with-kortix) instead.

### Your first session

A session is one unit of agent work, on its own sandbox and branch. From code, it takes six steps.

<Steps>
<Step title="Pick a project">
`kortix.projects.list()` returns the projects the token can open. `kortix.projects.provision({ name })` creates a project with a Kortix-managed repository and the starter configuration.
</Step>
<Step title="Create the session">
`kortix.project(projectId).sessions.create(input?)` returns the session with its `session_id`. The sandbox starts to provision now, in the background. All input fields are optional: `name`, `agent_name`, `model` (a bare managed id such as `glm-5.3-flash`, or `provider/model` for your own provider key; see [Models](/docs/#models)), `initial_prompt`, `labels`, `metadata`, `base_ref` and more. See [Create a session](/docs/#create-a-session).
</Step>
<Step title="Get the session handle">
`kortix.session(projectId, sessionId)` binds both ids. The handle has no network cost.
</Step>
<Step title="Wait until ready">
`session.ensureReady()` resolves when the runtime accepts work. It returns `runtimeSessionId`, the id of the root conversation in the runtime. It waits up to 180 seconds by default (`{ readyTimeoutMs }` changes it). It throws an `ApiError` with code `RUNTIME_UNAVAILABLE` when the sandbox fails or stops, or when the time runs out. `send()`, `stream()` and `messages()` call it for you.
</Step>
<Step title="Open the stream, then send">
Open `session.stream({ onEvent })` before `session.send(text)`, so no early event is lost. `send()` puts the prompt in the session's inbox and resolves when the prompt is stored, not when the turn ends. The turn ends on the `session.idle` event whose `sessionID` equals `runtimeSessionId`.
</Step>
<Step title="Read the conversation">
`session.messages()` returns `{ messages, hasMore }`. Each message is `{ info, parts }` in the Kortix transcript format, `kortix.transcript.v1`. `classifyTurn(message).parts` gives each part a `kind` you can switch on: `text`, `reasoning`, `tool`, `file` and more.
</Step>
</Steps>

The same calls work on both session harnesses, OpenCode and pi. See [Harnesses](/docs/#agents).

### A complete script

This file lists your projects, creates a session, waits for one turn, and prints the transcript.

```ts title="first-session.ts"
import { classifyTurn, createKortix, narrowChatEvent } from '@kortix/sdk';

const kortix = createKortix({
  backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1',
  getToken: async () => process.env.KORTIX_API_KEY ?? null,
});

async function main() {
  // 1. Pick a project, or create one.
  const projects = await kortix.projects.list();
  const project = projects[0] ?? (await kortix.projects.provision({ name: 'my-project' }));

  // 2. Create a session. The sandbox starts to provision now.
  const { session_id } = await kortix.project(project.project_id).sessions.create({
    name: 'sdk first session',
  });
  const session = kortix.session(project.project_id, session_id);

  // 3. Wait for the runtime. runtimeSessionId names the root conversation.
  const { runtimeSessionId } = await session.ensureReady();

  // 4. Open the stream before you send, so no event is lost.
  let sent = false;
  let finish!: () => void;
  const turnDone = new Promise<void>((resolve) => (finish = resolve));
  const stream = await session.stream({
    onEvent: (raw) => {
      const event = narrowChatEvent(raw);
      if (!event || !sent) return;
      if (event.type === 'session.error' && (!event.sessionID || event.sessionID === runtimeSessionId)) {
        console.error('session error:', event.error);
        finish();
      }
      if (event.type === 'session.idle' && event.sessionID === runtimeSessionId) finish();
    },
  });

  // 5. Send the prompt and wait for the turn to end.
  sent = true;
  await session.send('List the files in this repository.');
  await turnDone;
  stream.close();

  // 6. Print the transcript.
  const { messages } = await session.messages();
  for (const message of messages) {
    for (const part of classifyTurn(message).parts) {
      if (part.kind === 'text' && !part.synthetic) console.log(`[${message.info.role}] ${part.text}`);
    }
  }
  console.log(`session: ${session_id}`);
}

main().catch((error) => {
  console.error(error);
  process.exit(1);
});
```

Run it with `tsx` or Bun:

<CodeGroup>
```bash title="Node"
KORTIX_API_KEY=kortix_pat_... npx tsx first-session.ts
```

```bash title="Bun"
KORTIX_API_KEY=kortix_pat_... bun first-session.ts
```
</CodeGroup>

Each run creates a new session, so `ensureReady()` waits while its sandbox boots. The session also appears in the web app, with the same transcript.

- `narrowChatEvent` reduces the raw runtime events to the set a chat needs: message and part updates, session status, idle and error, questions, permissions and todos.
- `send(text, { model, agent })` overrides the model (`{ providerID, modelID }`) or the agent for one prompt. Model ids come from `kortix.projects.modelPicker(projectId)`. Agent names come from `kortix.projects.detail(projectId)`, in `config.agents`.
- To print text while it streams, to answer questions and permission requests, or to handle errors, see [Sessions from code](/docs/#sessions-from-code).

### Versions and stability

- `@kortix/sdk` publishes to npm with each Kortix platform release and carries the release's version number.
- Exported names, including types, are the public contract. A renamed export keeps a `@deprecated` alias that works until the next major version.
- The deprecated subpaths resolve until the next major version.
- `@kortix/sdk/internal/*` serves the Kortix web app and is outside semver. It can change in any release.
- Changes per release: the [CHANGELOG](https://github.com/kortix-ai/suna/blob/dev/packages/sdk/CHANGELOG.md).
- Runnable examples, from one `projects.list()` call to a multi-tenant server: [packages/sdk/examples](https://github.com/kortix-ai/suna/tree/dev/packages/sdk/examples) on GitHub. The npm package does not include them.

## SDK authentication

Every SDK request carries one bearer credential. `createKortix` reads it from
your `getToken` function and sends it as `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. This
page is for developers who call Kortix from a script, a backend, or an app
that people sign in to.

```ts
import { createKortix } from '@kortix/sdk';

const kortix = createKortix({
  backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1',
  getToken: async () => process.env.KORTIX_API_KEY ?? null,
});

await kortix.projects.list();
```

On a self-hosted instance, set `backendUrl` to `<your API origin>/v1`.

### Choose a credential

Pick the row that matches the caller. A credential holds the roles of the
principal it acts as, and nothing more.

| Caller | Credential | Acts as | Get it |
|---|---|---|---|
| A script, a CI job, your own backend | Personal access key, `kortix_pat_` | You, with your roles. Optionally one project only. | **Settings → Personal access keys**, or `kortix tokens new` |
| Unattended automation that must not act as a person | Service account key, `kortix_sa_` | The service account. It has no access until you assign it a role. | **Account settings → API keys**, or `kortix tokens service-accounts new` |
| Your product, where people sign in with their Kortix email, password, magic link, social login or SSO | The person's access token from [headless sign-in](#headless-sign-in) | The person who signed in | `kortix.auth.*` + `createKortixSession` |
| Your own web app with a "Sign in with Kortix" button | OAuth access token, `kortix_oat_` (1 hour; refresh token `kortix_ort_`, 30 days) | The person who approved your app | [`createKortixAuth`](#sign-in-with-kortix) |
| Code inside a Kortix App | App viewer token. The App's viewer scope must be `api`. | The person viewing the App, capped by their role | `kortix apps access <app> --viewer api`, then `kortixAppViewerToken()`. See [Apps](/docs/#know-who-is-looking). |
| Code inside a session sandbox | `KORTIX_TOKEN` | The session's agent (its service account), limited to that project and session. Its permissions are the agent's `kortix_permissions` capped by its ceiling role. See [What an agent may do](/docs/#agents). | Kortix sets it, with `KORTIX_API_URL` as the `backendUrl`. See [Sandboxes & previews](/docs/#computers). |

Creation, expiry, project binding and key rules are in
[API keys and tokens](/docs/#security).
A web app that already signs people in through the instance's Supabase passes
that JWT:
`getToken: async () => (await supabase.auth.getSession()).data.session?.access_token ?? null`.
New code uses [headless sign-in](#headless-sign-in) instead. A valid
credential without the needed permission gets `403`. Grant the role in
[Accounts & access](/docs/#teams).

### getToken and 401 recovery

The SDK calls `getToken` before every request. It caches nothing, so your code
owns token storage and refresh.

- `getToken` returns `null`: the SDK sends no request and throws `AuthError`
  with `code: 'NO_SESSION'`.
- The API answers `401`: the SDK calls `getToken.invalidate(rejectedToken)`
  when you attached one, calls `getToken` again, and replays the request once
  with the new token. A second `401` goes to the caller.
- Parallel `401`s on the same token share one `invalidate` call and one
  `getToken` call. A host that rotates a refresh token per call is not raced
  by its own requests.
- The SDK does not replay when the new token equals the rejected one, when the
  request body is a `ReadableStream`, or when the caller set its own
  `Authorization` header.

A `getToken` that caches must attach `invalidate`. Without it, the second
`getToken` call returns the same dead token and the SDK does not replay.
`session.getToken` from `createKortixSession` has no `invalidate`. A `401` on a
token that has not expired goes to the caller: handle it with `isAuthFailure`.

```ts
let cached: string | null = null;

const getToken = Object.assign(
  async () => (cached ??= await fetchTokenFromMyServer()),
  { invalidate: (rejected: string) => { if (cached === rejected) cached = null; } },
);

const kortix = createKortix({ backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1', getToken });
```

To branch on an auth failure, use `isAuthFailure(error)`. It is `true` for
`AuthError` and for any error with `status` `401` or `403`. It reads the
status, never the message text.

```ts
import { isAuthFailure } from '@kortix/sdk';

try {
  await kortix.projects.list();
} catch (error) {
  if (isAuthFailure(error)) redirectToSignIn();
  else throw error;
}
```

Set `clientVersion` to your app's name and version, for example
`clientVersion: 'my-app/1.4.0'`. The SDK sends it as `X-Kortix-Client-Version`.
Kortix writes it to request logs only. It grants nothing and never reaches the
audit log, which records the credential `getToken` returned.

Set `credentialHeader` when a backend proxy reads the credential from a custom
header, for example `credentialHeader: 'x-app-reader-token'`. The SDK sends the
raw token from `getToken` in that header and sends no `Authorization`. The `401`
replay still calls `getToken` again. A header of the same name that a call sets
itself wins.

### Headless sign-in

`kortix.auth.*` signs people up and in through the Kortix API at `/v1/auth/*`.
It needs no Supabase URL or key and works the same on Kortix Cloud and on a
self-hosted instance. Use it for a CLI, a native app, or your own sign-in
screen.

Create one session store and one client. The store keeps the tokens,
refreshes them 60 s before they expire, and feeds `getToken`.

```ts
import { createKortix, createKortixSession } from '@kortix/sdk';

const session = createKortixSession({
  storage: {                                   // optional: anything with get/set/remove
    get: () => localStorage.getItem('kortix'),
    set: (value) => localStorage.setItem('kortix', value),
    remove: () => localStorage.removeItem('kortix'),
  },
});

const kortix = createKortix({
  backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1',
  getToken: session.getToken,
});

const { session: tokens, user } = await kortix.auth.signInWithPassword({ email, password });
await session.set(tokens, user);

await kortix.projects.list();                  // runs as that person
```

Create the store with `createKortixSession` directly. Do not build a second
`createKortix` client only to reach `kortix.auth.session`: every
`createKortix` call shares one process-wide config, and the SDK warns when a
second call changes `getToken`.

#### Sign-in flows

| Flow | Calls |
|---|---|
| Sign up | `auth.signUp({ email, password, data?, redirect_to? })` returns `{ user, session, requires_email_confirmation }`. When `requires_email_confirmation` is `true`, `session` is `null` until the person confirms with the emailed link or with `verifyOtp({ type: 'signup' })`. |
| Password | `auth.signInWithPassword({ email, password })` |
| Magic link or code | `auth.sendMagicLink({ email, create_user?, redirect_to?, data? })`, then `auth.verifyOtp({ email, token, type: 'magiclink' })` with the code from the email |
| Social login (PKCE) | `auth.signInWithProvider({ provider, redirect_to, scopes? })` returns `{ url, code_verifier }`. Keep `code_verifier`, send the browser to `url`, then call `auth.exchangeCode({ code, code_verifier })` with the `code` from the redirect. `provider` is one the instance has configured, for example `google` or `github`. |
| Enterprise SSO | `auth.signInWithSso({ domain, redirect_to? })` returns `{ url }` to send the browser to. A `404` means the domain has no identity provider. See [Single sign-on](/docs/#security). |
| Password reset | `auth.resetPassword({ email, redirect_to? })`, then `auth.verifyOtp({ email, token, type: 'recovery' })`, then `auth.updatePassword({ password }, session.access_token)` with the session `verifyOtp` returned. The new password is 8 to 256 characters. |

Every flow that returns a session returns `{ session, user }`. Pass both to
`session.set(...)`. `redirect_to` must be an absolute `http` or `https` URL that
the instance allows.

#### The signed-in person

These calls take a token as an argument, so they work before `getToken` is
wired. `user`, `updateUserMetadata`, `updatePassword` and `mfa.*` need the
person's own access token. They refuse any other credential, such as a
personal access key, with `401 invalid_token`.

| Call | Does |
|---|---|
| `auth.user(accessToken)` | Returns `{ user }` |
| `auth.updateUserMetadata({ data }, accessToken)` | Writes profile metadata: display name, avatar, language. A password or email change is refused. |
| `auth.updatePassword({ password }, accessToken)` | Sets a new password |
| `auth.mfa.enroll({ factor_type, friendly_name?, phone?, issuer? }, accessToken)` | Enrolls a `totp` or `phone` factor |
| `auth.mfa.challenge(factorId, { channel? }, accessToken)` | Starts a challenge |
| `auth.mfa.verify(factorId, { challenge_id, code }, accessToken)` | Verifies the code. Returns the new token pair at the second assurance level at the top level (`access_token`, `refresh_token`, `expires_at`), not as `{ session, user }`. Store it with `session.set(result as AuthSession)`. |
| `auth.mfa.unenroll(factorId, accessToken)` | Removes a factor |
| `auth.refresh({ refresh_token })` | Rotates the token pair. `createKortixSession` calls it for you. |
| `auth.signOut(accessToken, { scope? })` | Revokes the session at Kortix. `scope`: `global` (default, every device), `local` (this session), `others` (every other session). |

To sign out, revoke the session, then clear the store:

```ts
const token = await session.getToken();
if (token) await kortix.auth.signOut(token);
await session.clear();
```

#### The session store

`kortix.auth.session(options?)` is the same function as
`createKortixSession(options?)`. Each call creates a new store, so create one
and keep it.

| Option | Default | Does |
|---|---|---|
| `storage` | none, memory only | `{ get, set, remove }`, sync or async: `localStorage`, a file, a cookie jar, a database row |
| `onChange` | none | Called after every sign-in, refresh and sign-out |
| `refreshSkewSeconds` | `60` | Refresh this many seconds before expiry |
| `request` | the client's `backendUrl` | `{ backendUrl?, fetch?, signal? }` for refresh calls |

| Method | Does |
|---|---|
| `getToken()` | A valid access token, refreshed first when needed. `null` when signed out. |
| `set(session, user?)` | Stores a session a `kortix.auth.*` call returned |
| `current()`, `user()` | The stored session and user. No refresh. |
| `refresh()` | Rotates now. Concurrent callers share one refresh. |
| `subscribe(listener)` | Calls `listener` on every change. Returns an unsubscribe function. |
| `load()` | Reads storage now instead of on first use |
| `clear()` | Forgets the session locally. Call `auth.signOut` first to revoke it. |

A refresh that Kortix rejects with a `4xx` signs the person out: the store
clears itself and `getToken()` returns `null`. A network failure keeps the
stored session for the next call.

#### Errors and limits

Every `kortix.auth.*` call throws `HeadlessAuthError` with `code`, `message` and
`status`.

| `code` | `status` | Cause |
|---|---|---|
| `sso_required` | `403` | The email's domain must sign in with SSO. Sign-up, password and magic link refuse it. Use `signInWithSso`. |
| `signup_closed` | `403` | The instance does not accept sign-ups from this address |
| `over_request_rate_limit` | `429` | Too many attempts. Wait for the `retry-after` header. |
| `invalid_token` | `401` | A call that needs the person's access token got another credential |
| `not_configured` | `0` | No `backendUrl`: create the client first |

All public `/v1/auth/*` routes share one limit: 30 attempts per minute per
client IP, counted on each API replica. Other codes come unchanged from the identity service.

### Sign in with Kortix

Sign in with Kortix makes Kortix the identity provider for a web app you run.
People sign in with their Kortix account. Your server knows who they are, and
your app calls Kortix as that person, with that person's roles. It is OAuth 2.1
with PKCE, and the browser never holds a Kortix token.

<Steps>
  <Step title="Register your app">
    Open **Account settings → API keys → OAuth apps** and select **Register app**, or
    register it in code. Registering needs `token.create` on the account.

    ```ts
    const app = await kortix.iam.oauthClients.create(accountId, {
      name: 'My dashboard',
      client_type: 'confidential',   // 'public' for a browser or native app: PKCE, no secret
      redirect_uris: ['https://dashboard.example.com/api/kortix/auth/callback'],
      scopes: ['profile', 'email', 'kortix'],
    });
    // app.client_id; app.client_secret is shown once
    ```

    Kortix compares redirect URIs byte for byte. They must use `https`, except
    on a loopback host (`localhost`, `*.localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]`). `kortix.iam.oauthClients` also has `list`, `get`, `update`,
    `rotateSecret` and `remove`. Field details are in
    [OAuth apps and connected apps](/docs/#security).
  </Step>
  <Step title="Create the auth kit on your server">
    ```ts
    // lib/kortix-auth.ts (server only)
    import { createKortixAuth } from '@kortix/sdk/server';

    export const auth = createKortixAuth({
      backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1',
      clientId: process.env.KORTIX_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID!,
      clientSecret: process.env.KORTIX_OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET,   // omit for a public client
      redirectUri: 'https://dashboard.example.com/api/kortix/auth/callback',
      cookieSecret: process.env.KORTIX_AUTH_COOKIE_SECRET!,   // at least 32 characters
    });
    ```
  </Step>
  <Step title="Mount one catch-all route">
    ```ts
    // app/api/kortix/auth/[...kortix]/route.ts (Next.js App Router)
    import { auth } from '@/lib/kortix-auth';

    const handle = (request: Request) => auth.handler(request);
    export { handle as GET, handle as POST };
    ```

    Any framework works: pass a Web `Request` to `auth.handler` and return its
    `Response`.
  </Step>
  <Step title="Gate pages and call Kortix as the person">
    ```ts
    import { auth } from '@/lib/kortix-auth';

    export async function GET(request: Request) {
      const gate = await auth.requireViewer(request);
      if (gate.response) return gate.response;          // 302 to /refresh or /signin

      const kortix = await auth.kortix(request);        // acts as the signed-in person
      return Response.json(await kortix.projects.list());
    }
    ```
  </Step>
  <Step title="Call Kortix from the browser">
    ```tsx
    import { createKortix } from '@kortix/sdk';
    import { SignInWithKortix, useKortixViewer } from '@kortix/sdk/react';

    const kortix = createKortix({
      backendUrl: window.location.origin + '/api/kortix/auth/proxy',
      getToken: async () => 'kortix-session',
    });

    function Header() {
      const { status, viewer } = useKortixViewer();
      if (status === 'signed-in') return <span>{viewer.email}</span>;
      return <SignInWithKortix className="button" />;
    }
    ```

    The browser sends the placeholder bearer `kortix-session`. The `/proxy`
    route replaces it with the person's real token on your server. Every SDK
    call works through it, including [`useSession`](/docs/#react).
  </Step>
</Steps>

<Callout type="warning">
  Never import `@kortix/sdk/server` or a module that imports it, such as
  `lib/kortix-auth.ts`, into browser code. It pulls in `node:async_hooks`, and
  `createKortixAuth` throws without `cookieSecret`. Write the browser config
  literally, as in step 5.
</Callout>

#### Options

| Option | Default | Does |
|---|---|---|
| `backendUrl` | required | Kortix API base, including `/v1` |
| `clientId` | required | The registered client id |
| `clientSecret` | none | The client secret. Omit it for a public client. |
| `redirectUri` | required | Absolute callback URL, identical to a registered one |
| `cookieSecret` | required | At least 32 characters. Encrypts the cookies with AES-256-GCM. |
| `scopes` | `['profile', 'email', 'kortix']` | Scopes to request |
| `basePath` | the `redirectUri` path without `/callback` | Where `handler` is mounted |
| `sessionTtlSeconds` | `2592000` (30 days) | How long a session lives without a new sign-in |
| `cookieSameSite` | `'lax'` | `'none'` only for an app that signs in while embedded in another origin. It needs `https`; over `http` the cookie stays `Lax`. |
| `fetch` | global `fetch` | Custom fetch for tests or edge runtimes |

| Scope | Grants your app |
|---|---|
| `profile` | The person's id, email and account memberships |
| `email` | The email address |
| `kortix` | Acting as the person on the whole Kortix API. Without it the token reaches only the identity routes. |

#### Routes the handler serves

All paths are under `basePath`, `/api/kortix/auth` in the example.

| Path | Does |
|---|---|
| `/signin?return_to=/path` | Starts sign-in with PKCE and a 10-minute transaction cookie, then redirects to Kortix |
| `/callback` | Exchanges the code, sets the encrypted `HttpOnly` session cookie, redirects to `return_to` |
| `/refresh?return_to=/path` | Rotates the token pair and redirects. `requireViewer` sends people here. |
| `/signout?return_to=/path` | Revokes the refresh token at Kortix and clears the cookie |
| `/me` | The person as JSON, or `401`. Refreshes an expired access token. |
| `/proxy/*` | Forwards the request to the Kortix API as the person |

`return_to` is always reduced to a same-origin path. When the person declines
consent, the callback redirects to `return_to` with `?kortix_auth_error=<code>`.
A missing or expired transaction answers `400` with `error: 'state_mismatch'`.
A failed code exchange answers with `error: 'token_exchange_failed'`.

#### Server and browser API

| From | API | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| `@kortix/sdk/server` | `auth.viewer(request)` | `{ userId, email, accounts, scopes, token, expiresAt }` or `null`. Read-only: it never rotates the refresh token. |
| `@kortix/sdk/server` | `auth.requireViewer(request)` | `{ viewer }`, or `{ response }` with a redirect to `/refresh` or `/signin` |
| `@kortix/sdk/server` | `auth.kortix(request)` | A request-scoped client that acts as the person. Throws `KortixAuthError` `unauthenticated` when signed out. |
| `@kortix/sdk/server` | `auth.signInUrl(returnTo?)`, `auth.signOutUrl(returnTo?)` | Link targets |
| `@kortix/sdk/react` | `useKortixViewer({ basePath? })` | `{ status, viewer, reload }`. `status` is `loading`, `signed-in`, `signed-out` or `error`. |
| `@kortix/sdk/react` | `<SignInWithKortix basePath? returnTo? />` | A link that starts sign-in and returns to the current page |
| `@kortix/sdk/react` | `kortixSignOutHref(basePath?, returnTo?)` | The sign-out link target |

Use `requireViewer` to gate a page. A page then never renders signed out for a
person whose refresh token is still valid. `createKortixAuth` throws
`KortixAuthError` with code `invalid_options` when `backendUrl` or `clientId`
is empty, `redirectUri` is not an absolute `http` or `https` URL, or
`cookieSecret` is shorter than 32 characters.

`useKortixViewer`, `<SignInWithKortix>` and `kortixSignOutHref` default
`basePath` to `/api/kortix/auth`. Pass `basePath` when you mount `handler`
elsewhere.

#### What the person sees

The first sign-in shows a Kortix consent screen with your app's name and
scopes. Kortix remembers the approval per person and app, so later sign-ins
return straight to your app. People revoke an app in code with
`kortix.accounts.connectedApps.list()` and `.revoke(clientId)`, or on the web
and in the CLI as described in
[Connected apps](/docs/#security). After a revoke the
app's tokens stop working and the app must ask again.

#### Discovery for other OAuth clients

The SDK derives every endpoint from `backendUrl`. A generic OAuth client reads
`GET /.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` on the API origin, also served at
`/v1/oauth/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server`. Open dynamic client
registration is at `POST /v1/oauth/register`. Kortix deletes a self-registered
client after 7 days when it has no approval and no token.

### Servers that act for many users

`createKortix` stores its config in one process-wide slot. That is correct for
a browser tab, a CLI or a single-tenant job. On a server that handles requests
for different people at the same time, the last `createKortix` call wins for
every request in flight. Use `@kortix/sdk/server` instead. It isolates each
request's config in Node's `AsyncLocalStorage` and runs in Node and Bun.

**One client per request.** `createScopedKortix` has the same shape as
`createKortix` and never touches the global config.

```ts
import { createScopedKortix } from '@kortix/sdk/server';

export async function GET(request: Request) {
  const kortix = createScopedKortix({
    backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1',
    getToken: async () => tokenFor(request),
  });
  return Response.json(await kortix.projects.list());
}
```

A scoped client's top-level `kortix.runtime()` throws, because it would resolve
another request's sandbox. Reach a session's runtime through its handle:
`const s = kortix.session(projectId, sessionId); await s.ensureReady(); s.runtime`.

**One scope per request.** `runWithKortix(config, fn)` sets the config for
everything `fn` awaits, including calls on a client made with `createKortix`.

```ts
import { runWithKortix } from '@kortix/sdk/server';

app.use(async (req, res, next) => {
  await runWithKortix(
    { backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1', getToken: async () => tokenFor(req) },
    async () => next(),
  );
});
```

**A proxy for your browser code.** `forwardKortixRequest` forwards one request
from your own route to the Kortix API with a token your server holds.

```ts
import { forwardKortixRequest } from '@kortix/sdk/server';

export async function handle(request: Request) {
  const url = new URL(request.url);
  const rest = url.pathname.replace(/^\/api\/kortix\//, '');
  return forwardKortixRequest({
    request,
    upstreamUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1/' + rest + url.search,
    token: await tokenFor(request),
  });
}
```

It removes the browser's `cookie` and `host` headers, sets the bearer, keeps
streamed response bodies, and removes `set-cookie` from the answer. When Kortix
is unreachable it answers `502` with `{ "error": "Upstream request failed" }`.
Point the browser client at the route with any non-null placeholder token:
`createKortix({ backendUrl: '/api/kortix', getToken: async () => 'proxy' })`.

To start sessions from such a server with session secrets and idempotency, see
[Start sessions from your server](/docs/#start-sessions-from-your-server).

## Sessions from code

A session is one agent run in its own sandbox, on its own git branch. This section drives a session from TypeScript with `@kortix/sdk`, from a script or from a server that starts sessions for its own customers. For the concept, see [Sessions](/docs/#computers); to set up the client, see [SDK](/docs/#sdk).

The shortest complete run:

```ts
import { createKortix } from '@kortix/sdk';

const kortix = createKortix({
  backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1',
  getToken: async () => process.env.KORTIX_API_KEY!,
});

const created = await kortix.project(projectId).sessions.create({ agent_name: 'my-agent' });
const s = kortix.session(projectId, created.session_id);

const { runtimeSessionId } = await s.ensureReady();
let finish!: () => void;
const turnDone = new Promise<void>((resolve) => (finish = resolve));

const stream = await s.stream({
  onEvent: (event) => {
    if (event.type === 'session.idle' && event.properties.sessionID === runtimeSessionId) finish();
  },
});

await s.send('List the open TODO comments in this repository.');
await turnDone;
stream.close();

const { messages } = await s.messages({ limit: 1 }); // the agent's last message: { info, parts }
```

### The session handle

```ts
const s = kortix.session(projectId, sessionId);
// the same handle: kortix.project(projectId).session(sessionId)
```

- A handle resolves only its own sandbox. It never falls back to the runtime of another session, so two handles in one process never cross.
- The `session_id` is also the name of the session's git branch.
- `ensureReady()` returns `sandboxId`. That is the sandbox provider's id, a different value from `session_id`.
- Creating a handle sends no request.

### Create a session

```ts
const created = await kortix.project(projectId).sessions.create({
  agent_name: 'my-agent',
  model: 'glm-5.3-flash',
  initial_prompt: 'Triage the open issues labeled bug.',
  labels: ['triage'],
  metadata: { app_ticket: 'T-142' },
});
created.session_id;
```

Every field is optional. With no input, the session runs the project's default agent and model and waits for a prompt. A create starts provisioning the sandbox at once. If the session needs a new secret value or a new connector credential, write it before the create.

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `initial_prompt` | The first prompt. The server delivers it when the runtime is up, so you need no `send()`. |
| `agent_name` | The agent to run. Default: the project's default agent. |
| `model` | The model id (see [Models](/docs/#models)). A model the account cannot use returns `400`. |
| `name` | The session title. Without it, the server makes a title from the first prompt. |
| `labels` | Up to 20 labels, each 1 to 64 characters after trimming. Duplicates drop. Matching is exact and case-sensitive. |
| `metadata` | A JSON object of your own keys, at most 16,384 characters of JSON per write. Server-managed keys, such as `source`, `trigger_kind` and `trigger_slug`, return `400`. Prefix your keys, for example `app_`. |
| `session_id` | A v4 UUID you generate, so you know the id before the create returns. A second create with the same id returns `409`. |
| `base_ref` | The branch or ref the session branch starts from. Default: the project's default branch. |
| `sandbox_slug` | The sandbox to boot, by its slug in `kortix.yaml`. Default: the agent's sandbox, then the project default, then `default`. |
| `provider` | The sandbox provider: `platinum`, or the legacy fallback `daytona`. Default: the project's pinned provider, else the platform's choice. |
| `runtime_context` | Non-secret values for the agent. See [Start sessions from your server](#start-sessions-from-your-server). |
| `connector_bindings` | Pins one connector account per connector alias: `{ gmail: { connection_id } }`. |
| `inherit_unbound` | With `connector_bindings` set, a connector you did not bind gets no account. `true` lets it use the project default. Default `false`. |
| `secrets` | Narrows which project secrets the sandbox receives, by identifier. A personal access key or service account key only. |
| `provider_secret_pools` | HTTP body only. Selects up to 10 account secret ids per model provider, for up to 20 providers. Needs the `pooled_provider_secrets` flag and the LLM gateway. |
| `space_id` | Experimental. The channel or DM to file the session in, with the `project_channels` flag on. See [Channels and DMs](#channels-and-dms-experimental). Ignored while the flag is off, but a value that is not a UUID returns `400`. |

`opencode_model` is the deprecated name of `model`; `model` wins when you send both. `require_connectors` is accepted and ignored.

Update a session later:

```ts
await s.update({ name: 'Bug triage', labels: ['triage', 'done'], metadata: { app_priority: null } });
const { items } = await kortix.project(projectId).sessions.listPage({ labels: ['triage', 'done'] });
```

`labels` replaces the list. `metadata` merges keys, and a `null` value removes a key. `listPage({ labels })` returns only sessions that carry every label. For a pre-booted session that is ready before the first prompt, see [Warm sessions](/docs/#computers).

### Channels and DMs (experimental)

<Callout type="warning">
Experimental. Every call in this section needs the project's `project_channels` flag, which is off by default. While it is off, every `spaces` route returns `403` `feature_disabled`, and session create and list ignore `space_id` (a create whose `space_id` is not a UUID still returns `400`).
</Callout>

A space is a channel of the project, or the caller's direct message (DM) with one agent. A thread in a space is an ordinary session whose `space_id` names the space. The thread's first prompt is its top message.

```ts
const p = kortix.project(projectId);

const spaces = await p.spaces.list(); // #general first, then channels oldest first, then the caller's DMs
const launch = await p.spaces.create({ name: 'Launch Week', description: 'Plans and status' }); // slug "launch-week"
await p.spaces.update(launch.space_id, { description: null }); // name, description (null clears), archived
const dm = await p.spaces.dm('kortix'); // the caller's DM with an agent, created on the first call

const thread = await p.sessions.create({ space_id: launch.space_id, initial_prompt: 'Draft the launch checklist.' });
const threads = await p.sessions.list({ spaceId: launch.space_id, parent: 'root' });
threads[0].first_prompt; // the thread's top message
threads[0].reply_count; // the messages after it; set on a space list only
```

| Call | Behavior |
| --- | --- |
| `spaces.list()` | The live channels, then the caller's DMs, as `ProjectSpace[]`. The first call creates `#general` (`is_default: true`). It also files every session that is in no space into `#general`. |
| `spaces.create({ name, description? })` | A channel. `name` is 1 to 80 characters, `description` at most 500. The slug (`a-z0-9-`) is unique in the project, archived channels included: a taken slug rejects with `ApiError` `409` `slug_taken`, `data.space_id` and `data.archived` name the channel that holds it, and `data.can_edit` says whether the caller may unarchive it. A name with no letter or digit returns `400` `invalid_name`. |
| `spaces.update(spaceId, { name?, description?, archived? })` | Rename, describe, archive (`true`) or unarchive (`false`) a channel. Only the channel's creator or a project manager (`project.members.manage`) may edit it: every listed space carries `can_edit`, and anyone else gets `403` `forbidden`. `#general` cannot be archived (`400` `default_channel`). A DM cannot be edited (`400` `dm_not_editable`). |
| `ProjectSpace.visibility` | `public` (every project member sees the channel) or `private` (only its members see the channel, its threads, links, Apps, members and notifications; anyone else gets `404` `space_not_found` or `404` on the thread, never `403`). `#general` and DMs are always `public`. A project manager outside a private channel lists, adds (never themself: `403` `manager_self_add`) and removes its members, renames and archives it, and reads nothing else. A manager who makes a channel private becomes a member of it. `spaces.create({ visibility: 'private' })` and `spaces.update(spaceId, { visibility: 'private' })` (public to private only; authors of its threads and links become members) answer `409` `private_channels_unavailable` until private channels ship. Absent on older APIs: read it as `public`. |
| `spaces.dm(agentName)` | The caller's DM with the agent. Only the caller sees it. An agent the project does not declare returns `404` `agent_not_found`; an agent the caller may not run returns `403`. |
| `spaces.apps(spaceId)` | The channel's Apps as `App[]`: the live Apps with a deployment made from one of its threads, only those the caller may open (the same filter as `apps.list()`). An App never deployed from a thread is in no channel. A caller without the App read permission gets `403`. |
| `spaces.links.list(spaceId)`, `links.add(spaceId, { url, title? })`, `links.pin(spaceId, linkId, pinned)`, `links.remove(spaceId, linkId)` | A channel's links, the most recently shared first, as `ProjectSpaceLink[]`. A link is added with `add` (`source: 'manual'`, `created_by` the caller) or collected from a person's message in a thread of the channel (`source: 'message'`; DMs, private threads and agent replies are not collected). Both start unpinned. Adding an address the channel holds keeps that row and its pin, takes the new title and moves it to the top. One row per address: a second share moves `last_shared_at` and `session_id` to that thread. `url` is http(s) only and at most 2,048 characters; `title` at most 200; anything else returns `400`. The stored `url` is its parsed form. A channel holds at most 200 added links and pins at most 200: one more returns `409` `link_limit`. It keeps the 500 most recently shared unpinned collected links; an added link is never pruned. Every project member may list, add, pin and unpin. `pin` returns the link. Unpinning only clears the pin: the link stays listed until someone removes it. `can_delete` is true for the link's creator and for a project manager; anyone else gets `403` `forbidden` on remove. Agents read the 20 most recently pinned links in a thread's `/drives/README.md`. Each link carries `display_title` (the title a member gave, else the page's fetched title, else the decoded path, else the host), `site_name` and `icon_url`. The server fetches the title and icon in the background, so both are `null` until then. `icon_url` is a remote URL: load it only through an image proxy. |
| `spaces.members.list(spaceId)`, `members.add(spaceId, userIds)`, `members.remove(spaceId, userId)`, `members.join(spaceId)`, `members.leave(spaceId)` | Who is in a channel, as `ProjectSpaceMemberList` (`{ members, implicit, can_add }`). In a public channel membership gates no read and no post, and every viewer reads the same list. A private channel is reachable only by its members. Each `ProjectSpaceMember` has `name` (the display name, else the email's local part, never the email), `avatar_url`, `is_creator`, `is_viewer`, `added_at` and `can_remove`. `#general` lists every project member (`implicit: true`); adding, joining or leaving it returns `400` `default_channel`. A channel create makes the creator a member, plus its `member_ids`. `add` takes up to 100 ids in one request: a member of the channel, its creator or a project manager may add (else `403` `forbidden`), and every id must be a project member (else `400` `not_project_member`, `user_ids` names them, nobody is added). A project manager removes anyone, the creator anyone but themself, a member only themself (`leave`); the creator is removed only by a manager (`403` `creator_not_removable`). `join` is open to every project member and idempotent. `ProjectSpace.is_member` says whether the caller is in the channel. |
