| `spaces.privateChannelsNotIn()` | Project managers only (else `403` `forbidden`): the private channels the caller is not in, archived ones included, as `PrivateChannelSummary[]` (`space_id`, `name`, `slug`, `member_count`, `created_by`, `created_by_name`, `created_at`, `archived_at`). No threads, files, memory or links. A channel its last member left has `member_count: 0`; a manager adds people to it with `members.add`. React: `usePrivateChannelsNotIn(projectId, { enabled })`. |
| `spaces.generalNotice()` | `#general`'s "turned on again" line as `ProjectSpaceGeneralNotice` (`{ kind: 'reenabled', at, count }`: `count` sessions started while Channels was off went into `#general` at `at`), or `null`. It is present for 7 days after the flag is turned on again with `#general` already there. It reads `GET /spaces`. |
| `spaces.onboarding.get()` | The caller's onboarding state as `ChannelsOnboarding`: `intro_seen` and `get_started: { visible, create_channel, remember, open_dm }`. A "Get started" row is done when its mark is present or its fact is true: the caller created a channel, saved memory in `#general`, or owns a DM. `visible` is false once the block is dismissed or all three rows are done. |
| `spaces.onboarding.mark(mark)` | Records one mark for the caller: `channels_intro`, `get_started.dismissed`, `get_started.channel`, `get_started.remember` or `get_started.dm`. It is idempotent. Another value returns `400`. Turning the flag on again marks `channels_intro` for every member. |
| `sessions.create({ space_id })` | A create with a channel `space_id` is project-visible. With no `agent_name`, it runs the channel's first agent (its default), else the project default. Whoever may post in a channel (every project member in `#general` and a public channel, a member in a private one) runs the channel's agents there, and the project default when the channel lists none, without an agent grant; replies (`POST .../prompts`) follow the same rule. Any other agent, and every session outside a channel, still needs the grant (`403` `agent_not_accessible` or `no_agent_access`). A rewind of an OpenCode session through the proxy is refused with `403` `not_message_author` unless the caller sent that message and nobody else sent one after it. A create in a DM is private and runs the DM's agent: an `agent_name` in the same call is overridden. Another member's DM, an archived channel and an unknown id return `404` `space_not_found`. |
| `sessions.create()` without `space_id` | Files the session into `#general`, private: only its creator sees it. A session that another session starts goes to its parent's space. |
| `sessions.create({ fork_from_session_id })` | The fork goes to its source's space and keeps its source's sharing: project-visible only when the source is, else private. A fork is never wider than its source. |
| `sessions.list({ spaceId })`, `listPage({ spaceId })` | One space's sessions that the caller may open, newest activity first. Each row carries `first_prompt` and `reply_count`. The cursor stays inside the space. A `spaceId` that is not a UUID returns `400`. Narrow it with `q` (matches the title, the first prompt, the agent and the person), `startedBy: 'me'`, `createdBy` (a user id: the threads that person started), `agent` (a slug) and `updatedAfter` (ISO 8601: activity at or after it). A `createdBy` that is not a UUID or an `updatedAfter` that is not a date returns `400`. |

A `visibility` that a server-side caller sets (a trigger, a chat integration) wins, except in a DM. From the SDK, change a thread's sharing after the create with `session(projectId, sessionId).setSharing(...)`.

Turning `project_channels` on files every session that is in no space into `#general`. Turning it off deletes nothing: the spaces and every `space_id` stay, and come back when the flag is on again. In React, use `useProjectSpaces`, `useProjectSpaceApps`, `useProjectSpaceLinks`, `useAddProjectSpaceLink`, `usePinProjectSpaceLink`, `useRemoveProjectSpaceLink`, `useProjectSpaceMembers`, `useAddProjectSpaceMembers`, `useRemoveProjectSpaceMember`, `useJoinProjectSpace`, `useLeaveProjectSpace`, `useProjectSpaceGeneralNotice`, `useChannelsOnboarding`, `useMarkChannelsOnboarding` and `useProjectSessions(projectId, { spaceId })` ([Hooks](/docs/#hooks)).

### Memory (experimental)

Experimental. Every call in this section needs the project's Files (the `drives` flag): while Files are off, every `memory` route returns `403` `feature_disabled`, and without Drive storage `503` `drive_storage_unavailable`. The Memory UI is a Channels surface (`project_channels`); these calls do not need that flag.

Memory is the Markdown files of the project's Drive, read and edited in place: nothing is copied. A layer is one folder. The Company layer (key `'company'`) is `/Company/Memory`; a channel's layer (key = the space id) is `/Company/Channels/<slug>/Memory`; a DM's layer (key = the space id, its owner only) is the owner's `/Users/<person>/Memory`, with the agent folder `Memory/<agent>` listed first. A page is one `.md` file. Its type comes from front-matter `type:`, else its first folder (`people/`, `decisions/`, `workstreams/`, `playbooks/`), else `Notes.md` is `note`, else `other`. Agents read and write the same files through their `/drives` mounts. Sources and "updated by" come from the file's front-matter (`sources:`, `updated_by:`, `updated_at:`).

```ts
const m = kortix.project(projectId).memory;
const layer = await m.layer(launch.space_id); // layer.folder: "/Company/Channels/launch/Memory"
const page = await m.pages.create(launch.space_id, { type: 'decisions', title: 'Launch date', body: '- Ships on Monday' });
await m.pages.update(page.page_id, { base_version: page.version_no, body: '- Ships on Tuesday' });
const { versions } = await m.pages.history(page.page_id);
// Restore a version: write its body back.
await m.pages.update(page.page_id, { base_version: (await m.pages.get(page.page_id)).version_no, body: versions[1].snapshot.body });
```

| Call | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `memory.layer(layerKey)` | The layer as `ProjectMemoryLayer`: `name`, `folder` (a Drive path), `agent_folder` (a DM's), `drive_id`, page `counts` per type, `other_files` (files that are not pages), `read_only` (an archived channel) and `can_write` (write access to the folder). Another member's DM and an unknown id return `404` `memory_not_found`. |
| `memory.pages.list(layerKey, { type? })` | One `ProjectMemoryPageSummary` per Markdown file (no body, with its Drive `path`), newest first, at most 500. |
| `memory.pages.create(layerKey, { type, title, summary?, body?, related?, agent_folder? })` | Writes `<folder>/<type>/<slug>.md` (`type: 'other'`: `<folder>/<slug>.md`) with front-matter `updated_by` and `updated_at`. In a DM it writes into the agent folder unless `agent_folder: false`. `body` at most 262,144 UTF-8 bytes (`413` `page_too_large`). An existing file returns `409` `title_taken` with `data.page_id`; an archived channel `409` `layer_read_only`; a secret `422` `secret_detected`. |
| `memory.notes.add(layerKey, { text, source_session_id? })` | Appends `- <text> — <name>, <date>` to the layer's `Notes.md` (a DM's: in the agent folder) and returns that page. |
| `memory.pages.get(pageId)` | The page as `ProjectMemoryPage`: `body` (the file without front-matter and without its `# ` title line), `sources`, `related`, `version_no` (the file mtime in ms), `can_edit`, `can_delete`. A file over 256 KiB returns `413` `page_too_large` with its `path`. |
| `memory.pages.update(pageId, { base_version, body })` | Replaces the body; front-matter and the title line are kept. A file changed since `base_version` returns `409` `version_conflict`. |
| `memory.pages.delete(pageId)` | Deletes the file for good and returns `{ ok, version_id }`. There is no trash. |
| `memory.pages.history(pageId)` | The page's versions from the drive's newest 100 Drive commits, newest first, at most 20, each with its `snapshot`: `{ versions }`. Older versions are in Files → Versions. To restore one, pass its `snapshot.body` to `memory.pages.update`. |
| `memory.search({ q, layer?, type?, limit? })` | Searches Company, every live channel and the caller's own Memory folder, or the one `layer`: case-insensitive text over title and body, title hits first. `q` is 2 to 200 characters (shorter returns `400` `invalid_input`); `limit` is 1 to 20 (default 20). |
| `memory.usage(sessionId)` | The thread's "Used memory": the files listed to its agent at the first turn, as `{ loaded_at, layers: [{ layer_key, scope, name, pages: [{ page_id, type, title }] }] }`. `loaded_at` is null before the first turn. Files deleted since are left out. A thread the caller cannot open returns `404` `session_not_found`. |

Every error is an `ApiError` whose `code` names it, and none reaches the host error handler. In React, use `useProjectMemoryLayer`, `useProjectMemoryPages`, `useProjectMemoryPage`, `useProjectMemoryHistory`, `useProjectMemorySearch`, `useSessionMemoryUsage` and the mutation hooks ([Hooks](/docs/#hooks)).

### Wait until ready

```ts
const { runtimeSessionId, runtimeUrl, sandboxId } = await s.ensureReady({ readyTimeoutMs: 300_000 });
```

`ensureReady()` provisions or resumes the sandbox and waits for its runtime.

- It asks the API to start the session in long polls of up to 30 s, with a 1 s pause, while the answer is `provisioning` or `starting` with `retriable: true`.
- The default deadline is 180 s. Pass `readyTimeoutMs` to wait longer.
- It throws an `ApiError` with `code: 'RUNTIME_UNAVAILABLE'` at the deadline, or at once on a terminal `failed` or `stopped` answer.
- Concurrent calls for one session share one start request. A second call on a ready handle returns at once.
- Pass `session`, the session's row from `sessions.get()` or `sessions.list()`, when you already have it. A `running` row that names its sandbox and `runtime_session_id` is used as is, without a start request. Any other row, or the row of another session, changes nothing.

```ts
const row = await kortix.projects.sessions.get(projectId, sessionId);
const { runtimeSessionId } = await s.ensureReady({ session: row });
```

Which methods ready the session for you:

| Behavior | Methods |
| --- | --- |
| Call `ensureReady()` themselves | `send`, `abort`, `rewind`, `restoreRewind`, `stream`, `messages`, `pending`, `answerPermission`, `answerQuestion`, `compact`, every `files.*` method |
| Throw `SessionNotReadyError` before a ready | `previewUrl`, `proxyUrl`, `sandboxPortUrl` |
| Read only the Kortix API, never the sandbox | `get`, `update`, `cost`, `scope`, `transcript`, `transcriptSync`, `log`, `messageAuthors`, `modelUsage`, `turn`, `attachments.read` |

`s.health()` never throws `SessionNotReadyError`. Before this handle has a runtime it returns `{ status: 0, ok: false }`.

A failed start is a cooldown, not a final verdict. A start after the cooldown tries again. The cooldown grows from 2 to 5 to 10 minutes. After 5 consecutive failures, `ensureReady()` throws `RUNTIME_UNAVAILABLE` at once. That verdict expires 30 minutes after the last failure. `s.restart()` clears it at once.

### Send prompts

```ts
await s.send('Refactor the auth module.');
await s.send('Write the tests.', { agent: 'tester' }); // this call only

s.setModel({ providerID, modelID }); // later send() calls on this handle
s.setAgent('reviewer');

await s.abort(); // end the running turn; the session stays
```

- `send()` puts the prompt in the session's durable prompt inbox, the same inbox the web app and the CLI use. It resolves when the prompt is stored, with `{ prompt_id, state, message_id }`. It does not wait for the turn. Read the reply from `stream()` or the transcript.
- `send()` throws an `ApiError` with `code: 'PROMPT_FAILED'` when the inbox row already failed.
- Model and agent precedence: the `send()` option, then `setModel()` / `setAgent()` on this handle, then the session's saved default.
- `setModel()` stays on the handle. To change the session's saved model, call `changeModel()`:

```ts
const { applied_live } = await s.changeModel('glm-5.3-flash');
```

The same model-id rule applies as for `model` at create. `changeModel()` restarts the runtime when the model changes on a running session, so a running turn ends. `applied_live` is `true` when a running session took the model now, and `false` when it applies at the next start. `push_failed: true` means the model is stored, but the running runtime still uses the old one. On a `stopped`, `failed` or `completed` session it returns `409` `SESSION_NOT_RUNNING`. Only the session owner or a project manager may call it. Anyone else gets `403`.

The Kortix web and mobile apps open a session on its saved model. A model set at create or with `changeModel()` is the model their next message uses, unless the person picks another model in that session. The same ranking is `sessionModelCandidates()` in the root barrel.

#### Steer, queue or interrupt

`send()` queues: the prompt waits for the running turn to end. To choose how a prompt reaches a running turn, write it with `s.prompts.create()`:

```ts
import { mintWireMessageId } from '@kortix/sdk';

const messageId = mintWireMessageId();
await s.prompts.create({
  clientMessageId: messageId,
  messageId,
  remintOnDelivery: true,
  delivery: 'steer',
  parts: [{ type: 'text', text: 'Use the staging database.' }],
});
```

| `delivery` | While a turn runs |
| --- | --- |
| `steer` | The running turn reads the prompt at its next step. The turn does not stop. |
| `queue` | The prompt waits for the turn to end, then runs as its own turn. |
| `interrupt` | The turn ends after the running tool. Then the prompt runs as its own turn. |

With no turn running, all three start a turn. A `steer` needs the runtime capability `session.steer` and must come from the member who started the turn. Otherwise the server delivers it as `queue`, and the inbox row shows `steer_fallback`:

- `unsupported`: the runtime does not list `session.steer`.
- `not_prompter`: another member started the running turn.
- `turn_ended`: the turn ended before the prompt reached it.

The inbox lists only prompts not yet delivered:

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `s.prompts.list()` | Prompts the session still owes, oldest first, with `state`, `reason` and `steer_fallback` |
| `s.prompts.edit(promptId, text)` | Replaces the text of a waiting prompt. `409` when the prompt is already on its way to the runtime |
| `s.prompts.interrupt(promptId)` | Turns a waiting prompt into an `interrupt` |
| `s.prompts.retry(promptId)` | Retries a `failed` prompt |
| `s.prompts.remove(promptId)` | Removes a prompt and returns it. `409` when the agent already reads it |
| `s.prompts.hold(true \| false)` | Holds or releases the whole queue |
| `s.turn()` | Running turns (`turns`, empty when idle), how the last turn ended (`last_ended`), recent failures (`recent_failures`), and whether the caller may stop the running turn (`can_stop`) |

#### Attach files

Upload a file to the project, then send its id in a file part:

```ts
const file = await kortix.project(projectId).attachments.upload(blobFile);
const fileMessageId = mintWireMessageId();
await s.prompts.create({
  clientMessageId: fileMessageId,
  messageId: fileMessageId,
  remintOnDelivery: true,
  parts: [
    { type: 'text', text: 'Summarize this report.' },
    { type: 'file', attachment_id: file.attachment_id, filename: file.filename, mime: file.mime },
  ],
});
```

- Limits: 50 MiB per file, 100 MiB per message, 20 files per message. A file must not be empty.
- An upload needs no running sandbox. Before the prompt runs, the server writes each file to `/workspace/uploads/.kortix-inbox/`.
- An upload that no prompt uses expires after 24 hours.
- A browser composer uses `usePromptAttachments`. See [React](/docs/#react).

### Stream events

```ts
const stream = await s.stream({
  onEvent: (event) => { /* one runtime event */ },
  onGapRehydrate: (gapMs) => { /* re-read state you cache */ },
  signal: abortController.signal,
});
stream.close();
```

`stream()` readies the session, then opens the session runtime's event stream through the Kortix API. It is server-sent events over a streaming `fetch`. There is no WebSocket. The same stream serves both harnesses.

1. Open the stream before you send, so you do not miss the first events.
2. Send the prompt.
3. Close the stream on `session.idle` for your runtime session id. A subagent's conversation sends its own `session.idle` with its own id.

The SDK keeps the connection alive for you:

- It reconnects at once (250 ms) after a healthy stream drops, else with backoff from 1 s up to 30 s plus up to 50% jitter, and resumes with `Last-Event-ID`.
- The sandbox sends a keepalive every 20 s. After 60 s with no frame, the SDK drops the connection and reconnects.
- `onGapRehydrate(gapMs)` runs after a reconnect when the dropped stream had delivered events, or when the gap was longer than 5 s.
- After 8 consecutive failed connects (a stopped or deleted sandbox), the stream stops. Open a new one after the session runs again.
- Events arrive in batches every 16 ms. Consecutive `message.part.delta` events for one part field arrive as one event: `properties.delta` holds the joined text, `id` is the last event's id, and `coalesced` lists the events it replaced. Append `delta` as for a single event.

The events a chat needs:

| `type` | When it fires |
| --- | --- |
| `message.updated` / `message.removed` | A message changed or was deleted. |
| `message.part.updated` / `message.part.removed` | A part (text, tool call, file) changed or was removed. |
| `message.part.delta` | Text was appended to a part field. |
| `session.status` | A conversation's busy state changed. |
| `session.idle` | A turn finished. |
| `session.error` | A turn failed. The event carries the error. |
| `permission.asked` / `permission.replied` | The agent asked for a permission, and the answer. |
| `question.asked` / `question.replied` / `question.rejected` | The agent asked a question, and the answer. |
| `todo.updated` | The agent's todo list changed. |

Each event has `type` and `properties`, for example `event.properties.sessionID`. The stream carries more event types than these. `narrowChatEvent(event)` from `@kortix/sdk` maps the events above, except `message.part.delta`, to typed chat events, maps `server.connected` to a `connection` event, and returns `null` for every other event. It merges `question.replied` and `question.rejected` into `question.answered`.

A host whose `fetch` cannot stream a response body, such as React Native, passes its own transport. The SDK keeps reconnect, resume, backoff and the keepalive check:

```ts
import { configureKortix, type RuntimeEventTransport } from '@kortix/sdk';

const transport: RuntimeEventTransport = async function* ({ url, headers, signal }) {
  // Yield { data, id?, retry? } for each server-sent message of ONE connection.
  // Return when the server ends it. Throw (with `status` for a non-2xx answer) when it fails.
};
configureKortix({ backendUrl, getToken, eventStreamTransport: transport });
```

A host without `visibilitychange` and `online` events calls `notifyHostSignal('visible' | 'online' | 'retry')`. The stream that `useSession` holds reconnects on it. A `stream()` handle does not listen to it.

A React app uses `useSession` instead of `stream()`. See [React](/docs/#react).

### Read the conversation

| Method | Returns | Wakes the sandbox |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `s.messages({ limit?, before?, conversationId? })` | A page of messages, oldest first, as `{ info, parts }` (`kortix.transcript.v1`), and `hasMore`. `conversationId` reads a subagent's conversation. | Yes |
| `s.transcriptSync({ limit?, before?, child? })` | Deprecated: read `s.log()`. The v1 copy of the conversation: every part, with tool inputs and outputs. `limit` at most 500. Pass `next_cursor` as `before` for the older window. `child` reads a subagent's saved transcript. | No |
| `s.log({ thread?, before_seq?, limit? })` | One page of the stored session log (`kortix.session/2`, `SessionLogPage`): the session record, every thread head, and the thread's newest `limit` messages (default 40, at most 500) by `seq`. `thread` defaults to the root thread; pass the first message's `seq` as `before_seq` for older ones. `null` when the session has no stored log yet (it is journaled at each turn end), or no such thread. | No |
| `s.transcript({ limit?, chars? })` | Deprecated: read `s.log()`. A short digest: text and tool calls without inputs and outputs. `limit` 1 to 500 (default 40), `chars` 80 to 5,000 per message (default 700). | No |
| `s.get()` | The session record: status, agent, model, labels, metadata. | No |
| `s.messageAuthors()` | The member or session that sent each message, keyed by message id, and the member who stopped each stopped turn (`stops`). | No |
| `s.modelUsage()` | The model that answered each turn, and `fallback_from` when a fallback model answered. | No |

A `transcriptSync` window also stops at 1,000,000 characters of JSON. It keeps its newest messages, and `next_cursor` then names the oldest one it kept.

#### Answer the agent

```ts
const { statuses, permissions, questions } = await s.pending();
await s.answerPermission(permissions[0].id, 'once'); // 'once' | 'always' | 'reject'
await s.answerQuestion(questions[0].id, [['Yes']]); // one answer list per question; null dismisses
```

#### Control the session

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `s.abort()` | Ends the running turn. |
| `s.rewind(messageId)` / `s.restoreRewind()` | Rolls the conversation back to a user message, and undoes that before the next prompt. Needs `session.rewind`. |
| `s.compact(model?)` | Summarizes the conversation into a shorter context. Needs `session.compact`. |
| `s.stop()` | Stops the sandbox. The disk, the branch and the conversation stay. |
| `s.restart()` | Stops and starts the sandbox in place. Clears a failed-start verdict. |
| `s.delete()` | Deletes the session and its sandbox. Resolves to `{ ok, restore_until }`: the session is restorable until then (30 days); after that its messages, files and branch are deleted. |
| `s.restore()` | Restores a deleted session before `restore_until`. Its next start boots a new sandbox from the branch. A refusal throws an `ApiError` with `code` `session_not_deleted`, `session_sandbox_removal_pending` (retry in a minute) or `session_purged`. See [Restore a deleted session](/docs/#computers). |
| `s.recover()` | Continues a lost session (the provider removed its sandbox) on a new sandbox. Its next start boots from the branch and loads the saved conversation back into the agent. A refusal throws an `ApiError` with `code` `session_not_lost`, `session_transcript_missing` (nothing changed), `session_sandbox_removal_pending` (retry in a minute) or `session_log_restore_off`. See [Continue a lost session](/docs/#computers). |
| `s.configState()` / `s.reloadConfig({ force? })` | Reads whether the session runs the current config from the base branch, and loads that config into the running session. The reload restarts the runtime. While a turn runs it returns `409` `SESSION_BUSY`; `force: true` ends the turn. |
| `s.setSharing(intent)` | Sets who can open the session. See [Who can open a session](/docs/#computers). |
| `s.publicShares.create({ transcript: true })` | Makes one read-only link to the conversation. See [Public links](/docs/#computers). |
| `s.reminders.create(input)` | Schedules a prompt into this session. See [Triggers & reminders](/docs/#automations). |

The full method list is in [SDK reference](/docs/#sdk-reference).

### Files and previews

`s.files` reads and writes the session's live sandbox. Every call readies the session first.

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `list(dir)`, `read(path)`, `readBlob(path)` | Lists a directory and reads a file as text or as a `Blob`. |
| `status()` | The git status of changed files. |
| `findFiles(query, { type?, limit? })`, `findText(pattern)` | Finds files by name and by content. |
| `write(path, blob)` | Writes a file and overwrites it in place. |
| `upload(file, targetPath?, filename?)` | Writes a new file. It never overwrites: a taken name gets a unique name. |
| `create`, `copy`, `remove`, `mkdir`, `rename` | Edits the file tree. |

Previews need a ready handle:

```ts
await s.ensureReady();
const url = s.previewUrl(3000, '/docs'); // a URL a browser tab opens
const fixed = s.proxyUrl('http://localhost:3000/'); // rewrite a localhost URL the agent printed
const direct = s.sandboxPortUrl(3000); // the authenticated API proxy URL, for a bearer-token client
const ports = await s.previews(); // ports the runtime exposes
```

See [Ports and previews](/docs/#computers).

### Runtime capabilities

A session runs on one of two harnesses, OpenCode or pi. `s.health()` lists what the runtime supports in `health.capabilities`. Hide a control when its capability is absent:

```ts
import { runtimeSupports } from '@kortix/sdk';

await s.ensureReady();
const { health } = await s.health();
if (!runtimeSupports(health?.capabilities, 'session.compact')) hideCompactButton();
```

| Capability | Feature |
| --- | --- |
| `session.rewind` | Roll back to a message, and restore it |
| `session.compact` | Summarize the conversation on demand |
| `session.commands` | Project slash commands |
| `session.fork` | Fork a session at a message |
| `session.subagents` | Subagent child sessions |
| `session.mcp` | MCP servers the runtime connects itself |
| `session.todo` | The runtime's todo list |
| `session.shell` | A shell command run as a turn |
| `session.attach` | Attach the harness's own terminal client |
| `session.config` | A runtime config document a client reads and patches |
| `session.steer` | A prompt read by the running turn at its next step |
| `session.log.v2` | The runtime event stream serves the session log as `log.*` frames (`?schema=v2`) |

- OpenCode lists all 12. It lists `session.steer` only on OpenCode 1.18.15 or later.
- pi lists `session.subagents`, `session.compact`, `session.commands`, `session.steer` and `session.log.v2`.
- A route for an unlisted feature answers `501 feature_not_supported`.
- `runtimeSupports` returns `true` before a health answer arrives, and for a runtime that lists no `session.*` capability.

See [Harnesses: OpenCode and pi](/docs/#agents).

### Start sessions from your server

Your server holds one Kortix credential and starts sessions for its own customers. Your customers never sign in to Kortix.

1. Get a credential. A personal access key (`kortix_pat_…`) acts as you. A service account key (`kortix_sa_…`) acts as itself and needs a project role first. See [API keys and tokens](/docs/#security).
2. Create the client with `createScopedKortix` from `@kortix/sdk/server`. It never writes the process-wide config, so one request never reads another request's config or sandbox. See [Servers that act for many users](/docs/#servers-that-act-for-many-users).

Both credentials give the session the origin `backend`. The request body cannot set the origin. A token from inside a session sandbox is never `backend`.

```ts
import { createScopedKortix } from '@kortix/sdk/server';

const kortix = createScopedKortix({
  backendUrl: process.env.KORTIX_API_URL!,
  getToken: async () => process.env.KORTIX_API_KEY!,
});

const created = await kortix.project(projectId).sessions.create({
  agent_name: 'support',
  initial_prompt: 'Answer ticket ' + ticket.id + '.',
  labels: ['support', 'customer:' + customer.id],
  metadata: { app_customer_id: customer.id, app_ticket_id: ticket.id },
  runtime_context: { customer_id: customer.id, plan_tier: customer.tier },
  connector_bindings: { gmail: { connection_id: supportInboxConnectionId } },
  secrets: ['HELPDESK_API_KEY'],
});
```

On a scoped client, `kortix.runtime()` throws. Reach a session through `kortix.session(projectId, sessionId)`.

The same kind of create over HTTP, with an idempotency key:

```bash
curl -X POST "https://api.kortix.com/v1/projects/<project_id>/sessions" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer kortix_pat_…" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -H "Idempotency-Key: my-app:ticket-123:reply" \
  -d '{
    "agent_name": "support",
    "initial_prompt": "Answer ticket 123.",
    "runtime_context": { "customer_id": "c-42" },
    "secrets": ["HELPDESK_API_KEY"]
  }'
```

The API answers `201` with the session. The full route reference is at https://api.kortix.com/v1/docs.

#### Retry a create safely

- Send one `Idempotency-Key` per logical create: 1 to 255 characters of `A-Z a-z 0-9 . _ : + / = -`. A replay with the same key returns the same session.
- A key is unique across all of Kortix. A key that another project or account already used returns `409` `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_CONFLICT`. Put your own unique ids in the key.
- A replay while the first create still runs answers `202` with `status` and `session_id`.
- A replay with different `connector_bindings`, `provider_secret_pools`, `secrets` or `runtime_context` returns `409` `IDEMPOTENCY_BINDING_CONFLICT`, `IDEMPOTENCY_PROVIDER_POOL_CONFLICT`, `IDEMPOTENCY_SECRETS_CONFLICT` or `IDEMPOTENCY_CONTEXT_CONFLICT`.
- A key whose session was deleted returns `409` `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_SESSION_DELETED`. Use a new key.
- `sessions.create()` in the SDK sends no `Idempotency-Key`. To retry an SDK create, generate `session_id` yourself. A retry that answers `409` `session_already_exists` means the first create succeeded.

#### Pass context, accounts and secrets

`runtime_context` gives the agent non-secret values, such as your customer id.

- Values are strings (at most 4,096 characters), numbers, booleans or `null`.
- Keys are 1 to 64 characters: a lowercase letter, then lowercase letters, digits, `.`, `_` or `-`. A key with a segment such as `token`, `secret`, `password`, `credential`, `api_key`, `private_key`, `authorization` or `cookie` is rejected.
- At most 64 keys and 16 KiB of JSON.
- The sandbox receives the object as JSON in the environment variable `KORTIX_SESSION_CONTEXT`.
- You set it at create. Scope changes do not touch it.

`connector_bindings` pins the connector account each alias uses, as `{ alias: { connection_id } }`, at most 64 entries.

- When you set it, an alias you did not bind gets no account. Pass `inherit_unbound: true` to let unbound aliases use the project default.
- A shared (`project`) account works for a personal access key and a service account key.
- A private (`member`) account works only for its owner, and only in a private session. A new session is private. Binding a private account to a shared session returns `409`. A service account never uses a private account.
- The credential never enters the sandbox. Kortix resolves it on every connector call. See [Connectors, connections and accounts](/docs/#connectors).

`secrets` narrows the project secrets the sandbox receives, from the agent's grant down to the identifiers you list. It never widens the grant. `[]` delivers no project secrets. Only a `backend`-origin caller may set it; any other caller gets `403` `origin_override_forbidden`. See [Secrets](/docs/#security).

Tag each session with your own ids in `labels` and `metadata`. Find a customer's sessions with `listPage({ labels: ['customer:c-42'] })`.

### Scope and cost

Read and change a running session's secrets and connector accounts:

```ts
const scope = await s.scope();
scope.secrets_allowlist; // null: the agent's grant applies
scope.connector_bindings_configured; // false: the session uses the project defaults

const next = await s.rescope({
  secrets: ['HELPDESK_API_KEY'],
  connector_bindings: { gmail: { connection_id: otherConnectionId } },
});
next.applied_live; // true when the sandbox took the new secrets now
```

Each field you send replaces its whole previous value. A field you leave out stays as it is.

| Value | `secrets` | `connector_bindings` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `null` | Use the agent's secret grant | Remove the override and use the project defaults |
| `[]` / `{}` | No project secrets | No connector account at all, project defaults included |
| A list / a map | Exactly these secrets, inside the agent's grant | Exactly these accounts |

- Sending `{}` where you meant `null` blocks every connector.
- A connector change applies on the next connector call.
- A secret change restarts the runtime when the delivered set changes. `applied_live` says whether it applied now or applies at the next start. `push_failed: true` means the change is stored, but the running runtime still has the old secrets.
- A removed secret stops reaching the sandbox. It cannot leave a model context or a process that already read it. Rotate the secret when that matters.
- A rescope cannot widen past the agent's grant.
- Only the session owner or a project manager may change the scope.

Read usage without waking the sandbox:

```ts
const record = await s.cost(); // LLM and compute totals, tokens, model usage, compute time, ledger entries

const page = await kortix.billing.sessionCosts.list({ accountId, projectId, from, to, limit: 25 });
```

`sessionCosts.list` covers every session of the account in a half-open UTC window `[from, to)`. The default window is the last 30 days. Filter by `projectId` or `ownerId`. See [Track usage](/docs/#billing).

### Session errors

A session create returns these errors. The `code` field of the JSON body holds the code.

| Status | Code | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `400` | `INVALID_SESSION_MODEL` | The model id is malformed or the account cannot use the model. |
| `400` | `INVALID_SESSION_CONNECTOR_BINDINGS` | The binding map is malformed. |
| `400` | `INVALID_SESSION_RUNTIME_CONTEXT` | The runtime context breaks a key, value, entry or size rule. |
| `400` | `INVALID_SESSION_SECRETS` | The secrets list is malformed or has more than 128 identifiers. |
| `400` | `INVALID_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY` | The Idempotency-Key header is longer than 255 characters or has a character outside the allowed set. |
| `400` | `UNKNOWN_SANDBOX_TEMPLATE` | The sandbox slug names no sandbox in the project. |
| `402` | `subscription_required` / `insufficient_credits` | The account cannot start a session. |
| `403` | `origin_override_forbidden` | A caller that is not a backend origin sent a secrets list. |
| `403` | `CONNECTOR_NOT_ASSIGNED` | The agent is not granted a connector you bound. |
| `404` | `CONNECTOR_CONNECTION_NOT_FOUND` | The account does not exist for that connector in this project, or the caller may not use it. |
| `404` | `SECRET_IDENTIFIER_NOT_FOUND` | The secrets list names an unknown project secret. |
| `409` | `CONNECTOR_CONNECTION_INACTIVE` | The account or its connector is inactive, disabled or not connected. |
| `409` | `PERSONAL_CONNECTOR_CONNECTION_REQUIRES_PRIVATE_SESSION` | A private account is bound to a session that is not private. |
| `409` | `SECRET_IDENTIFIER_KEY_COLLISION` | Two listed secrets set the same environment variable. |
| `409` | `session_already_exists` | A session with this session id exists. |
| `409` | `IDEMPOTENCY_*_CONFLICT` | Another create already holds the key, or this key was used before with a different body. |
| `409` | `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_SESSION_DELETED` | The key belongs to a deleted session. |
| `500` | `SESSION_CREATE_FAILED` | The create failed. The body has retry set to true: retry it. |
| `503` | `SESSION_MODEL_RESOLUTION_FAILED` | The server could not resolve the default model. Retry it. |

`s.rescope()` returns these errors:

| Status | Code | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `400` | `INVALID_SESSION_SCOPE` | The body is malformed, or it has neither field. |
| `403` | `NOT_IN_AGENT_GRANT` | A secret is outside the agent's grant. |
| `403` | `NOT_GRANTED_CONNECTOR` | A connector is outside the agent's grant. |
| `403` | `SECRET_IDENTIFIER_NOT_AVAILABLE` | A secret does not exist or the session may not use it. |
| `403` | `CONNECTOR_CONNECTION_NOT_FOUND` / `CONNECTOR_CONNECTION_INACTIVE` | The account is unknown, unusable or inactive. |
| `409` | `AGENT_GRANT_UNRESOLVED` | The server could not read the agent's grant. Retry it. |
| `409` | `SECRET_IDENTIFIER_KEY_COLLISION` | Two listed secrets set the same environment variable. |
| `409` | `PERSONAL_CONNECTOR_CONNECTION_REQUIRES_PRIVATE_SESSION` | A private account is bound to a session that is not private. |

A rescope by anyone other than the session owner or a project manager returns `403` with no code.

### Errors and retries

Every SDK call rejects with an `Error` subclass. Check `instanceof`, then branch on `status` or `code`:

```ts
import { ApiError, BillingError, SessionNotReadyError, formatBillingErrorForUI } from '@kortix/sdk';

try {
  await kortix.project(projectId).sessions.create({ agent_name: 'my-agent' });
} catch (err) {
  if (err instanceof BillingError) {
    const ui = formatBillingErrorForUI(err); // { alertTitle, alertSubtitle }
  } else if (err instanceof ApiError) {
    console.error(err.status, err.code, err.detail);
  } else {
    throw err;
  }
}
```

| Class | When it throws | Key fields |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `ApiError` | Any failed request: an error status, a network failure, a timeout or an abort | `status`, `code`, `detail`, `response`, `url`, `endpoint`, `timeout` |
| `AuthError` (extends `ApiError`) | `getToken()` returned `null`. No request was sent | `code` is `NO_SESSION` |
| `BillingError` (extends `ApiError`) | Every `402` | `code`, for example `insufficient_credits`; `detail.message` |
| `RequestTooLargeError` (extends `ApiError`) | A `431`, usually too many files in one request | `detail.suggestion` |
| `SessionNotReadyError` | A preview method ran before `ensureReady()` | `name` is `SessionNotReadyError` |

- `code` is the API's error code, or the status as a string.
- An aborted request has `name: 'AbortError'` and `code: 'ABORTED'`. It is not a failure.
- A request that passes its own timeout has `code: 'TIMEOUT'`. The default timeout is 30 s per request.
- `formatBillingErrorForUI(err)` returns `null` for any error that is not a billing error.
- `isSandboxNotReadyError(err)` and `isRuntimeStartingError(err)` are true while the sandbox is starting, stopped or parked. Show a waiting state and retry.
- A `401` is sent once more with a fresh token from `getToken()`. See [getToken and 401 recovery](/docs/#gettoken-and-401-recovery).

The SDK retries some requests before your code sees an error:

| Method | Retried | Never retried |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `GET`, `HEAD` | Up to 2 times, after 250 ms and 500 ms, on `502`, `503`, `504` or a network failure | A `500` or any other status |
| `DELETE` | Up to 2 times on a network failure with no response | Any received status |
| `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH` | Never | — |

To make a create retry-safe, see [Retry a create safely](#retry-a-create-safely).

## React

`@kortix/sdk/react` is the React layer of the SDK. It gives you one hook that runs a whole session view, a headless chat kit, and data hooks for accounts, projects, sessions, secrets, triggers, Apps, notifications and access checks. It works in React and React Native.

### Set up

<Steps>
<Step title="Install">
Install the SDK and its two optional peers: `react` 18 or later and `@tanstack/react-query` 5.75.2 or later.

```bash
npm install @kortix/sdk react @tanstack/react-query
```
</Step>
<Step title="Create the client">
Create the client once, at module scope. Every hook reads this client. See [SDK](/docs/#sdk) for the options and [Authentication](/docs/#sdk-authentication) for `getToken`.

```ts
// lib/kortix.ts
import { createKortix } from '@kortix/sdk';

export const kortix = createKortix({
  backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1',
  getToken: async () => getMyToken(),
});
```
</Step>
<Step title="Add the providers">
Import that module once, then wrap the tree in `QueryClientProvider`. `useSession`, the prompt hooks and most data hooks are React Query hooks. They throw without a provider.

```tsx
'use client';
import '@/lib/kortix';
import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { useState } from 'react';

export function Providers({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {
  const [queryClient] = useState(() => new QueryClient());
  return <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>{children}</QueryClientProvider>;
}
```
</Step>
</Steps>

Keep one client and one `QueryClient` per host. A second `createKortix()` call with other options re-points every earlier client.

### useSession

`useSession(projectId, sessionId, options?)` runs one session view end to end. It polls `POST /start` until the sandbox is ready, opens the event stream, syncs messages and status, paints saved history, and sends prompts. Call it once per session view.

```tsx
import { useSession } from '@kortix/sdk/react';

export function SessionView({ projectId, sessionId }: { projectId: string; sessionId: string }) {
  const s = useSession(projectId, sessionId);

  if (s.messages.length === 0 && s.phase !== 'ready') {
    if (s.phase === 'error') return <NotRunning stage={s.stage} onRetry={s.retry} />;
    if (s.conversationEmpty) return <Composer onSend={s.send} />;
    return s.savedTranscript === 'none' ? <Booting stage={s.stage} /> : <MessageSkeleton />;
  }

  return (
    <>
      {s.messages.map(({ info, parts }) => (
        <Message key={info.id} info={info} parts={parts} />
      ))}
      {s.sendError && <SendError error={s.sendError} />}
      <Composer busy={s.isBusy} disabled={s.phase !== 'ready'} onSend={s.send} onStop={s.cancel} />
    </>
  );
}
```

The sandbox is ready when `/start` answers `stage: 'ready'`. The hook runs no separate health check. While the session starts, render by this rule:

| State | Render |
| --- | --- |
| `messages` has rows | The transcript. Saved history paints before the sandbox wakes. |
| No rows and `conversationEmpty` is `true` | The composer. The session has no messages and nothing to wait for. |
| No rows and `savedTranscript` is `'none'` | A boot screen with `stage`. Nothing can show until the runtime answers. |
| No rows and `savedTranscript` is `'loading'` | Placeholder rows. A saved copy is on its way. |

Saved history is the transcript copy the API writes at each turn end. The first window holds the newest 40 messages, at most 1,000,000 characters. `loadOlder()` reads older windows, also while the sandbox is off. Live messages then reconcile into it by message id.

On an API older than the session log (`GET …/log` answers `404` with no `code`), an open reads the first v1 window once: `GET …/transcript?shape=sync&history=true`. The session-open snapshot (`GET …/snapshot`) asks for no v1 window (`?transcript=0`). `useSession` passes that answer to its `useSessionSync` as `mirror`. If you mount a second `useSessionSync` for the root conversation next to `useSession`, pass `mirror: null` so it does not read the window a second time. Omit `mirror` in two cases, so that the hook makes the one read itself:

- Next to `useSession({ chatEngine: false })`. That `useSession` makes no saved-history read.
- On a `savedChild` hook. It reads the sub-agent's own window, which the open does not read.

To show a session's saved copy before `useSession` binds it, for example on a connecting screen, call `readSavedSessionCopy({ projectId, sessionId, runtimeSessionId })` from `@kortix/sdk`. It reads the session log first and returns `{ envelope, empty, log }`. `log` is the session log's newest page (`GET …/log`): apply it to the session store (`applySessionLogPage`) and render `useSessionLogThread`. No `GET …/transcript` is read then. A new session with no stored log yet answers `404` with `code: 'session_log_missing'`: `log` is `null`, `envelope` is `null`, and nothing is read. Only when the log answers `404` with no `code` (an API older than the session log) does it read the v1 copy: `log` is absent, and you pass `envelope` to `useSessionSync` as `mirror`. `empty` is `true` when the copy proves the conversation empty and the turn record shows no ended or open turn. The function never throws. The `sessionLog` option is deprecated and ignored.

The SDK holds that copy for the open that follows, for up to 5 minutes. When `useSession` binds the session, the saved-history read answers from the held copy. The window is downloaded once per open. `resetIdentityState()` forgets the held copy.

To show a sub-agent's saved history, call `useSessionSync(childRuntimeSessionId, { kortixSessionScope: sessionId, savedChild: true })`. It reads the session log first: a page goes in the session store, and the thread comes from `useSessionLogThread(parentSession, childRuntimeSessionId)`. Only when the log answers `404` with no `code` (an API older than the session log) does it read the child's v1 saved window from the parent session. The device keeps no copy of it. While the session store holds the parent session's log, `useSessionSync` reads no saved copy, whatever `sessionLog` says.

#### Returns

| Field | Type | What it holds |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `phase` | `'starting' \| 'ready' \| 'error'` | The overall state. Enable sends at `ready`. `error` when `stage` is `failed` or `stopped`, or `/start` failed. |
| `stage` | `string \| null` | The raw `/start` stage: `provisioning`, `starting`, `ready`, `stopped` or `failed`. For boot screens. |
| `startError` | error or `null` | The terminal `/start` failure. Render it instead of a spinner. |
| `failure` | object or `null` | The typed terminal provisioning failure from `/start`. |
| `retry` | `() => void` | Polls `/start` again. Use it for a Retry button. |
| `messages` | `{ info, parts }[]` | The transcript. Parts stream in live. |
| `savedTranscript` | `'loading' \| 'shown' \| 'none'` | Whether saved history can show before the sandbox wakes. An unknown is `loading`. |
| `conversationEmpty` | `boolean` | The saved copy proves the session has no messages, no turn ended since, and nothing is open or queued. |
| `freshness` | `'idle' \| 'loading' \| 'fresh' \| 'stale' \| 'error'` | The transcript read state. `loading` or `error` with no rows is a wait or a failure, not an empty session. |
| `retryTranscript` | `() => void` | Reads the transcript tail again. |
| `hasOlder`, `isLoadingOlder`, `loadOlder` | `boolean`, `boolean`, `() => Promise<void>` | Paging toward older messages. |
| `status` | `SessionStatus` | The runtime status of the session. |
| `working` | `WorkingProjection` | `{ state: 'idle' \| 'working', pendingDelivery?, source, turnId, since }`. The one working answer. |
| `isBusy` | `boolean` | `working.state === 'working'`. |
| `canStop` | `boolean` | Whether this viewer may stop the running turn. `false` only when the server's `/turn` answer says `can_stop: false`: in a shared session only the member who started the turn, the session owner or a project manager may stop it. Show no Stop then. |
| `presenceAwake` | `{ until: Date, capAt: Date } \| null` | With `browserPresence`: `until` is when the sandbox stops after the latest presence renewal, and `capAt` is the time presence cannot keep it awake past. Read again every 5 minutes while the person is present, when a turn ends, when the control plane closes a turn, and when the sandbox becomes ready. `null` while the person is absent, for a person who may not start the session, once the session start reports the sandbox not ready, and from the start of a turn until the next renewal answers. |
| `isSending` | `boolean` | A `send` request is in flight. |
| `sendError` | `KortixSendError \| null` | The last `send` failure. A new `send` clears it. |
| `questions`, `permissions` | arrays | Open agent questions and tool-approval requests. A permission here is one tool approval, not an access-control permission. |
| `hasPending` | `boolean` | At least one question or permission is open. |
| `diffs`, `todos` | arrays | Live file diffs and the todo list. |
| `rewindMessageId`, `rewindPending`, `rewindError` | `string \| null`, `boolean`, `KortixSendError \| null` | The staged rewind, a rewind or restore in flight, and its last failure. |
| `models` | `FlatModel[]` | Selectable models. |
| `agents`, `defaultAgent`, `commands` | arrays, `string \| null`, array | Selectable agents, the default agent and slash commands. Available before the sandbox starts. |
| `agentName` | `string \| null` | The agent the session was created with, from the first `/start` answer. `null` when none was bound. |
| `picks` | `SessionPicks` | This session's chosen `model`, `agent` and `variant`, with `setModel`, `setAgent` and `setVariant`. `send` uses them. |
| `runtimeSessionId` | `string \| null` | The runtime's root session id, or `null` while it resolves. `opencodeSessionId` is a deprecated alias. |
| `runtimeUrl` | `string \| null` | The session runtime's proxy URL, or `null` until `stage` is `ready`. For a terminal attach or a direct runtime call. |

#### Actions

| Action | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `send(text, override?)` | Sends a prompt through the session's prompt inbox. The message shows at once, before the server answers. `override` is `{ model?, agent?, variant? }` for this message only. It does nothing before `phase` is `ready`. Failures go to `sendError`; it never throws. |
| `sendParts(parts, override?)` | Sends text and file parts. `override` also takes `clientMessageId`: send a failed submission again with the same value and the server delivers it once. Throws a not-ready error before `ready`. |
| `cancel()` | Stops the running turn. It holds the queued prompts first, so a queued prompt does not start the next turn. Resolves `{ status: 'aborted' }`, `{ status: 'timed-out' }` or `{ status: 'failed', error }`. |
| `rewind(messageId)` | Rewinds the session to a user message and restores file state. The removed messages stay recoverable until the next prompt. Throws while the session is busy. |
| `restoreRewind()` | Restores the removed messages before another prompt commits the rewind. |
| `runCommand(command, args, options?)` | Runs a project slash command. `options` is `{ agent?, model?, variant? }`. |
| `answerQuestion(id, answers)` | Answers an open question. `answers` is `string[][]`, one array per question. |
| `rejectQuestion(id)` | Rejects an open question. |
| `answerPermission(id, reply, message?)` | Answers a tool approval. `reply` is `'once'`, `'always'` or `'reject'`. |

The three reply actions remove the item only after the server accepts the reply. On failure they throw a `KortixSendError` and the item stays open. `removeQuestion` and `removePermission` are deprecated: they never reach the agent, so the run stays blocked.

#### Options

| Option | Default | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `enabled` | `true` | `false` holds the whole hook: no `/start`, no stream. Use it until a precondition passes. |
| `waitMs` | `15000` | The long-poll budget sent to `/start`. The server clamps it. |
| `chatEngine` | `true` | `false` when your app mounts its own chat surface for this session. Then `messages`, `diffs` and `todos` are empty, questions and permissions stay live, and the session log is still negotiated (`useSessionLogThread` reads it). |
| `subscribeMessages` | `true` | `false` re-renders the caller only when the transcript changes shape, not on each streamed delta. Draw the rows in a child with `useSessionMessages`. |
| `replayStartStash` | `true` | Sends a first prompt that a "new session" screen saved, once the session is ready and empty. |
| `initialRuntimeSessionId` | `null` | A runtime session id your server already holds. The transcript renders from it while `/start` runs. The `/start` answer wins. |
| `browserPresence` | `false` | Holds this tab's presence lease while a person uses the page (input in the last 10 minutes). The lease routes notifications and returns the awake window (`presenceAwake`) for a person who may start the session; the sandbox stops the idle window — 15 minutes by default — after its latest turn, with the page open or not. |
| `presenceAlerts` | `false` | `true` tells the server this tab shows its own notifications, so the server skips phone and Web Push while the tab is in use. Pass `true` only when the project's `notification_center` flag is on and this tab's browser notifications are on and permitted. Needs `browserPresence`. |
| `presencePageExit` | `false` | Ends the presence lease when the page closes. Off, the lease ends at its 90-second expiry. Needs `browserPresence`. Pass the project's `notification_center` flag. |

#### Send errors

`sendError`, `rewindError` and the reply actions use one type, `KortixSendError`: `{ kind, message, cause }` plus one detail field per kind. `message` is ready to display.

| `kind` | Meaning | Extra field |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `billing` | The API refused the turn with `402`. | `billing`: the parsed `BillingError`. |
| `connector` | The session needs a connector that has no usable connection. The API refused the turn before the sandbox saw it. | `connectors`: `{ id, slug, name, authorization_strategy }[]`. `project` means one shared connection fixes it. `user` means the person the session runs as must connect. |
| `runtime-not-ready` | The sandbox is still starting. Try again when `phase` is `ready`. | none |
| `runtime-error` | Any other failure. | `gateway` when the LLM gateway answered: `{ provider?, code?, suggestion?, upstreamStatus?, requestId? }`. |

`classifySendError(error)` turns any thrown value into a `KortixSendError`.

### Pending prompts and turn outcomes

`send` covers a composer that sends one prompt at a time. Use these hooks for a queue, for steering a running turn, and to say why a turn ended. They take the same `projectId` and `sessionId` as `useSession`.

#### useSessionPrompts

`useSessionPrompts(projectId, sessionId)` reads and writes the session's prompt inbox on the server. The server delivers pending prompts in order, as `delivery` says.

| Member | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `prompts` | The pending rows. Each has `prompt_id`, `state` (`queued`, `delivering`, `waiting`, `failed`), `delivery`, `placement`, `full_text` and `last_error`. A delivered prompt leaves the list. |
| `enqueue(input)` | Adds one prompt. Resolving means the prompt is stored, not delivered. |
| `edit(promptId, text)` | Replaces a waiting row's text and keeps its place. |
| `remove(promptId)` | Removes a waiting row and returns it, so an undo can enqueue it again. |
| `interrupt(promptId)` | Turns a waiting row into an `interrupt`: the running turn ends after its running tool, then this row runs. |
| `retry(promptId)` | Runs this row next. Use it for a failed row. |
| `hold(held)` | Holds or releases the whole queue. A new send or `retry` releases it. |

`edit`, `remove` and `interrupt` throw a `409` for a row that is already on its way to the runtime.

`enqueue` takes `delivery` (`steer`, `queue` or `interrupt`) and an optional `placement`. The delivery modes and `steer_fallback` are in [Sessions from code](/docs/#send-prompts). `placement` only says where the pending row shows: `transcript` in the message list (implied by `interrupt`), `composer` in the list above the input (implied by `steer` and `queue`).

#### useSessionWorking

`useSessionWorking(projectId, sessionId, { runtimeSessionId })` returns the same `WorkingProjection` as `useSession().working`. It stays `working` while a prompt waits in the inbox, and sets `pendingDelivery: true` while that prompt is still on its way. Keep Stop visible whenever `state` is `working`.

#### Why a turn ended

An interrupted turn's transcript carries only an abort error. The API records the cause the sandbox named, for example its memory guard. A stop that someone asked for is never a failure.

```tsx
import { turnEndNotice } from '@kortix/sdk';
import { useSessionTurnOutcome } from '@kortix/sdk/react';

const outcome = useSessionTurnOutcome(projectId, sessionId);
const notice = turnEndNotice(outcome, userMessageId, { hasError, isAbort });
// { kind: 'sandbox-memory', usedPct, detail }
// | { kind: 'cause', name, message }
// | { kind: 'unexplained' }
// | null
```

Pass the id of the user message that started the turn. `hasError` is `true` when the transcript shows an error on that turn, and `isAbort` is `true` when that error is an abort. A non-abort error returns `null`, because the transcript already explains it.

`useSessionTurnOutcome` makes no request. It reads the cache that `useSessionWorking` keeps fresh. Map each `kind` to your own copy. `null` means show what the transcript shows. `turnEndCause(outcome, messageId)` returns the raw recorded cause, or `null`.

#### Turn-end notifications

`useSession` calls the `onNotify` you pass to `createKortix` when a turn ends: `{ kind: 'task-complete', sessionId, sessionTitle }` or `{ kind: 'session-error', sessionId, errorTitle, sessionTitle }`. Show it only when the person is not looking at that session.

While the view is mounted, its event stream raises these. When the view unmounts with a turn still running, for example because the person opened another page, `useSession` hands the turn to a watch:

- The watch listens for `kortix.control.turn` frames on the session's control stream (`GET …/events?channels=control`), so a hidden window that throttles its timers still hears the end. While that stream is down, it reads `GET …/turn` every 5 s, every 15 s after 5 minutes. It stops after 12 hours.
- When no turn is open, it raises `task-complete`, or `session-error` with the cause the API recorded. A stop that someone asked for raises nothing.
- These two events also carry `projectId`, and `task-complete` carries `watchedSinceMs`: when the watch started, in the host's `Date.now()`. A host that relays completions between tabs skips one it already showed after that instant.
- Mounting a view of the session again ends the watch. The view's stream announces from then on, so one end is never announced twice.
- A host without `onNotify` gets no watch and no extra `/turn` reads.

### Build a chat UI

Start with `SessionThread` from [`@kortix/ui/session`](/docs/#the-thread-alone). It renders the whole chat from one `useSession` result: the transcript, tool calls, prompts, the send error and the composer.

```tsx
import { useSession } from '@kortix/sdk/react';
import { SessionThread } from '@kortix/ui/session';

function Chat({ projectId, sessionId }: { projectId: string; sessionId: string }) {
  const session = useSession(projectId, sessionId);
  return (
    <div className="h-dvh">
      <SessionThread session={session} />
    </div>
  );
}
```

It is the thread the Kortix web app renders. `ToolRegistry.register(name, Row)` replaces one tool row, and its building blocks (`UserMessage`, `ToolPartRenderer`, `SessionChatInput`) compose into a layout of your own. Setup, styles and props: [Kortix UI](/docs/#kortix-ui).

#### Render the parts yourself

To draw every part with your own components, use the headless chat kit. `messages` are runtime messages. The kit turns each one into typed parts you can render with no knowledge of the harness.

```tsx
import { toolViewModel } from '@kortix/sdk';
import { renderParts, useChatTurns, type UseSessionResult } from '@kortix/sdk/react';

function Transcript({ messages }: { messages: UseSessionResult['messages'] }) {
  const turns = useChatTurns(messages);
  return turns.map((turn) =>
    turn.isEmpty ? null : (
      <Row key={turn.message.info.id} role={turn.message.info.role} error={turn.error}>
        {renderParts(turn.parts, {
          text: (p) => (p.synthetic ? null : <Markdown key={p.id}>{p.text}</Markdown>),
          reasoning: (p) => <Thinking key={p.id} text={p.text} />,
          tool: (p) => <ToolCard key={p.id} view={toolViewModel(p.tool)} status={p.tool.status} />,
          file: (p) => <Attachment key={p.id} part={p} />,
          subtask: (p) => <Subtask key={p.id} part={p} />,
          patch: () => null,
          snapshot: () => null,
          agent: () => null,
          retry: (p) => <RetryNotice key={p.id} part={p} />,
          compaction: () => <CompactedNotice />,
          step: () => null,
          unknown: () => null,
        })}
      </Row>
    ),
  );
}
```

| Export | From | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `useChatTurns(messages)` | `@kortix/sdk/react` | One `TurnView` per message: `{ message, parts, error?, isEmpty }`. Memoized on the `messages` array. |
| `renderParts(parts, renderers)` | `@kortix/sdk/react` | Calls the renderer for each part's `kind`. The type requires one renderer per kind, so a new kind fails your build, not your UI. |
| `classifyPart(part)`, `classifyTurn(message)` | `@kortix/sdk` | The classifiers behind `useChatTurns`, for code outside React. |
| `toolViewModel(tool)` | `@kortix/sdk` | A typed view of a tool call: `web-search`, `shell`, `file-read`, `file-write`, `file-edit`, `search`, `task`, `todo`, `question`, or `generic`. |

Part kinds: `text`, `reasoning`, `tool`, `file`, `subtask`, `patch`, `snapshot`, `agent`, `retry`, `compaction`, `step` and `unknown`. A `text` part with `synthetic: true` is internal to the runtime; skip it.

A streaming turn changes the transcript about every 16 ms. To keep the rest of the view still, call `useSession(projectId, sessionId, { subscribeMessages: false })` in the view, and read the rows in the transcript component with `useSessionMessages(session)`. Pass the whole `useSession` result. Only that component re-renders per delta. `useSessionMessages(session, { throttleMs })` paces the updates.

More on parts, tools and turn grouping: [SDK reference](/docs/#custom-transcript-rendering).

#### Read the session log

`useSessionLog(session)` returns the whole session log (`SessionLog`): every thread, its messages by `seq`, and their blocks, plus `source`: `'v2'` (the API's log) or `'v1'` (the conversion of the v1 rows, below). `useSessionLogThread(session, threadId?, options?)` returns one thread: the root thread, or the sub-agent thread `threadId` names. `options.throttleMs` paces the v1 rows as `useSessionMessages` paces them (a transcript reads a streaming turn at most once per window). Pass the whole `useSession` result. Both start no request: `useSession` loads the log. `useSessionLog` changes with every message; to read session fields (`restore_grade`, `grade_counts`, `harness`, `ext`) use `useSessionLogRecord(session)`: the session record without threads, null while the session reads v1, unchanged through message puts and deltas, so its consumer re-renders only on a page or a session patch.

```tsx
import { useSession, useSessionLogThread } from '@kortix/sdk/react';

function Thread({ projectId, sessionId }: { projectId: string; sessionId: string }) {
  const session = useSession(projectId, sessionId, { subscribeMessages: false });
  const root = useSessionLogThread(session);
  if (!root) return null; // no runtime session id yet, or no message yet
  return <ol>{root.messages.map((m) => <li key={m.message_id}>{m.role}: {m.blocks.length} blocks</li>)}</ol>;
}
```

On v2, `useSessionLogThread` adds the v1 rows the log does not hold yet, converted with `fromV1` and placed by time as `useSessionMessages` places them (an older page `loadOlder` read goes before the thread's messages, an optimistic send after them), until the log's put of the same id replaces them; on v1 the conversion already holds them. Render a thread's turns with `sessionLogTurns` ([Block turns](/docs/#block-turns)).

On v2, `useSessionLogThread(session, threadId)` reads a sub-agent thread's newest page the first time a row asks for it. `useSessionLogOlder(session, threadId?)` pages one thread back: it returns `{ hasOlder, isLoadingOlder, loadOlder }`, and `loadOlder()` reads `GET …/log?thread=&before_seq=` before the thread's first `seq`. `threadId` omitted is the root thread, which `useSession().loadOlder` already pages. It returns `hasOlder: false` while the session reads v1. A sub-agent's chat (a view with no `useSession` of its own) reads its thread and its older pages with these two hooks, passing the parent session as `session`.

Every open reads the log first: `GET /v1/projects/:projectId/sessions/:sessionId/log`. No project flag is read, and the runtime is not asked: the stored log is the API's, so a stopped computer shows it too. While that read runs and after it answers a page, `useSession` reads no v1 saved copy: the open asks the snapshot for no transcript window, older pages come from `GET …/log?thread=&before_seq=` (`useSessionLogOlder` pages any thread), and a sub-agent thread loads its first page when it shows.

A new session with no stored log yet (`404`, `code: 'session_log_missing'`) stays on the log with nothing in it: no v1 copy is read, `savedTranscript` is `none` until a message arrives, and the log is read again at the end of each turn. Only when the log answers `404` with no `code` or an invalid page (an API older than the session log) does the session read v1. Both hooks then return the `fromV1` conversion of the v1 rows `useSessionMessages` renders (`source: 'v1'`): one root thread whose id is the runtime session id, with the turn that runs kept open (its calls stay `pending` or `running`). On v1 a `threadId` is a runtime session id. Without a `runtimeSessionId`, or before the store holds a row for it, both hooks return `null`. A message keeps its identity while its row is unchanged.

Both harnesses read v2: an OpenCode thread (converted from v1) and a pi thread (written natively).

An SDK older than the session log reads v1. An API older than the session log answers `404` with no `code` for `GET …/log`, and the session reads v1. On v2, `useSession` paints the device's saved copy of the log first, then the API's page, and asks the session stream for `schema=v2`. What the stream carries depends on the server:

- The runtime side is complete: on `schema=v2` the runtime's stream carries `log.*` frames in place of the `message.*` transcript frames, plus every other frame (status, permissions, questions, todos). A message arrives as a streaming record when it starts, then grows with each streamed delta.
- The API relays `schema=v2`: a runtime that lists `session.log.v2` streams its own `log.*` frames, and the API converts the v1 frames of an older runtime.

A session without a stored log yet (a new one) reads v1 and tries the log again at the end of each turn. `useSessionMessages`, `useSession().messages` and `classifyTurn` return the same rows either way. A session-log message converts with `toV1`, the conversion the API uses for v1 readers, and replaces the v1 row with the same id. In a thread converted from v1 (OpenCode) the rows are exact. A pi thread's rows carry what its blocks map, with a tool call's `details` as `state.metadata`; render its blocks (`sessionLogTurns`) for the full record. A deleted message stays out. A row the log does not hold yet, such as the streaming turn or an optimistic send, keeps its place. A row keeps its identity while its message is unchanged.

### Hooks

Most hooks below are React Query hooks. Unless the row names another return value, a hook returns the query result (`data`, `isLoading`, `error`, …) plus the mutations the row names. A write refreshes the matching reads.

| Hook | What it gives you |
| --- | --- |
| `useAccounts({ userId, enabled })` | The caller's accounts. Pass the signed-in person's `userId` in any host where the person can change without a reload. |
| `useProjects(accountId?, options?)` | The projects in one account. |
| `useProjectSessions(projectId, { scope, parent, startedBy, q, labels, limit, spaceId, createdBy, agent, updatedAfter, keepPreviousData, refetchInterval, pollFirstPageOnly })` | One project's sessions, paged, newest activity first. Read `sessions` (flat) and `fetchNextPage`. `parent: 'root'` returns top-level sessions with `child_count`. `startedBy` is `'me'`, `'others'` or `'automated'`. `q` searches every session the caller can open. `scope: 'project'` is the manager inventory. `spaceId` (experimental, `project_channels` flag) lists one channel or DM, with `first_prompt` and `reply_count` on each row. `createdBy` (a user id) keeps the sessions that person started, `agent` (a slug) the ones that run that agent, `updatedAfter` (ISO 8601) the ones with activity at or after that time. `keepPreviousData: true` keeps the previous filter's rows (`isPlaceholderData`) while a changed filter loads, so a search field does not blank the list. `refetchInterval` (ms, or a function of the loaded sessions) polls every loaded page; add `pollFirstPageOnly: true` (experimental) to poll page one only and keep older loaded pages as they are. |
| `useProjectSpaces(projectId, { enabled })`, `useCreateProjectSpace(projectId)`, `useUpdateProjectSpace(projectId)`, `useOpenProjectDm(projectId)` | Experimental, `project_channels` flag only: keep `enabled: false` while the flag is off (the route returns `403`). The channels and the caller's DMs as `ProjectSpace[]`. `create.mutate({ name, description? })`, `update.mutate({ spaceId, input })` and `openDm.mutate(agentName)` refresh the list. Creating a channel and opening a DM also refresh the onboarding state. See [Channels and DMs](/docs/#channels-and-dms-experimental). |
| `useProjectSpaceGeneralNotice(projectId, { enabled })`, `useChannelsOnboarding(projectId, { enabled })`, `useMarkChannelsOnboarding(projectId)` | Experimental, `project_channels` flag only. `#general`'s "turned on again" line (`{ kind, at, count }` or `null`); its key sits under the spaces key, so a spaces refresh refreshes it. The caller's onboarding state (`intro_seen`, `get_started`). `mark.mutate('channels_intro')` records a mark and refreshes the onboarding state. Every memory write refreshes it too. |
| `useProjectSpaceApps(projectId, spaceId)`, `useProjectSpaceLinks(projectId, spaceId)`, `useAddProjectSpaceLink(projectId, spaceId)`, `usePinProjectSpaceLink(projectId, spaceId)`, `useRemoveProjectSpaceLink(projectId, spaceId)` | Experimental, `project_channels` flag only. One channel's Apps (`App[]`), and links (`ProjectSpaceLink[]`, pinned and shared in its threads). Each link carries `display_title`, `site_name` and `icon_url` (null until the server fetched the page). Disabled until both ids are set. `add.mutate({ url, title? })`, `pin.mutate({ linkId, pinned })` and `remove.mutate(linkId)` refresh the links. |
| `useProjectSpaceMembers(projectId, spaceId)`, `useAddProjectSpaceMembers(projectId, spaceId)`, `useRemoveProjectSpaceMember(projectId, spaceId)`, `useJoinProjectSpace(projectId, spaceId)`, `useLeaveProjectSpace(projectId, spaceId)` | Experimental, `project_channels` flag only. A channel's people (`ProjectSpaceMemberList`: the same list for every viewer; `#general` is every project member). Disabled until both ids are set. `add.mutate(userIds)` adds in one request, `remove.mutate(userId)`, `join.mutate()` and `leave.mutate()`; each refreshes the spaces list and the members under it. |
| `useProjectMemoryLayer(projectId, layerKey)`, `useProjectMemoryPages(projectId, layerKey, { type? })`, `useProjectMemoryPage(projectId, pageId)`, `useProjectMemoryHistory(projectId, pageId)` | Experimental, needs the project's Files (`drives`): pass `{ enabled: false }` while Files are off. A layer key is `'company'` or a space id; a page is one Markdown file in the layer's Drive folder. History lists at most 20 versions (`data.versions`). Disabled until the ids are set. See [Memory](/docs/#memory-experimental). |
| `useAddProjectMemoryNote(projectId, layerKey)`, `useUpdateProjectMemoryPage(projectId)`, `useDeleteProjectMemoryPage(projectId)` | `note.mutate({ text })`, `update.mutate({ pageId, input: { base_version, body } })`, `remove.mutate(pageId)` (deletes the file for good). Update writes the returned page into its cache slot. Every write refreshes every memory read of the project (`qk.project.memory(projectId)`) and the onboarding state. Errors are `ApiError` with `code` (`version_conflict`, `secret_detected`, …). |
| `useProjectMemorySearch(projectId, { q, layer, type, limit }, { enabled })`, `useSessionMemoryUsage(projectId, sessionId, { enabled })` | Experimental, needs the project's Files (`drives`). Search runs while `q` has 2 to 200 characters after trimming and keeps the previous hits (`data.hits`) while the next query loads, also when `q` drops below 2: render hits only for a valid `q`. Debounce the input in your component: every distinct `q` is one request. A memory write refreshes the hits. Usage is the thread's "Used memory" (`data.layers`); once `loaded_at` is set it never goes stale, and no memory write invalidates it. |
| `useSessionChildren(projectId, parentSessionId, { q, limit, enabled })` | The sessions one parent started, paged. Keep `enabled: false` until the parent row opens. |
| `useProjectModels(projectId)` | Selectable models as `FlatModel[]`. Empty while the list loads. |
| `useProjectModelPickerCatalog(projectId)` | The full model-picker record per model (`reasoning_options`, `temperature`, `limit`). `undefined` while it loads. |
| `useModelAccess(projectId)` | The project's disabled providers and models, and `setEnabled({ target, id, enabled })` with `target` `'provider'` or `'model'`. |
| `useProjectConfig(projectId)` | The project's config summary (default agent and slash commands) as `ProjectConfigSummary`, or `undefined` while it loads. |
| `useVisibleAgents({ projectId })` | The agents a person can pick, as `Agent[]`. |
| `useProjectSecrets(projectId)` | Secrets, with `upsert`, `remove`, `setPersonal` and `removePersonal`. Values are never returned. |
| `useAccountSecretResources(accountId, projectId?)`, `useSessionProviderSecretPools(projectId, sessionId)` | Account-level secret resources, and the provider-key pools one session can use. |
| `useProjectTriggers(projectId)` | Triggers, with `create`, `update`, `remove`, `fire`, `dryRun` and `replay`. `fire.mutate(slug)` or `fire.mutate({ slug, payload? })` returns `FireProjectTriggerResponse`. `dryRun.mutate(slug \| { slug, payload? })` returns `ProjectTriggerDryRunResult` and writes nothing. `replay.mutate({ slug, runId, confirmUnverified? })`. A fire and a replay refresh the triggers and every run list. A dry run refreshes nothing. |
| `useTriggerRuns(projectId, slug, { enabled?, refetchInterval?, limit? }?)`, `useProjectTriggerRuns(projectId, { status? }?, { enabled?, refetchInterval? }?)` | One trigger's runs, and every run of the project, as an infinite query paged by `next_cursor`. Read `data.pages[].runs`, and call `fetchNextPage()` while `hasNextPage` is true. No polling by default; pass `refetchInterval` (ms) to poll while a run is queued or running. `limit` (1 to 100, server default 25) sets the runs per page of `useTriggerRuns`; a set limit is its own cache entry. |
| `useProjectTriggerEventTypes(projectId, connector)` | The connector events a connector can trigger on. Idle until `connector` is set. |
| `useProjectReminders(projectId)`, `useSessionReminders(projectId, sessionId)` | Reminders, with `update` and `remove`. The project hook adds `updateMany` and `removeMany`. |
| `useChangeRequests(projectId, status?)` | Change requests, with `open`, `merge`, `close` and `requestChanges`. |
| `useProjectApps(projectId)` | Apps, with `create`, `update`, `start`, `stop` and `remove`. |
| `useAppDeployments`, `useAppDeployment` | Deployments with `deploy` and `rollback`, and one deployment with its build log. |
| `useAppMachines(projectId, appId)`, `useAppVolumes(projectId, appId)`, `useAppVolumeSnapshots(projectId, appId, volumeId)`, `useAppAccess` | Machines with `start`, `stop`, `restart`, `destroy` and `scale` (polled every 5 s). Volumes with `snapshot`, `restore`, `fork`, `extend` and `destroy`. A volume's snapshots, newest first. `useAppAccess` returns `{ policy, session, update }`. See [Apps](/docs/#build-on-apps-from-typescript). |
| `useKortixAppViewer()` | Inside an App: the person who is looking, as `{ status: 'loading' \| 'anonymous' \| 'viewer', viewer }`. |
| `useConnectorQuery(projectId, slug, action, args, { account, enabled, staleTime })` | One connector action's output as a cached query. Use it only for actions that read: every fetch runs the action. See [Connectors](/docs/#connectors). |
| `useConnectorSetup(token, { backendUrl, storage, openPopup, onOpened })` | The flow behind a connect link (`/connect/<token>`): `phase` (`loading`, `ready`, `starting`, `opened`, `connected`, `error`), `info`, `error`, `connectedAs` and `connect()`. You supply the popup opener. |
| `useNotificationInbox({ userId, limit, enabled })` | The caller's newest notifications (`limit` 1 to 50, default 20), `unreadCount`, `markRead(ids)`, `markAllRead()` and `markSessionRead(sessionId)`. Polls every 60 s while the page is visible. |
| `useNotificationPreferences({ userId, enabled })` | Push and email choices per kind, and `update(patch)`. |
| `useSessionWatch({ userId, projectId, sessionId, enabled })` | Whether the caller follows one session, and `setWatching(boolean)`. |
| `useFeatureFlag(projectId, key)` | `{ enabled, isLoading }`. `enabled` is `true` only when the server says so. Loading and errors read `false`. |
| `useRuntimeSupports(capability)` | Whether the open session's runtime serves a capability such as `session.rewind` or `session.compact`. `true` until the runtime first answers. See [Runtime capabilities](/docs/#runtime-capabilities). |
| `useCan(target, action)` | `{ allowed, reason, isLoading, isError }` for one action. `target` is `{ accountId }`, `{ accountId, resource }` or `{ projectId }`. Loading and errors read `allowed: false`. |
| `useCans(target, actions)` | One request for several actions on one target. Returns a map keyed by action. Keep `actions` stable across renders. |
| `usePermissionsFor(accountId, memberUserId, probes)` | What another member can do. Needs `member.read` on the account. |
| `useGatewayOverview`, `useGatewaySeries`, `useGatewayBreakdown`, `useGatewaySessions`, `useGatewayErrors` | LLM gateway usage for a project over `days` (default 30). |
| `useGatewayLogs`, `useGatewayLog` | Gateway request logs, paged, and one log entry. |
| `useGatewayBudgets`, `useSetGatewayBudget`, `useDeleteGatewayBudget` | Gateway budgets. |
| `useGatewayKeys`, `useCreateGatewayKey`, `useRevokeGatewayKey` | Gateway keys. |
| `useGatewayRoutingPolicy(projectId)` | The project's model routing policy. |

Rules that apply to several hooks:

- `useCan` and its siblings decide what to show and enable. The API checks every write again. Ask for the action the route checks, for example `PROJECT_ACTIONS.PROJECT_GITOPS_PUSH` (`project.gitops.push`), never for a role. After a write that changes access, call `invalidatePermissionProbes(queryClient, { accountId, userId })`. A verdict is cached for 5 minutes until then. Action names are in [Accounts & access](/docs/#teams).
- The three notification hooks key their cache by `userId`. Pass the signed-in person's id. Gate each with `enabled: useFeatureFlag(projectId, 'notification_center').enabled`. The flag is off by default. See [Notifications](/docs/#computers).
- After a runtime restart or a config reload, call `resetRuntimeQueries(queryClient)`. The next read then comes from the new runtime.

### Fast opens and offline

#### Start the open read early

`prefetchSessionOpen(queryClient, projectId, sessionId)` starts the read that a session view needs before the view mounts. It sends one `GET …/snapshot?transcript=0` request: the session row, the current turn and the prompt queue. It asks for no v1 transcript window: the open reads the session log. `useSession` then uses that request instead of its own.

```tsx
import { prefetchSessionOpen } from '@kortix/sdk/react';
import { useQueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';

function SessionLink({ projectId, sessionId }: { projectId: string; sessionId: string }) {
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();
```
