  <Card icon="atom" title="React" href="/docs/sdk/react">The `useSession` hook and the data hooks the chat reads.</Card>
  <Card icon="app-window" title="Apps" href="/docs/apps#build-an-internal-tool">Build an internal tool as a Kortix App.</Card>
</CardGroup>

## Generative UI

The agent answers in markdown. When a project turns on **Generative UI**, the agent may add
` ```openui ` blocks for structured content: a ranked recommendation, a comparison, a chart from
tool data, places on a map. Short answers, explanations, and code stay prose. A guide, a trip, or a
shortlist reads as short paragraphs with one block per section.

### How the agent learns the blocks

The catalog ships as the `kortix-genui` system skill, not as system-prompt text. With the `genui`
[project flag](/docs) on, every new session lists the skill by name and a
one-line description. The agent loads the full catalog only when an answer needs a block, so a turn
without UI pays nothing for it. With the flag off, the skill is hidden. An agent with an explicit
`skills:` list in `kortix.yaml` sees the skill only when the list names `kortix-genui`.

### Install

Generative UI uses two optional peer dependencies. Install them before you import any
`@kortix/sdk/genui` entry; without them the import fails:

```bash
npm i zod @openuidev/lang-core@0.3.1
```

`@openuidev/lang-core` sends one pseudonymous PostHog event at install time. Set
`OPENUI_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1` or `DO_NOT_TRACK=1` to turn it off. Runtime telemetry is off unless
you opt in.

| Import | Holds | Loads the peers |
|---|---|---|
| `@kortix/sdk/genui/fence` | `genuiVersionFromClassName`, `genuiVersionOf`, `splitGenui`: fence detection | No |
| `@kortix/sdk/genui` | `parseGenui`, `genuiToMarkdown`, `genuiBlockToMarkdown`, the catalog | Yes |
| `@kortix/sdk/genui/react` | `GenuiBlock`, `defaultGenuiComponents` | Yes |

The root `@kortix/sdk` and `@kortix/sdk/react` entries never load the peers. Check a fence with
`@kortix/sdk/genui/fence` in your main bundle, and load the renderer lazily once a block exists.

### Render blocks

`@kortix/sdk/genui/react` exports `GenuiBlock`, a headless renderer. You pass a component for each
block name, or `defaultGenuiComponents` for unbranded HTML defaults.

| Prop | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `code` | The fence body so far |
| `version` | `genuiVersionFromClassName(className)` or `genuiVersionOf(tag)` |
| `streaming` | This block's fence is still open while the message streams. Pass `false` once the fence closes, so a finished block settles while the reply goes on |
| `components` | Block name → your component (`GenuiComponentProps`) |
| `renderMarkdown` | Your markdown renderer, used for fallbacks. Pass a stable function (module constant or `useCallback`): a new function re-renders the block's markdown parts and node views |
| `renderPending` | A node the model has not finished, while the block streams. Default: nothing. Return a fixed-height box for heavy nodes (a chart, a table) so the layout does not jump when the node settles |
| `enabled` | `false` renders the block as markdown (a viewer setting) |
| `onSettled` | One event per block: outcome, component names, timing. Never content |

A Checklist keeps its ticks in the component only: nothing is stored or sent.

### Plain text hosts

`genuiToMarkdown(text)` replaces every block with deterministic markdown. Charts become tables with a
`Source:` line; maps become place lists; tabs and accordions expand.
Blocks inside blockquotes and list items convert too. For a reply that is still streaming, pass
`genuiToMarkdown(text, { streaming: true })`: the unfinished last block shows no "cut off" note.
Steps become a numbered list; a Checklist becomes a task list (`- [ ]`).

A host on an older `@kortix/sdk` does not know a block added later (Steps and Checklist arrived after
v1). It shows *This content could not be displayed.* in that block's place, not the block's markdown.
Update the SDK to render or convert new blocks.

### Blocks

Stack, Card, Stat, StatRow, Table, Compare, RankedList, BarChart, LineChart, PieChart, Map, Steps,
Checklist, Tabs, Accordion, Badge, Callout, Image, Link. Limits (counts, lengths) are enforced by the SDK; a block that
breaks a limit is dropped and its siblings still render.

### Safety

Blocks hold data, never code. Every link and image URL must be absolute `http` or `https`. Generated
UI cannot run tools or change state.

## SDK reference

This section lists the public surface of `@kortix/sdk`: each entry point, each member of the client, each method of the project and session handles, and the framework-free modules. It is for developers who already have a client and need the exact method name and arguments. The client table gives the route prefix of each member. To install the package and run a first session, start at [SDK](/docs/#sdk).

```ts
import { createKortix } from '@kortix/sdk';

const kortix = createKortix({ backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1', getToken });

const p = kortix.project(projectId);           // every project resource, id bound
const s = kortix.session(projectId, sessionId); // one session, both ids bound
await p.secrets.list();
await s.send('Run the tests.');
```

Each client method calls the Kortix API. Session runtime methods reach the session's sandbox through the API at `{backendUrl}/p/…`. Route paths in this section are relative to `backendUrl`, which ends in `/v1`. Each route is in the [REST reference](https://api.kortix.com/v1/docs). A failed call throws an `ApiError` with `status`, `code` and `details`.

### Entry points and stability

| Import | Status | What it holds |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `@kortix/sdk` | Stable | The client and every framework-free module. It never imports React or `node:*`. It runs in browsers, Node 18 or later, Bun and edge runtimes. |
| `@kortix/sdk/react` | Stable | The React hooks. It is the only entry that imports React. See [React](/docs/#react). |
| `@kortix/sdk/server` | Stable | Server helpers for Node and Bun (table below). It imports `node:async_hooks`. Never bundle it for a browser. |
| `@kortix/sdk/wire-message-id` | Stable | `mintWireMessageId` and `WIRE_MESSAGE_ID` in one module with no imports. The root exports the same names. |
| `@kortix/sdk/workspace-search` | Stable | `createWorkspaceSearchClient(fileClient)`: ranked file and folder search over a session workspace, with a cached index. `fileClient` has `listFiles(path)` and `findFiles(query, options?)`. |
| `@kortix/sdk/message-queue` | Deprecated | Client-side prompt queue reducers. Kortix orders prompts on the server: use `s.send` or `useSession().send`. |
| `@kortix/sdk/turns`, `/files`, `/session`, `/session/url`, `/projects-client`, `/platform-client`, `/api-client`, `/auth`, `/config`, `/event-stream`, `/opencode-client`, `/opencode-errors`, `/feature-flags`, `/fresh-sessions`, `/instance-routes` | Deprecated | Aliases. Each re-exports names that the root already exports. Import from the root. |
| `@kortix/sdk/sync-store`, `/server-store`, `/sandbox-connection-store`, `/opencode-pending-store`, `/idb-sync-cache` | Deprecated | Aliases of the internal web-app stores. They have no replacement. Read session state with `useSession` from `@kortix/sdk/react`. |
| `@kortix/sdk/internal/*` | Internal | State stores for the Kortix web app. Outside semver. Do not import. |

Deprecated subpaths and names resolve until the next major version. The package also ships two browser bundles, `dist/kortix.esm.min.js` and `dist/kortix.global.js` (`window.Kortix`). See [Install](/docs/#install-2).

#### `@kortix/sdk/server`

| Export | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `createScopedKortix(config)` | The same client as `createKortix`, but every call, including calls on `project()` and `session()` handles, runs with this config only. Create one per request in a server that acts for many people. |
| `runWithKortix(config, fn)` · `getScopedConfig()` | Run `fn` with a request-scoped config (Node `AsyncLocalStorage`) · read the config in scope. |
| `forwardKortixRequest(options)` | Forward one same-origin request to the Kortix API with an upstream token. It strips host credentials and keeps a streaming response body. |
| `createKortixAuth(options)` | Sign in with Kortix for your own app: OAuth 2.1 with PKCE, an encrypted session cookie, refresh, sign-out, `/me` and a same-origin `/proxy`. Register the client with `kortix.iam.oauthClients.create`. See [Sign in with Kortix](/docs/#sign-in-with-kortix). |
| `readAppViewer(request, options?)` | Inside a Kortix App: verify the signed `x-kortix-app-viewer` header with `KORTIX_APP_VIEWER_SECRET` and return the viewer, or `null`. |
| `createAppViewerKortix(request, options)` | Inside a Kortix App: a client that calls the Kortix API as the viewer. |
| `createKortixAppGuard(options)` | One viewer resolver for an App behind the Kortix gate or set to `public`. `requireViewer` and `requireGroup` return a viewer or a `Response`. |
| `KortixAuthError`, `AppViewerUnavailableError`, `safeReturnTo`, `APP_VIEWER_HEADER`, `APP_VIEWER_TOKEN_HEADER`, `APP_VIEWER_SECRET_ENV`, `KORTIX_SESSION_SENTINEL` | Errors, a return-URL guard and the header and environment variable names. |

App viewers are described in [Know who is looking](/docs/#know-who-is-looking).

### The client

`createKortix(config)` returns one client. A second call with a different `backendUrl` or `getToken` re-points every earlier client and logs one warning. The options are in [Create the client](/docs/#create-the-client).

| Member | What it reaches | Routes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `config` | The config in effect. | none |
| `auth` | Headless sign-up, sign-in, magic link, OTP, OAuth, SSO, MFA, refresh, sign-out. | `/auth/…` |
| `accounts` | Accounts, members, invites, API keys, connected apps, account secrets, branding, the audit log. | `/accounts/…` |
| `iam` | Assignments, roles, permissions, groups, agent identities, OAuth clients, permission probes. | `/accounts/{accountId}/iam/…` |
| `accountInvites` | The invitee's side of an account invite. | `/account-invites/…` |
| `projects` | Project list, create, provision and model access. | `/projects/…` |
| `project(projectId)` | The [project handle](#the-project-handle). | `/projects/{projectId}/…` |
| `session(projectId, sessionId)` | The [session handle](#the-session-handle). | `/projects/{projectId}/sessions/{sessionId}/…` |
| `connectors` · `connector(slug)` | Connector calls with a token that carries its project, such as a session token. Same methods as `p.connectors` (data plane) and `p.connector(slug)`. | `/connectors/…` |
| `github` | GitHub App installations and repository links, per account. | `/projects/github/…` |
| `gitBackend` | The instance git backend that holds Kortix-managed repositories. | `/projects/git/backend` |
| `billing` | Plan, credit balance, transactions, usage, subscription. | `/billing/…`, `/usage/…` |
| `notifications` | The caller's inbox, preferences, Web Push and device tokens. | `/notifications/…` |
| `capture` | Kortix Capture: one account's recorded screen, actions and audio, and the device sign-in approval. | `/accounts/{accountId}/capture/…`, `/capture/…` |
| `marketplace` | The public marketplace catalog and its sources. | `/marketplace/…` |
| `sandboxShares` | Public share links for one sandbox port. | `/p/share` |
| `connectStatus()` | Whether the managed OAuth connect provider is configured on this instance. | `/connectors/connect-status` |
| `mobileClientPolicy()` | `{ minimum_version, store_url_ios, store_url_android }`. `null` means the operator set no value. Needs no token. | `/clients/mobile` |
| `validateToken()` | Checks a pasted API key. Never throws. | `/accounts/me` |
| `runtime()` | The runtime client of the process-wide active sandbox. Throws on a scoped client. Use `s.messages()` and the other session methods instead. | sandbox |

#### `kortix.projects`

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `list()` · `listForAccount(accountId)` | Projects you can open · projects in one account. |
| `get(projectId)` · `detail(projectId)` | Summary · full detail with config. |
| `provision(input)` | New project on a new Kortix-managed repository: `{ name, account_id?, seed_starter?, starter_template?: 'general-knowledge-worker' \| 'minimal', marketplace_items?, source_item_id?, default_branch?, icon?, icon_glyph?, idempotency_key? }`. |
| `create(input)` · `createRepo(input)` | From an existing `repo_url` · on a new GitHub repository. |
| `update(projectId, input)` · `archive(projectId)` | Change settings · archive. |
| `llmCatalog(projectId)` · `modelPicker(projectId)` | Full model catalog · the compact list for a model picker. |
| `modelAccess(projectId)` · `setModelAccess(projectId, { target: 'provider' \| 'model', id, enabled })` | Read · turn on or off one provider or model. Provider id `kortix` is the Kortix-managed models. Disabling the current default returns `409 cannot_disable_default`. |
| `sandboxHealth(projectId)` · `sandboxTemplates(projectId)` | Sandbox build health · build templates. |
| `sessions(projectId)` · `createSession(projectId, input?)` | Same as `p.sessions.list()` · `p.sessions.create()`. |

Send `idempotency_key` (1 to 200 characters of `A–Z a–z 0–9 . _ : -`, one UUID per create) when a retry is possible. A repeat with the same key returns the first project (`201`, same `project_id`). A repeat while the first call still runs returns `409 provision_in_flight`: retry with the same key. The key identifies the attempt, not the payload.

#### `kortix.github` and `kortix.gitBackend`

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `github.getInstallation(accountId)` · `github.listInstallations(accountId)` | The account's GitHub App installation · installations the user can reach. |
| `github.listLinkableInstallations(input)` · `github.linkInstallation(input)` · `github.saveInstallation(input)` · `github.deleteInstallation(accountId, installationId?)` | Installations the GitHub user may link · link · record · unlink one. |
| `github.listRepositories(accountId, installationId?)` · `github.listRepositoryBranches(…)` | Repositories the installation sees · their branches and default branch. |
| `github.linkRepository(input)` | Import a repository as a project. |
| `github.replaceProjectRepository(input)` | Point a project at another repository. Send `expected_repo_url`: a stale value returns `409`. New sessions use the new repository. Running sessions keep their checkout. |
| `github.storeUserToken(input)` | Store the user's GitHub authorization, needed to create a repository in a personal GitHub account. |
| `gitBackend.get()` | `{ configured, kind: 'app' \| 'pat' \| null, owner }`. Any signed-in user can read it. |
| `gitBackend.repositories({ search?, limit? })` | Repositories under that owner. Self-host operators only; others get `403`. |

Repositories, branches and change requests are explained in [Git](/docs/#git).

#### `kortix.marketplace` and `kortix.sandboxShares`

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `marketplace.items(options?)` · `marketplace.item(id)` · `marketplace.itemFile(id, path)` | Browse the catalog · one item · one file of an item. |
| `marketplace.marketplaces()` · `marketplace.featured()` | All marketplaces · featured marketplaces. |
| `marketplace.sources.list()` · `.add(input)` · `.remove(id)` | Marketplace sources. |
| `sandboxShares.list(sandboxId)` · `.create({ sandboxId, port, ttl?, label? })` · `.revoke(sandboxId, token)` | Public share links for one sandbox port. |

To install an item into a project, see [Install from the marketplace](/docs).

### Accounts, IAM, billing and notifications

#### `kortix.auth`

Methods: `signUp`, `signInWithPassword`, `sendMagicLink`, `verifyOtp`, `signInWithProvider`, `exchangeCode`, `refresh`, `resetPassword`, `updatePassword`, `updateUserMetadata`, `signInWithSso`, `mfa.enroll`, `mfa.challenge`, `mfa.verify`, `mfa.unenroll`, `user`, `signOut`.

`session(options)` returns a session store. It saves the tokens through a storage adapter you supply. It refreshes them 60 seconds before they expire. Pass its `getToken` to `createKortix`. See [Headless sign-in](/docs/#headless-sign-in).

#### `kortix.accounts`

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `list()` · `get(accountId)` · `create({ name })` · `updateName(accountId, name)` · `leave(accountId)` | Accounts you belong to, and their lifecycle. |
| `members(accountId)` · `invite(accountId, input)` · `updateMemberRole(accountId, userId, role)` · `removeMember(accountId, userId)` | Members and their account role. |
| `invites(accountId)` · `cancelInvite(accountId, inviteId)` · `resendInvite(accountId, inviteId)` | Pending invites. |
| `tokens.list(accountId?, { mine? })` · `tokens.create({ accountId?, name, expiresAt?, projectId? })` · `tokens.revoke(tokenId, accountId?)` | Personal access keys (`kortix_pat_…`). `mine: true` lists only yours. `projectId` binds the key to one project. |
| `connectedApps.list()` · `connectedApps.revoke(…)` | OAuth and MCP clients this person approved, across all accounts. Route `/oauth/grants`. |
| `secretResources.list` · `.create` · `.rotate` · `.retry` · `.remove` · `.grant` · `.revoke` · `.setAccess` | Account-level provider secrets and which projects may use them. |
| `branding.get(accountId)` · `.update(accountId, { app_name })` · `.uploadAsset(accountId, kind, file)` · `.removeAsset(accountId, kind)` · `.reset(accountId)` | Organization branding. `kind`: `logo`, `icon`, `favicon`, or `logo_dark`, `icon_dark`, `favicon_dark`. |
| `audit.log(accountId, filters?)` · `audit.export(accountId, { format: 'csv' \| 'jsonl', …filters })` | The audit log, filtered by project, session, actor, source, outcome, request, resource, action or time · the same events as a file. |
| `audit.activity(accountId, { since?, until?, projectId?, actor?, client?, actorType?, credentialKind? })` | Who was active and how often, per UTC day: per user, the active days, distinct active hours and events. `client: 'browser'` keeps only a person in a web page. |
| `audit.webhooks.list` · `.create` · `.update` · `.remove` | Signed webhooks that stream audit events to a SIEM. |

Account features are explained in [Accounts & access](/docs/#teams), keys in [API keys and tokens](/docs/#security).

#### `kortix.iam`

Kortix has one grant record, the assignment: one principal (`user`, `group`, `service_account`, `pending` or `project`), one role, one scope (`account` or one `project`), an optional object (`agent`, `skill`, `secret`, `app`, `trigger` or `connection`) and an optional `expiresAt`.

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `assignments.list(accountId, filter?)` | Live assignments. Filter by `principalType` with `principalId`, `scopeType`, `scopeId`, `objectType`, `objectId`, `roleId`, `includeExpired`. |
| `assignments.create(accountId, { principal, scope, object?, expiresAt?, roleId \| roleKey })` · `assignments.revoke(accountId, assignmentId)` | Grant · revoke. `roleKey` names a built-in role: `owner`, `admin`, `member`, `manager`, `agent-user`. |
| `permissions.list(accountId)` · `permissions.forRole(…)` | The permission catalog as data (`action`, `scope_type`, `resource_type`, `delegable`, `description`, `area`, `level`, `implies`) · one role's permissions. |
| `roles.list` · `.create` · `.update` · `.setPermissions` · `.remove` · `.usage` | Custom roles. Without the account's `rbac` entitlement the API returns `402 entitlement_required`. |
| `groups.list` · `.get` · `.create` · `.update` · `.remove` · `groups.members.list` · `.add` · `.remove` | Groups and their members. |
| `agentIdentities(accountId)` | Agent principals, for binding a role to an agent. |
| `oauthClients.list` · `.get` · `.create` · `.update` · `.rotateSecret` · `.remove` | Sign in with Kortix clients. The secret is returned once, on create and on rotate. |
| `can(accountId, userId, { action, resourceType?, resourceId? })` · `canBatch(accountId, userId, probes)` | Ask whether a principal may do one action · many, in one round trip, answers in input order. |

Gate a control in your UI with `can` or `canBatch` on the action the route checks, not on a role name. `PROJECT_ACTIONS` (root export) names every project action the API answers about, for example `PROJECT_ACTIONS.PROJECT_GITOPS_MERGE` is `project.gitops.merge`. The API answers an unknown action with `400`. For triggers, probe `TRIGGER_CONTROL_ACTIONS` and pass the result to `triggerControlsFrom(cans)`: it returns `{ canCreate, canFire, canUpdate, canDelete }`, one flag per trigger route, each `false` until the probe allows it. Create, update and delete also need `project.write`, because their routes load the project for write first.

For change requests and the review inbox, probe `REVIEW_CONTROL_ACTIONS`. `changeRequestControlsFrom(cans)` returns `{ canOpen, canMerge, canClose }`: open needs `project.write` + `project.gitops.push`, merge needs `project.write` + `project.gitops.merge`, close and reopen need `project.write`. `reviewVerdictAllowed(item, verdict, cans)` says whether to offer one verdict on one review item. Approving a change request merges it, so it follows `canMerge`. Every other verdict on a change request or a native item needs `project.write` + `project.review.act`. A connector call offers approve and reject to the viewer the API lets decide it: a project manager, or the person who launched the session when a person started it (not an API token or a service account). The API says so per call: `can_resolve` on each `SessionAuditAction` (`getSessionAudit`, and the session snapshot's `audit` leg), and `canResolve` on a connector call's review action (`mapApiReviewItem`). Show Approve and Deny only when it is not `false`. A viewer the API would refuse gets the review item with status `waiting`. `resolvers` (both shapes) says who decides instead: `managers` for a session started with an API token or a service account, else `managers_and_launcher`.

#### `kortix.accountInvites`

`listMine()` lists pending invites addressed to your email. `describe(inviteId)`, `accept(inviteId)` and `decline(inviteId)` act on one invite. They need no account membership.

`acceptAndLand(inviteId, invite?)` accepts and returns `{ accepted, landing }`. `landing` is where to open: `{ kind: 'project', projectId, accountId }` for the first project the invite granted, or `{ kind: 'account', accountId }` for a workspace invite. Pass the invite you already read (from `listMine()` or `describe()`): an accept that grants nothing, such as an admin invite, then lands in the first project the invite names. `accountInviteLanding(accepted, invite?)` is the same rule without the request, for an invite accepted earlier.

#### `kortix.billing`

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `accountState(accountId?)` · `accountStateMinimal(accountId?)` | Plan, credit balance and limits · the short form. |
| `creditBreakdown(accountId?)` · `transactions(params?)` · `transactionsSummary(params?)` | Credit balance by bucket · transaction history · totals. |
| `usageHistory(params?)` · `usageRollup(params?)` | Usage over time · usage grouped by model, provider or day. |
| `sessionCosts.list({ accountId?, projectId?, limit?, offset? })` · `sessionCosts.get(sessionId, options?)` | Usage records per session · one session's model and compute ledger. |
| `tierConfigurations()` | The named amounts (Starter, Pro, Scale) and the minimum and maximum monthly amount. |
| `setPlan({ monthlyUsd, successUrl, cancelUrl, locale?, accountId? })` | Subscribe to, or change, the credits plan. `monthlyUsd` is a whole dollar from 20 to 10,000; one credit is one cent. It resolves to `{ status: 'checkout_created', checkout_url }` when the account has no paid subscription (redirect there), `{ status: 'updated' \| 'unchanged' }` when an existing subscription changed in place, or `{ status: 'payment_pending', client_secret }` when a raise needs payment confirmation. A raise is charged now and grants the prorated credits at once; a cut applies at the next renewal. React: `useSetBillingPlan()` from `@kortix/sdk/react`. |
| `subscription.createPortalSession(returnUrl, accountId?)` · `.cancel(feedback?, accountId?)` · `.reactivate(accountId?)` | Manage the subscription. `cancelScheduledChange` and `prorationPreview` are retired and reject with `ENDPOINT_RETIRED`. |
| `credits.purchase(input)` · `credits.autoTopupSettings(accountId?)` · `credits.configureAutoTopup(input)` | Buy credits · read and set auto top-up. |

Before you render a purchase control, ask `canOfferTopup(state)` (root export) about the account state: `true` only when the plan may buy credits and the caller may change billing. `canManageBilling(state)` answers the second half alone. Both return `false` while `state` is `undefined` or `null`, so a member never sees a control the API answers with `403`.

Plans and credits are explained in [Plans & credits](/docs/#billing).

#### `kortix.notifications`

Every method needs a person's credential. A service account gets `403`.

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `list({ limit?, before? }, { signal? })` | Newest rows first: `{ notifications, unread_count, next_before }`. `limit` is 1 to 50, default 20. Pass `next_before` as `before` for the next page. |
| `markRead({ ids })` · `markRead({ all: true })` · `markRead({ sessionId })` | Mark 1 to 100 rows, all rows, or one session's rows read. Returns `{ updated, unread_count }`. |
| `preferences()` · `updatePreferences({ kinds })` | Push and email per kind: `{ kinds, email_available }`. A patch changes only the kinds it names. |
| `webPushPublicKey()` · `registerWebPushSubscription({ endpoint, keys })` · `unregisterWebPushSubscription(endpoint)` | Web Push for this browser. Registration needs a browser sign-in: a personal access key gets `403`. |
| `registerDeviceToken(input)` · `unregisterDeviceToken(token)` · `releaseDeviceToken(token, { backendUrl? })` | A native app's push token. `releaseDeviceToken` needs no credential: a signed-out app removes its token. |

Kinds (`INBOX_NOTIFICATION_KINDS`): `turn_done`, `turn_error`, `question`, `permission`, `shared`, `automation_failed`, `automation_recovered`. Defaults are in `DEFAULT_NOTIFICATION_PREFERENCES`. The inbox writes rows only for projects with the `notification_center` feature flag on. The flag is off by default. While it is off, `s.watch()` and `s.setWatch()` return `403 feature_disabled`. The session handle adds `watch()`, `setWatch(watching)` and `presence(…)`. See [Notifications](/docs/#computers).

#### `kortix.capture`

Capture belongs to the account, not to a project. `kortix.capture.account(accountId)` binds one account. Every account call answers `403 capture_disabled` while Capture is off for the account. Each member reads their own devices and timeline. Reading another member needs the `capture.read_all` permission and writes a `capture.member_view` audit event. Account owners and admins have it by default. See [Capture](/docs/capture).

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `account(id).workspace.get()` · `.workspace.setEnabled(on)` | `{ account_id, enabled, can_read_all, can_manage, updated_at }` · turn Capture on or off (`capture.manage`). |
| `account(id).devices.list({ userId?, scope? })` · `.devices.revoke(deviceId)` · `.devices.assetUrl(deviceId, name)` | Devices with live status (`recording`, `paused`, `permission_missing`, `not_recording`, or `offline` after 120 s without a status). `scope: 'account'` lists every member's devices. · Revoke a device's token. · A 5-minute signed URL of an action screenshot. |
| `account(id).policy.get()` · `.policy.set(policy)` | The account's capture policy: layers, privacy, retention, recording, notice. A write needs `capture.manage`. |
| `account(id).timeline.get(query?)` · `.timeline.items(query?)` · `.timeline.days(query?)` | App and window runs for a UTC day or a window up to 31 days · frames, actions and audio in a window, 500 of each · the days with recorded items, newest first. |
| `account(id).search({ q, kinds?, app?, from?, to?, limit?, userId?, scope? })` | Full-text search over on-screen text, actions and audio, newest first. |
| `account(id).frame(frameId, { userId? })` · `.media(chunkId, { userId? })` | One frame with its on-screen text and a 5-minute signed URL of its video · signed URLs of an item's video and audio. |
| `deviceGrant(userCode)` · `approveDevice(userCode, accountId?)` · `denyDevice(userCode)` | The person's half of the device sign-in. |
| `me.search(query)` · `me.timeline(query)` · `me.frame(frameId)` | Reads for the person the token acts for, in the token's account. A credential that acts for no person gets `403 capture_no_human`. |

React (`@kortix/sdk/react`): `useCaptureWorkspace`, `useSetCaptureEnabled`, `useCaptureDevices` (polls every 10 s), `useRevokeCaptureDevice`, `useCapturePolicy`, `useSetCapturePolicy`, `useCaptureDays`, `useCaptureTimeline`, `useCaptureTimelineItems`, `useCaptureSearch`, `useCaptureChunkMedia`, `useCaptureDeviceGrant`, `useApproveCaptureDevice`, `useDenyCaptureDevice`. Each account hook takes the account id first. A `null` id sends no request.

### The project handle

`kortix.project(projectId)` returns `p`, with the project id bound to every method. Creating the handle sends no request.

#### Project

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `get()` · `detail()` · `update(input)` · `archive()` | Read · full detail with config · change settings · archive. |
| `llmCatalog()` · `modelPicker()` · `modelAccess()` · `setModelAccess(change)` · `sandboxHealth()` | As on `kortix.projects`, project id bound. |
| `validateManifest(raw)` | Validate `kortix.yaml` text on the server, with the schema a merge uses. |
| `audit(options?)` | The project's audit timeline. |
| `skills.create(input)` | Write a skill file to the default branch. |
| `attachments.upload(…)` · `.delete(…)` · `.createController(options?)` | Prompt attachments, and a controller that tracks uploads for a composer. |
| `onboardingComplete(…)` | Mark project onboarding done. |
| `connector(slug)` · `session(sessionId)` | One connector handle · the session handle. |

#### Sessions

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `list(options?)` | One page of sessions as an array. |
| `listPage(options?)` | One page plus `next_cursor`. A cursor is valid only for the filters that issued it. |
| `create(input?)` | Create a session. The sandbox starts in the background. See [Create a session](/docs/#create-a-session). |
| `ensureWarm()` | Pre-create the session a present user is about to start. Ignore failures. |

| Option | Values |
| --- | --- |
| `scope` | `visible` (default) or `project`: the manager inventory with unavailable, warm and deleted sessions. |
| `limit` · `cursor` | 1 to 200 rows · the `next_cursor` of the previous page. |
| `parent` | `root` (top-level sessions with `child_count`) or a session id (its children). |
| `startedBy` | `me`, `others` or `automated`. |
| `q` | Search over title, starter label, trigger slug and id prefix. Up to 200 characters. |
| `labels` | Sessions that carry every label. |

Each row carries `parent_session_id` and `initiator: { type: 'member' | 'trigger' | 'channel' | 'api' | 'system', id, label }`. `sessionParentId(session)` reads the parent.

#### Files, git and change requests

These methods read the project repository. To read and write files in a running session, use `s.files`.

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `files.listDirectory({ ref?, path?, cursor?, limit? })` | One folder level: `{ entries, truncated, next_cursor }`. Use it to build a file tree. Without `cursor` or `limit`, one response carries up to 5,000 entries. With either, the response is a page of `limit` entries (default 500, at most 2,000). Pass `next_cursor` as `cursor` for the next page while it is a string: stop when it is `null` or absent (an API older than paging omits it). Every page reads the commit the first page read. |
| `files.list(options?)` | Every file under a path, recursively, cut at 1,000. A cut response carries `X-Kortix-Truncated: 1`. |
| `files.read(path, ref?)` · `files.history(path, { ref?, limit?, skip? })` | A file at a ref · its commits. |
| `files.search(query, { content?, ref?, limit? })` | Search file names, or contents with `content: true`. A content match carries `line_number` and `line_text`. |
| `files.archive(options?)` · `files.archiveRequest(options?)` | Download a tarball · the same as a request your host streams to disk. |
| `git.commits()` · `git.commit(sha)` · `git.commitDiff(sha)` | The log · one commit · its diff. |
| `git.branches()` · `git.versionDiff(…)` | Branches · the diff between two refs. |
| `git.inviteCollaborator(…)` | Invite a GitHub user to a Kortix-managed repository. |
| `gitToken()` | A fresh scoped push token for a Kortix-managed repository. A bring-your-own repository returns `409`. |
| `changeRequests.list(status?, { limit?, cursor? })` · `.get(crId)` · `.diff(crId)` · `.mergePreview(crId)` | Read change requests. `list` returns one page, newest number first: 50 by default, at most 500. Pass `next_cursor` back as `cursor`; it is `null` on the last page. |
| `changeRequests.open(input)` · `.update(crId, input)` · `.merge(crId, input?)` · `.close(crId, input?)` · `.reopen(crId, input?)` | Change request lifecycle. |
| `changeRequests.requestChanges(crId, input)` | Record feedback and, optionally, send it to the session that made the change. |

Read a large folder page by page:

```ts
let cursor: string | undefined;
do {
  const page = await p.files.listDirectory({ path: 'src', limit: 500, cursor });
  render(page.entries);
  cursor = page.next_cursor ?? undefined; // null or absent ends the loop
} while (cursor);
```

Routes: `/projects/{projectId}/files`, `/commits`, `/branches`, `/version-diff`, `/change-requests`. See [Git](/docs/#git).

#### Review and approvals

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `review.list(params?)` · `.get(id)` · `.submit(input)` · `.act(id, …)` · `.bulkAct(input)` | The Review page inbox: change requests, approvals and agent outputs. `list` returns one page (`{ review_items, next_cursor }`, 50 items by default, at most 200): Needs you, then Waiting, then Done. Filter with `segment`, `kind` and `sessionId`; pass `next_cursor` back as `cursor`. A walk is not a snapshot: an item decided between two pages can come back on a later page, so key the rows by `review_item_id`. `get` reads any item by id, including `cr:<id>` and a pending `call:<id>`. `useReviewItemPages(projectId, query)` (`@kortix/sdk/react`) is the same list as an infinite query; read its rows with `reviewItemsOf(query.data)`. |
| `review.summary()` | `{ needs_you_total, needs_you_by_session }`: what waits on the caller, counted on the server. Read it for a badge or a per-session dot, never the list. `useReviewSummary(projectId)` polls it every 60 seconds. |
| `approvals.list(options?)` · `approvals.sessionsNeedingInput(options?)` | Connector calls that wait for a decision · sessions blocked on one or on an open agent question, with a count per session. `useSessionsNeedingInput(projectId)` (`@kortix/sdk/react`) reads the same list every 30 seconds, and again on each question event of an open session. |
| `approvals.resolve(executionId, 'approve' \| 'deny', { note? })` | Decide one call. `note` goes to the agent as an instruction. A decision covers that call only. |

Policies and approvals are explained in [Policies and approvals](/docs/#connectors).

#### Secrets, tokens and setup links

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `secrets.list()` · `secrets.upsert(input)` · `secrets.remove(name)` | Secret metadata · create or update a write-only value with its delivery policy · delete. Each listed secret carries `status` for the shared value and `mine.status` for your own value: `'undecryptable'` when the server can no longer decrypt it. `upsert` a shared value again; `setPersonal` or `removePersonal` your own. |
| `secrets.setStrategy(identifier, 'runtime' \| 'egress' \| 'broker' \| 'denied', options?)` | Set the exposure: `runtime` is environment, `egress` is enforced, `denied` is none. `broker` keeps the value out of the session. |
| `secrets.broker(identifier, request)` | Send one policy-bound HTTPS request with the secret. Needs a session-scoped token. |
| `secrets.setPersonal(name, { value?, active? })` · `secrets.removePersonal(name)` | Your own value for a secret, used in your sessions. `active: false` keeps your value but uses the shared one. `removePersonal` deletes your value. |
| `secrets.setGitCredential(input)` | The credential for a bring-your-own git repository. |
| `secrets.listProviderOAuth()` · `.startProviderOAuth(…)` · `.pollProviderOAuth(…)` · `.removeProviderOAuth(provider)` | Subscription logins for model providers, through a device-code flow. |
| `tokens.list()` · `tokens.create(input?)` · `tokens.revoke(tokenId)` | Project API keys. Kortix also mints one per session as `KORTIX_TOKEN`. |
| `setupLinks.requestSecret(input)` · `setupLinks.requestConnector(input)` | A link a person opens to enter a secret or connect an app, without full project access. A connector link works on `composio` and `pipedream` connectors, and on `mcp` connectors with one-click sign-in. |

Exposure policies and flags are explained in [Secrets](/docs/#security).

#### Connectors and policies

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `connector(slug).run(action, args, options?)` | The action's output. Throws `ConnectorCallError`, or `ConnectorApprovalPendingError` while a call waits for approval. |
| `connector(slug).call` · `.describe(action?)` · `.accounts()` · `.paginate(action, args, { next, maxPages? })` · `.uploadAttachment(content, input)` | The raw result · schemas · callable accounts · a page iterator · stage a file. |
| `connectors.call(tool, args?, { account? })` · `connectors.callAction(slug, action, args, options?)` | Call `<connector>.<action>` · the same with types from `kortix connectors types`. |
| `connectors.catalog(options?)` · `.tools()` · `.search(query)` · `.describe(tool)` · `.accounts(slug)` · `.uploadAttachment(…)` | Find and inspect callable tools and accounts. |
| `connectors.list()` · `.config(slug)` · `.create(input)` · `.remove(slug)` · `.sync()` · `.auth.discover(input)` | Configure connectors. `auth.discover` reads auth from an OpenAPI spec, a Postman collection or an endpoint. |
| `connectors.setName` · `.setSensitive` · `.setCredentialMode` · `.setCredential` · `.deleteCredential` | Connector settings and its stored credential. |
| `connectors.policies.get(slug, { policySet? })` · `connectors.policies.set(slug, policies, { policySet? })` | One connector's base rules, or with `policySet` the rules of that policy set. A rule is `{ match, action, conditions? }`. |
| `connectors.policySets.list(slug)` · `.upsert(slug, name, { description? })` · `.rename(slug, name, newName)` · `.remove(slug, name)` | The connector's [policy sets](/docs/#connectors): list each set with its rules and the agents that use it · create one or set its description · rename it and every agent reference · delete it (`409`, `policy_set_in_use`, while an agent uses it). The root exports `listConnectorPolicySets`, `upsertConnectorPolicySet`, `renameConnectorPolicySet` and `deleteConnectorPolicySet`. |
| `connectors.connections.list()` · `.listAll()` · `.reconcile(…)` · `.reconcileMember(…)` · `.updateCredential(…)` · `.revoke(…)` · `.activate(…)` · `.setDefault(…)` · `.rename(…)` · `.share(…)` · `.addComputer(…)` | Connected accounts: shared or private, defaults, labels, sharing and paired computers. |
| `connectors.pipedream.listApps` · `.listSections` · `.connect` · `.finalize` | Legacy Pipedream connect for OAuth apps. |
| `connectors.listMoves()` · `connectors.move(slug, { connectionId? })` | Move a Pipedream or Composio connector to its Kortix MCP app. It keeps its slug. `listMoves` returns `ConnectorMove[]`. `move` returns `ConnectorMoveResult`: `status: 'sign_in'` with the `connection_id` to sign in on, or `status: 'moved'`. The root exports the same calls as `listConnectorMoves(projectId)` and `moveConnector(projectId, slug, { connectionId? })`. See [Move a Pipedream or Composio connector to Kortix](/docs/#connectors). |
| `connectors.discover.list` · `.sections` · `.detail` · `.tools` · `.install` | The app catalog: every MCP server, REST and GraphQL API and Composio app. `list` and `sections` take `q`, `category`, `kinds` (`mcp`, `composio`, `openapi`, `graphql`) and `oneClick`. `detail(id)` lists the app's ways to connect, best first, the first `recommended`. `tools(id, way?)` lists what one way can do before install. `install({ id, way?, name?, slug? })` adds a named connector profile and returns `next` (`connect`, `credential` or `ready`). |
| `policies.list()` · `policies.set(policies)` | Project policies. |
| `agentIdentities()` · `setAgentScope(agentName, scope)` | The project's agents as principals · bind secrets, connectors and policy sets to one agent. `scope.policy_sets` is `{ <slug>: <name> \| null }`, merged per connector; `null` or `'base'` removes the entry. |

Accounts, the account a call uses, typed calls and errors are explained in [Connectors](/docs/#connectors) and [Call connectors from code](/docs/#connectors).

#### Triggers and reminders

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `triggers.list()` | Every trigger, with parse errors and `triggers_paused`. |
| `triggers.create(input)` · `triggers.update(slug, input)` · `triggers.remove(slug)` | Write a trigger. `type` is `cron`, `webhook`, `event` or `app` (`monitor` is refused with `MONITOR_REMOVED`). `name` and `type` are required. `prompt_template` is required unless `target` (`{ app, path?, method?, payload? }`) calls an App. A schedule takes `cron`, `every` or `run_at`. `type: 'app'` takes `app` and, optionally, `filter: { 'event.name': … }`. With the `project_channels` flag on, `space_id` names the channel or DM its runs post to as threads (`null` clears it): a DM must be the caller's own (`403` otherwise), and an unknown or archived space is `404 space_not_found`. |
| `triggers.fire(slug, { payload?, dryRun? }?)` | Run one trigger now. It works while triggers are paused. `payload` deep-merges over the manual payload at the root, for example `{ body: { … } }`. `dryRun: true` writes nothing and returns `ProjectTriggerDryRunResult`: `rendered_prompt`, `filter_matches`, `session_key` and `payload`. A real fire returns `status` (`fired`, `queued` or `deduped`), `session_id`, `command_id` and `run_id`. |
| `triggers.runs(slug, { cursor?, limit? }?)` · `triggers.projectRuns({ status?, cursor?, limit? }?)` | One trigger's runs · every run of the project, newest first, as `{ runs: ProjectTriggerRun[], next_cursor }`. `limit` is 1–100, default 25. `status` is `queued`, `running`, `fired`, `failed`, `skipped` or `rejected`. A page can hold fewer than `limit` runs while `next_cursor` is set: follow `next_cursor` until it is `null`. |
| `triggers.replay(slug, runId, { confirmUnverified? }?)` | Fire the trigger again with the run's stored payload and the current spec. Returns `409 run_in_progress` while the run is queued or running, `409 payload_unverified` for an unverified webhook payload unless `confirmUnverified: true`, and `422 payload_truncated` for a payload over 64 KiB. |
| `triggers.setActivation(paused)` | Pause or resume every trigger of the project. |
| `triggers.eventApps()` · `triggers.eventTypes({ connector } \| { app, source? })` | Connected apps with connector events (not Kortix Apps) · the event types of one connector or app. |
| `reminders.list()` · `reminders.updateMany(…)` · `reminders.removeMany(…)` | Reminders on every session you can open: list, pause or resume, delete. One session's reminders are `s.reminders`. |

Every field of `create` is in [Triggers & reminders](/docs/#automations).

#### Apps

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `list()` · `get(appId)` · `create(input)` · `update(appId, input)` · `remove(appId)` | App inventory and lifecycle. |
| `artifacts.register(input)` · `artifacts.uploadArchive(…)` · `artifacts.finalize(…)` | Upload a build. |
| `deployments.create(appId, …)` · `.list(appId)` · `.get(…)` · `.logs(…)` · `.remove(…)` | Releases and their logs. `remove` deletes one release that is not live. |
| `rollback(appId, deploymentId)` · `start(appId)` · `stop(appId)` | Move traffic to an earlier release · start · stop. |
| `machines.list(appId)` · `.start` · `.stop` · `.restart` · `.destroy(appId, machineId)` | Machines. Destroying one keeps its volume. |
| `scale(appId, { group: count })` | Machines per process group, 0 to 20. |
| `exec(appId, input)` | Run one command in a machine. Needs `project.app.admin`. Kortix audits each call. |
| `volumes.list(appId)` · `.create` · `.update` · `.destroy(appId, volumeId, confirmName)` · `.snapshots` · `.snapshot` · `.restore(appId, volumeId, snapshotId)` · `.fork` | Persistent volumes and their snapshots. |
| `access.get(appId)` · `access.update(appId, …)` · `access.session(appId)` · `access.agents(appId)` | Who can open the App · an access session · agents granted the App in `kortix.yaml`. |
| `token(appId)` | A 15-minute sign-in token for the App that names the caller. Needs the `member_tokens` capability. |
| `authEnv(appId)` | `KORTIX_AUTH_ISSUER`, `KORTIX_AUTH_AUDIENCE` and `KORTIX_AUTH_JWKS`, to verify App tokens without a network call. |

Apps are generally available in every project. Apps routes still require project membership and the appropriate Apps permission. Routes: `/projects/{projectId}/apps/…`. See [Build on Apps from TypeScript](/docs/#build-on-apps-from-typescript).

| Method | What it does |

See [Sandboxes & previews](/docs/#computers).

#### Settings and feature flags

`p.updateFeatureFlag(key, enabled)` sets one flag for the project with `PATCH /projects/{projectId}/features`. `key` is one of `FEATURE_FLAG_KEYS` (type `FeatureFlagKey`). `enabled: null` clears the override. The caller needs `project.settings.write`. A route behind a flag that is off returns `403 { error, code: 'feature_disabled', feature }`: test it with `isFeatureDisabledError(error)` and read the key with `featureDisabledKey(error)`. See [Feature flags](/docs).

### The session handle

`kortix.session(projectId, sessionId)` (or `p.session(sessionId)`) returns `s`. Creating the handle sends no request. Runtime methods call `ensureReady()` first. Each one is explained in [Sessions from code](/docs/#sessions-from-code).

| Area | Methods |
| --- | --- |
| Lifecycle | `get()`, `update(input)`, `delete()`, `restore()`, `start()`, `startOrThrow()`, `restart()`, `stop()` |
| Readiness | `ensureReady({ readyTimeoutMs?, session? })`, `health()` (never throws on a non-2xx status) |
| Prompts | `send(text, { model?, agent? })`, `abort()`, `prompts.create` · `.list` · `.remove` · `.retry` · `.edit` · `.interrupt` · `.hold` |
| Live events | `stream({ onEvent, onGapRehydrate?, signal? })` returns a handle with `close()` |
| Conversation | `messages({ conversationId?, limit?, before? })`, `pending()`, `answerPermission(requestId, 'once' \| 'always' \| 'reject', message?)`, `answerQuestion(requestId, answers \| null)`, `compact(model?)`, `rewind(messageId)`, `restoreRewind()` |
| Server-side reads | `turn()`, `log(query?)` (one page of the stored session log, `null` when the session has none; answers while the sandbox is stopped), `transcript(options?)` and `transcriptSync(options?)` (deprecated: the v1 transcript; read `log`), `messageAuthors()`, `modelUsage()`, `cost()`, `audit(limit?)` |
| Files and previews | `files.list` · `.read` · `.readBlob` · `.status` · `.findFiles` · `.findText` · `.upload` · `.write` · `.create` · `.copy` · `.remove` · `.mkdir` · `.rename`, `previews()`, `previewUrl(port, path?)`, `proxyUrl(url)`, `sandboxPortUrl(port)`, `attachments.upload` · `.read` |
| Model, agent and scope | `setModel(model)`, `setAgent(agent)` (every later `send` on this handle uses it, until you change it; `send(text, { model, agent })` overrides it for one prompt), `changeModel('provider/model')` (persisted, restarts the runtime), `scope()`, `rescope(scope)`, `providerSecretPool.list` · `.get` · `.set` |
| Config | `configState()`, `reloadConfig(input?)`, `reloadConfigStream(…)` |
| Sharing and people | `setSharing(intent)`, `publicShares.list` · `.create` · `.revoke`, `participants()`, `watch()`, `setWatch(watching)`, `presence({ tab_id, active, alerts? }, { keepalive? })` (answers `awake_until` and `awake_cap_at`) |
| Git and reminders | `commit(input?)`, `reminders.list` · `.create` · `.update` · `.remove` |

### Framework-free modules

The root `@kortix/sdk` exports these modules next to the client. Use them for one operation without the facade, or for pure helpers.

| Module | Names | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Files | `files`, `FileExistsError`, `uploadNativeFile` | File operations on the process-wide active sandbox, with the same methods as `s.files`. A host with several sessions uses `s.files`. |
| Session health | `getSessionHealth(runtimeUrl?, init?)`, `isRuntimeReady(health)`, `detectLocalhostUrls`, `rewriteLocalhostUrl`, `proxyLocalhostUrl`, `parseLocalhostUrl`, `buildWebProxyUrl` | Probe a runtime and rewrite an agent's `localhost` URLs into reachable proxy URLs. |
| Event stream | `openEventStream`, `narrowChatEvent` | The live stream behind `s.stream`, and a narrower for chat events. |
| Auth | `authenticatedFetch`, `getAuthToken` | A `fetch` that adds the token from your `getToken`. |
| API client | `backendApi` | The typed HTTP client under every method. Use it only for an endpoint with no method. |
| Errors | `ApiError`, `isFeatureDisabledError`, `featureDisabledKey` | Error types and checks. |
| Composer lists | `projectConfigAgentsToRuntimeAgents`, `composerSelectableAgents`, `resolveComposerAgent`, `pickerProviderList`, `flattenModels`, `createModelVisibility`, `modelInDefaultView`, `resolveComposerModel`, `resolveModelDefault`, `sessionModelCandidates`, `latestPromptModel`, `modelRefToKey` | Build an agent picker and a model picker with the rules of the Kortix web app. Every function is pure. |
| Transcript export | `formatTranscript(session, messages, options?)` | One session as a Markdown string. |
| Sandboxes by id | `restartSandbox(id)`, `stopSandbox(id)`, `getSandboxById(id)` | Act on a session's sandbox with only its `session_id`. One `GET /v1/sessions/:id` finds the project, however old the session is. A provider external id still works, but only for a session on its project's first sessions page. With the project id, use `s.restart()`, `s.stop()` and `s.get()`. |
| Sandboxes, every project | `getSandbox()`, `ensureSandbox()`, `listSandboxes(id?)` | Read each project's first sessions page only (default 50 rows). They need a project to find older sessions: use `p.sessions.listPage()`. |

#### Files

```ts
import { FileExistsError, files } from '@kortix/sdk';

const { content } = await files.read('/workspace/README.md');
await files.upload(file, '/workspace/uploads', file.name, {
  onProgress: ({ loadedBytes, totalBytes }) => console.log(loadedBytes, '/', totalBytes),
});

try {
  await files.create('/workspace/notes.md');
} catch (error) {
  if (error instanceof FileExistsError) console.log('exists:', error.path);
  else throw error;
}
```

- `upload` sends a file of 8 MiB or less in one request. A larger file goes in 8 MiB chunks, and each chunk is checked on arrival. `onProgress` runs after each chunk.
- `write(path, blob)` replaces the file at `path`. It fails when `path` is a folder.
- `create(path)` makes an empty file and never overwrites. When anything exists at `path` it throws `FileExistsError` (`status` `409`, `code` `FILE_EXISTS`).
- `uploadNativeFile({ uri, name, type? }, targetPath?, baseUrl?)` uploads a React Native device file in one request. The bytes stream from `uri` and never enter the JS heap.

#### Session health

`getSessionHealth()` never throws on a non-2xx status. It returns `{ status, ok, health, body, hop, upstreamStatus }`. `isRuntimeReady(health)` is `true` when the runtime accepts work. `s.health()` is the same probe for one session.

### Custom transcript rendering

Pure functions that turn session messages into renderable parts. Use them to build your own chat view. They have no React and no DOM.

#### Classify parts and turns

`classifyPart(part)` turns one part of the Kortix transcript format (`kortix.transcript.v1`, `KORTIX_TRANSCRIPT_SCHEMA`) into a `ClassifiedPart`, keyed by `kind`:

| `kind` | Fields |
| --- | --- |
| `text` | `id`, `text`, `synthetic`. Do not render a `synthetic` part as a chat bubble. |
| `reasoning` | `id`, `text` |
| `tool` | `id`, `tool: ToolView` |
| `file` | `id`, `filename?`, `mime`, `url`, `isImage`, `isPdf` |
| `subtask` | `id`, `description`, `agent`, `prompt`, `model?` |
| `patch` | `id`, `hash`, `files`, `fileCount` |
| `snapshot` | `id`, `snapshot` |
| `agent` | `id`, `name` |
| `retry` | `id`, `attempt`, `message`, `createdAt` |
| `compaction` | `id`, `auto`, `overflow`, `tailStartId?` |
| `step` | `id`, `phase: 'start' \| 'finish'`, `snapshot?`, `reason?`, `cost?`, `tokens?` |
| `unknown` | `raw`: a part type this SDK version does not know. It never throws. |

`ToolView` fields: `name`, `kind` (from `toolKind`), `title`, `status` (`pending`, `running`, `done`, `error`), `input`, `output`, `error`, `outputParsed` (the parsed JSON output, for output up to 256 KiB), `outputText`, `files`, `diff`, `diagnostics`, `answers`, `childSessionId`. A tool that reports `success: false` inside a completed state has `status: 'error'`. `toToolView(part)` builds a `ToolView` from one tool part or one session-log `ToolCallBlock`. Read these fields, not a harness's own metadata: the same card then renders every harness.

`classifyTurn(message)` returns `{ parts, error?, isEmpty }` for one assistant message. `isEmpty` is `true` when the turn has no error and no visible part.

```ts
import { classifyTurn, toolViewModel } from '@kortix/sdk';

for (const part of classifyTurn(message).parts) {
  if (part.kind === 'text' && !part.synthetic) render(part.text);
  if (part.kind === 'tool') {
    const view = toolViewModel(part.tool);
    if (view.kind === 'shell') render(view.command, view.stdout, view.exitCode);
  }
}
```

#### Tools

- `toolKind(name)` maps any harness's tool name to one `ToolKind`: `read`, `list`, `glob`, `grep`, `write`, `edit`, `apply_patch`, `bash`, `pty`, `web_search`, `webfetch`, `media`, `show`, `task`, `delegate`, `sessions`, `todowrite`, `question`, `memory`, `connectors`, `automations`, `projects`, `skill`, `context`, `retired` or `other`.
- `toolInfo(name)` returns `{ label, category }`. `getToolInfo(name, input)` returns `{ icon, title, subtitle }` for a tool card. An unknown name falls back to `humanizeToolName(name)`.
- `inputPath(input)` reads a file tool's path from `filePath`, `file_path` or `path`.
- `toolViewModel(tool)` returns one shape per tool family:

| `kind` | Fields |
| --- | --- |
| `web-search` | `query`, `results?`, `answer?`, `error?` |
| `shell` | `command`, `stdout?`, `exitCode?` |
| `file-read` · `file-write` | `path`, `preview?` |
| `file-edit` | `path`, `diff?` (lines of `{ type: 'added' \| 'removed' \| 'unchanged', text }`) |
| `search` | `pattern`, `matches?` |
| `task` | `description`, `agent?` |
| `todo` | `items` |
| `question` | `questions`, `answers?` |
| `generic` | `label`, `inputPretty?`, `outputPretty?`. Every other tool. Always safe to render. |

#### Turns, status and helpers

| Names | What they do |
| --- | --- |
| `groupMessagesIntoTurns(messages)` · `collectTurnParts(turn)` | Pair each user message with the assistant messages that answered it · all parts of one turn. |
| `findLastTextPart(parts)` · `turnHasSteps(parts)` | The final answer text · whether the turn has tool, compaction, snapshot or patch parts. |
| `getTurnStatus(parts, childMessages?)` · `getWorkingState(…)` · `formatDuration(ms)` | A status line such as `Reading files...`. Pass `childMessages` to show a sub-agent's activity. |
| `getTurnError(turn)` · `getChildSessionError(messages)` · `unwrapError(error)` | Turn and sub-agent errors, normalized. |
| `getRetryInfo(status)` · `getRetryMessage(status)` | Retry state `{ attempt, message, next, details? }` · the full retry message. |
| `getChildSessionId(part)` · `getChildSessionToolParts(messages)` · `rootRuntimeSession` · `directSubsessions` · `runtimeSessionsOf` · `projectSessionForRuntimeId` | Connect a delegating tool call to its child conversation. |
| `getPermissionForTool(permissions, callID)` · `getHiddenToolParts(…)` · `isToolPartHidden(…)` | Match a pending permission or question to its tool call. |
| `isTextPart`, `isToolPart`, `isReasoningPart`, `isFilePart`, `isAgentPart`, `isCompactionPart`, `isSnapshotPart`, `isPatchPart`, `isStepPart`, `getPartText` | Type guards. |
| `sortSessions(now)` · `childMapByParent(sessions)` · `allDescendantIds(map, id)` | Session list order and hierarchy. |
| `getFilename`, `getDirectory`, `relativizePath`, `stripAnsi`, `formatTokens` | Formatting. |

The grouping and status functions accept the structural types `PartLike`, `MessageInfoLike`, `TurnLike`, `ToolStateLike` and `SessionStatusLike`, so your own message shapes work. Never mint a message id by hand: use `mintWireMessageId()`.

### Session log types

The root exports the types of the Kortix session log, the harness-neutral record of a session, schema `kortix.session/2`. Kortix stores every session as a session log. The SDK exports the types and three constants, with no validators. In React, `useSessionLog` and `useSessionLogThread` return a session's log once the session reads it: see [Read the session log](/docs/#read-the-session-log).

| Name | What it is |
| --- | --- |
| `SESSION_LOG_SCHEMA` · `SESSION_LOG_MINOR` | `'kortix.session/2'` · `1` |
| `SessionLog` | The record: threads, todos, pending questions and permissions, harness history, `restore_grade`. |
| `SessionLogThread` · `SessionLogMessage` | One thread (the main thread or a sub-agent) · one message with `seq`, `role`, `kind`, `status`, `model`, `usage`, `blocks`. |
| `SessionLogBlock` | The union of `TextBlock`, `ReasoningBlock`, `AttachmentBlock`, `ToolCallBlock`, `CompactionBlock`, `SubtaskBlock`, `StepBlock`, `HarnessBlock`, keyed by `type`. |
| `ToolResultContent`, `CompactionLayoutEntry`, `ThreadInteraction`, `ModelRef` | Parts of the records above. |
| `KNOWN_TOOL_KINDS` · `KnownToolKind` · `SessionLogToolKind` | The tool kinds every reader renders: `shell`, `read`, `write`, `edit`, `patch`, `list`, `glob`, `grep`, `web_fetch`, `web_search`, `todo`, `task`, `question`, `plan`, `mcp`, `other` · the tool kind of a block, any string. Render an unknown kind as `other`. |
| `SessionLogTodo` | One todo item. |
| `RestoreGrade` · `GradeCounts` | How faithfully the record restores (`native`, `converted` or `partial`) · what is missing. |

A text over 256 kB is stored by reference. This applies to a `TextBlock` and to a `text` item of a tool result (`ToolResultContent`). Its `text` holds the first 16 kB, `bytes` holds the UTF-8 length of the full text, and `ref` is `kortix-attachment://<project_id>/<session_id>/<attachment_id>`. `kortix.session(projectId, sessionId).attachments.read(attachmentId)` returns the full text as a `Blob` (`text/plain; charset=utf-8`), also while the sandbox is stopped. The v1 transcript reads return the full text.

```ts
import type { SessionLog, SessionLogBlock } from '@kortix/sdk';

function toolCalls(log: SessionLog): SessionLogBlock[] {
  return log.threads.flatMap((t) => t.messages.flatMap((m) => m.blocks)).filter((b) => b.type === 'tool_call');
}
```

#### Tool calls from v1 parts (transitional)

Three functions bridge a host that renders blocks while its rows are still v1 (`useSessionMessages`). All three are transitional.

| Name | What it does | Removed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `toolCallBlock(part)` | The `ToolCallBlock` of a v1 tool part: the block `fromV1` builds for it. An open call stays open (`pending` or `running`, no `result`). A call that streams its input has the text so far as its `input`. The same part object returns the same block. | In the SDK major that removes the v1 rows. |
| `toolCallMetadata(call)` | The harness's display metadata of a tool call: the whole `state.metadata` of the v1 part it came from, such as bash `command`, read `loaded`, task `sessionId` or edit `filediff`. A record a harness wrote natively carries its `details` (`result.details`, else the adapter's own ext: pi keeps them in `ext.pi.data.details`) and `exit` (from `result.exit_code`). One shared empty object when there is none. | When the session-log schema carries display metadata. |
| `sessionLogOfV1Rows(sessionId, runtimeSessionId, rows)` | The `fromV1` live view of one runtime session's v1 rows: the `SessionLog` `useSessionLogThread` returns on v1 (one root thread, the runtime session id as its id, the open turn kept open). For rows a host reads without a `useSession`, such as a sub-agent's chat. A message keeps its identity while its row object is unchanged. | In the SDK major that removes the v1 rows. |

`toToolView`, `getChildSessionId`, `partOutcome`, `contextToolTrigger` and `shouldShowToolPart` accept a `ToolCallBlock` as well as a v1 tool part, and return the same answer for both.

```ts
import { getChildSessionId, toolCallBlock, toolCallMetadata } from '@kortix/sdk';

const call = toolCallBlock(part); // part: a v1 tool part from useSessionMessages
const child = getChildSessionId(call); // a running task's child session
const command = toolCallMetadata(call).command; // bash
```

#### Reading a tool call

What a host draws a `ToolCallBlock` with. Block-native: they stay.

| Name | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `toolCallStatus(call)` | The status in the words a renderer compares: `pending`, `running`, `completed` or `error`. |
| `toolCallRawOutput(call)` | A completed call's output text as the tool returned it (its result's text items, joined); `''` for any other call. One string per call object. |
| `toolCallError(call)` | A failed call's error text: its semantic error (`result.error.message`), else its result text, as `toV1` gives a v1 part's `state.error`. A harness that closes a failed call with `is_error` and the tool's text only still shows that text. `undefined` for a call that did not fail. |
| `subAgentCalls(thread)` | The tool calls a sub-agent row lists: every shown call (`shouldShowToolPart`) of the thread's replies, in order. One array per thread object; the same empty array for `null`. |

#### Block turns

The turn model over session-log blocks: what a transcript renders a thread from. Each name replaces a v1 helper over `MessageWithParts` (see [Turns, status and helpers](#turns-status-and-helpers)); the v1 helpers stay until the next major.

| Name | What it does | Replaces |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `sessionLogTurns(thread, options?)` | One `SessionLogTurn` per user message: `id`, `user`, `assistant` (the replies, by `reply_to` then order), `partial` (the prompt is not loaded), `blocks` (every reply block), and the derived fields `status` (`pending`, else the last reply's status), `error` (`code`, `message`, `details`, `raw`), `cost` (the replies' usage priced with the platform markup; a compaction's summary usage is a reply's; a message converted from v1 is priced over its v1 parts, exactly as `getTurnCost` prices them), `compaction` (`block`, `hasContent`, `inFlight`), `threads` (the sub-threads it spawned), `plan` (owns the plan card), `shell` (shell mode's bash call), `response` (the last text block), `attachments`, `live`, `queued`, `retrying`. Options: `busy`, `liveMessageId`, `held` (the prompts the inbox holds, in queue order), `threads`, `pricing`. A turn keeps its identity while its messages and derived fields do. A message out of context keeps its turn; `hideOutOfContext: true` leaves out every message with `in_context: false` that is not streaming (a failed attempt, an aborted empty reply, an old root's history). | `groupMessagesIntoTurns`, `collectTurnParts`, `getTurnError`, `getTurnErrorDetails`, `getTurnErrorRawText`, `getTurnCost`, `compactionTurnInfo`, `getShellModePart`, `findLastTextPart`, `isAttachment`, `getWorkingState`, `resolveWorkingTurn`, `hasRetryingAssistantTurn`, `getChildSessionId` |
| `sessionLogTurnSegments(blocks, options?)` | The segments of a turn: `burst` (a run of non-text blocks), `text`, `standalone` (a `show` or sub-agent call, or a call named in `standaloneCallIds`), `show-group` (adjacent `show` calls). | `segmentTurn`, `groupShowSegments` |
| `splitSessionLogTurnWork(segments, { working })` | The settled-turn fold: `work`, `hoisted` (the `show` deliverables), `final` (the last text). `null` while the turn works or has no work. | `splitTurnWork` |
| `sessionLogBurstSteps(blocks)` | The rows of a burst: `thought` (a run of reasoning; `running` only while its message streams), `block`, `group` (same-family calls, with `family`, `label`, `status`). An answered question is its own row. | `mergeBurstSteps`, `stepLabel` |
| `sessionLogCallStatus(call, live)` | The status a call renders with: `pending`, `running`, `complete`, `error`, or `interrupted`. An open call, or one the closure rule closed (`result.synthetic`, or `result.error.code` `'interrupted'`), is `running` (started) or `pending` on a live turn and `interrupted` on any other. | new |
| `sessionLogOpenCall(call)` | The call in the open shape `sessionLogCallStatus(call, true)` names: a call the closure rule closed reads `running` (started) or `pending`, without its `result`; every other call is itself. One object per call. Render a thread's calls through it so the v1 live view and the v2 page draw one call the same; `sessionLogCallStatus(call, turn.live)` says whether it is interrupted. | the host's open-call shim |
| `sessionLogBurstSummary(blocks)` | A burst's step counts: `total` (a run of reasoning is one), `failed`, `completed`, `hasPlumbing`. | `burstSummary` |
| `sessionLogBurstRunning(blocks, { working, trailing? })` | The burst is in progress: never once the turn stops working; the trailing burst for the whole working turn; another one while a call is open or a thought is still written. | the host's `burstIsRunning` |
| `sessionLogShowGroupCall(calls)` | A `show-group` segment as one `show` call: the first call's ids, `input.items` the carousel (a failed call keeps its slot as an `error` item), `running` while a call is open, else `complete`. | the host's `mergeShowParts` |
| `sessionLogAnsweredQuestionCall(call, answers, input?)` | A `question` call as answered with `answers` the harness has not confirmed yet: `complete`, the answers in its metadata (`toToolView(call).answers`), `input` replaced when given. | the host's synthetic answered part |
| `sessionLogRetrying(thread)` | The thread's last reply is open with a retryable provider error: its turn's `retrying`, read without grouping turns, so the busy state a host passes as `busy` can include it. False on a native record (no retry flag). | `hasRetryingAssistantTurn` |
| `sessionLogActivityText(turn, childMessages?)` | The activity line of a live turn ("Reading files..."); a running task reads its sub-thread's messages. | `getTurnStatus` |
| `sessionLogThreadMetrics(thread, options?)` | One thread's `cost` (every reply's usage priced: its reported cost, else the lookup, with the platform markup), `messages` (`all`, `user`, `assistant`; a compaction counts as its user marker and, once it is no longer streaming, its summary reply) `tokens` (`input`, `output`, `reasoning`, `cacheRead`, `cacheWrite`: every priced reply's usage summed) and `context` (the last reply, an assistant message or a compaction, with tokens: its `usage` is the context size). Options: `pricing`. What a context modal's overview and its sub-agent rows show. | `getSessionCost` over v1 rows, a host's own token and message counts and last-reply lookup |
| `classifySessionLogMessage(message)` | `classifyTurn` of one session-log message: its blocks as `ClassifiedPart`s and its error as `TurnError` (the gateway's own sentence, which `message.error.message` does not carry for a converted record). A compaction is one message: the marker's parts, then the summary's. For a host whose renderers take `ClassifiedPart`s (the TUI, the white-label demo). | `classifyTurn` over v1 rows |
| `sessionLogPromptMessage(prompt, threadId)` | The user message of a queued inbox prompt (`SessionPrompt`) the thread has not placed: id `message_id`, else `queued-<prompt_id>`. Append it after the thread's messages and name it in `held`. | the host's synthetic row |
| `isSessionLogRestoreNote(message)` | The message is the note a partial restore leaves for the agent: a user message whose text block id starts with `prt_kortix_restore_g` (on v2 it also carries `origin: 'notification'`; the v1 transcript has no origin). Render it as a notice, not as a person's prompt. | new |
| `sessionLogRestoreNotice(session)` | What a partial restore could not bring back, for the person: `{ generation, missing }`, `missing` a list of `{ kind, count }` (`attachment`, `text`, `tool_input`, `tool_result`, `cut_point`, `other`) with counts above zero. Reads `restore_grade`, `grade_counts` and `ext.kortix.data.restore` of the session record (`useSessionLog`). Null unless the grade is `partial` and a restore wrote the record. | new |
| `sessionLogHarnessSwitches(history, turns)` | The switches `harness.history` records, by the id of the turn each lands on (the first turn whose prompt `seq` is at or above `from_seq`): `{ harness, previous, version, at }`. A new version of the same harness is no switch. A switch inside a page not loaded yet is left out. | new |
| `sessionLogReferencedTexts(messages)` | The texts the API stored by reference (a text over 256 kB keeps a 16 kB preview, plus `ref` and `bytes`) that a transcript renders, text blocks and tool results' `content` items (not `model_content`), once per `ref`: `{ ref, bytes }`. Read one with `fetchSessionAttachment(ref)`. | new |
| `sessionLogWithFullTexts(thread, texts)` | `thread` with each text whose `ref` is a key of `texts` replaced by the whole text, without `ref` and `bytes`. Untouched messages, and the thread when nothing changes, keep their identity. | new |
| `sessionLogFullTextAction(refs)` | How a host offers the whole of `refs`: `'inline'` up to `SESSION_LOG_FULL_TEXT_INLINE_MAX_BYTES` (1 MiB) in all, else `'download'` (a multi-MB text rendered as markdown stalls the page). | new |
| `sessionLogFullTextPlan(refs)` | Which of `refs` show inline and which download: the smallest texts fill the 1 MiB inline budget first, only the rest download (`{ inline, download }`, both in record order). Nothing downloads exactly when `sessionLogFullTextAction` says `'inline'`. | new |

A pending permission or question matches a call by `call.call_id`: `getPermissionForTool(permissions, call.call_id)`, `getQuestionForTool(questions, call.call_id)`. `freshSendHint` and `fallbackBusyRowAfterTurnId` accept a `SessionLogTurn[]` as well as v1 turns, and `stepLabel` any session-log block (a non-call block reads by its `type`).

```ts
import { sessionLogTurns, sessionLogTurnSegments, sessionLogCallStatus } from '@kortix/sdk';

const turns = sessionLogTurns(thread, { busy, liveMessageId: working.turnId, held: heldIds, threads: log.threads });
for (const turn of turns) {
  for (const segment of sessionLogTurnSegments(turn.blocks)) {
    if (segment.kind === 'standalone') render(segment.block, sessionLogCallStatus(segment.block, turn.live));
  }
}
```

### Deprecated names

Each name below is marked `@deprecated` and stays until the next major version. Your editor flags each call.

| Deprecated | Use instead |
| --- | --- |
| `p.updateExperimentalFeature(key, enabled)` · `updateExperimentalFeature` · `ExperimentalFeatureKey` · `ExperimentalFeatureView` | `p.updateFeatureFlag` · `updateFeatureFlag` · `FeatureFlagKey` · `FeatureFlagView` |
| `s.runtime` | The session methods: `messages`, `pending`, `answerPermission`, `answerQuestion`, `compact`, `send`, `abort`, `rewind` |
| `OpencodeClient` · `OpencodeClientConfig` · `createOpencodeClient` · `OpenCodeEvent` · `OpencodeAgentConfig` · `ProjectOpenCodeSession` · `OpenCodeProjectInfo` | `RuntimeClient` · `RuntimeClientConfig` · `createRuntimeClient` · `RuntimeEvent` · `RuntimeAgentConfig` · `ProjectRuntimeSession` · `RuntimeProjectInfo` |
| `projectConfigAgentsToOpenCodeAgents` · `parseOpenCodeErrorPayload` · `formatOpenCodeRuntimeError` | `projectConfigAgentsToRuntimeAgents` · `parseRuntimeErrorPayload` · `formatRuntimeError` |
| `OpenCodeConfigIssue` · `OpenCodeConfigInvalidError` · `getOpenCodeConfigInvalidError` · `isOpenCodeConfigInvalidError` | `RuntimeConfigIssue` · `RuntimeConfigInvalidError` · `getRuntimeConfigInvalidError` · `isRuntimeConfigInvalidError` |
| `p.sessions.claimWarm(input)` · `claimWarmProjectSession` | Open the session from `p.sessions.ensureWarm()` and prompt it |
| `p.connectors.setAuthorizationStrategy` · `setConnectorAuthorizationStrategy` | The call changes nothing. To create an account under one owner, pass `owner` to `connectorConnect`. |
| `getConnectionPolicies` · `setConnectionPolicies` | `p.connectors.policies.get` · `p.connectors.policies.set`. For other rules per agent, a [policy set](/docs/#connectors). |
| `pipedreamConnect` · `pipedreamFinalize` | `connectorConnect` · `connectorFinalize` |
| `listDiscoverIntegrations` · `getDiscoverIntegration` · `DiscoverIntegration*` types | `listDiscoverConnectors` · `getDiscoverConnector` · `DiscoverConnector*` types |
| `SessionScopeResult` · `toWorkspaceRelative` | `SessionScope` · `toDaemonPath` |
| `setCachedAuthToken` · `setBootstrapAuthToken` | Return the token from your own `getToken` |
| `claimNext`, `enqueue`, `createSessionQueue` and the other `@kortix/sdk/message-queue` names | `s.send`, or `useSession().send` in React |
| `wireIdClock`, `wireIdClockDelta`, `maxWireIdClock`, `newestWireIdClock`, `mintWireMessageIdAbove` and the `WIRE_ID_*` constants | `mintWireMessageId`. Message ids are opaque. |
| `billing.checkout.createSession` · `billing.checkout.confirmSession` · `createPerSeatCheckout` · `claimPerSeatBilling` | `billing.setPlan(input)`. All reject with `ENDPOINT_RETIRED`. |
| `billing.subscription.scheduleDowngrade` · `billing.subscription.cancelScheduledChange` · `billing.subscription.prorationPreview` | `billing.setPlan(input)`, or `billing.subscription.createPortalSession`. They reject with `ENDPOINT_RETIRED`. |

Other deprecated root names wrap routes that the API does not serve. They reject with `ENDPOINT_RETIRED`, resolve `null`, or return `404`, and they have no replacement.
